**What it proves.** It writes the e2e halves of A12 to A14 and A18's mirrored scene; they are proved when the architect's runs are green. By its own run: typecheck, lint and the whole unit suite.

### M4 — Who draws: the declaration, the reservation, `drawnBy`, the wire minor

**Starts when** its base holds the first pull request. The branch `ux-wave/U2b-2` at `1ee6dca93` does: it holds the chain through U3b's merge of U1c, and with it M1 to M3 and the view switch's fix `11e183013`. It does not wait for check 1: nothing here reads a mod's answer.

**At its end.** The owner channel publishes `drawnBy` with a session's lane, decided by §A; a launch's advertisement is reserved in the index before the launch is sent and bound to its id after; an installation's declaration is read at the launch and at the loaded plugin's path after a reload; the wire has one more minor, carrying the optional key and the command `session.ui.renderSite`. `TO_HUMAN_CONTRACT_VERSION` is 9 and the mods list reads "Version 9". Every launch still advertises 8 or nothing: the one switch of `contract.ts` is off, and no released installation carries the declaration. For the summary (§M): the owner service answers `ownerChannel.folds` with the runs of the mod's file for a session of this machine; the mods list's to-human row carries `summaries`, and `mods.setFoldSummaries` writes it to the index's own facts; every launch decided while it is off carries `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off` in its settings env.

**What it touches.** `src/shared/owner/contract.ts`, `state.ts`, new `src/shared/owner/draws.ts`; new `src/services/owner/declaration.ts` (the one reader of the file); `src/services/owner/resolve-style.ts` (the applicable installations' paths, and whether hooks load), `launch.ts` (the advertisement, the reservation before the send, the bind), `store.ts` (the column, `reserveLaunch`), `service.ts` and `register.ts` (the status's subscription, the published field, the start's reconciliation); `src/services/sessions/launch-mapping.ts` (what is written into the settings env); `src/services/index/migrations.ts` (`ADDED_COLUMNS`); the command: `src/shared/ipc/schema/session.ts`, `names/session.ts`, `grades/session.ts`, `remote-unavailable.ts`, the engine host's handler beside `session.ui.render`'s (`src/engine-host/ipc.ts` and what it calls), `src/shared/machine/address-paths.json`, `src/shared/ipc/routes.json` (the snapshot of every command's route class, which each new command changes); `src/shared/ipc/wire.ts` and the two generated files; their tests, and the tests that pin 8 by its digit (`src/shared/owner/contract.test.ts`, `src/services/owner/launch.test.ts`, `src/services/sessions/service.test.ts`, `src/services/sessions/launch-mapping.test.ts`), which pin `TO_HUMAN_MIRROR_VERSION` by name afterwards. For the summary: new `src/shared/owner/folds.ts` (the file's shape and the reader's limits, pure) and `src/services/owner/folds.ts` (the one place that opens the mod's file); `src/services/owner/store.ts` (`foldSummaries`, `setFoldSummaries`), `mods.ts`, `launch.ts` and `register.ts`; `src/shared/mods/registry.ts` (`summaries`); `src/shared/ipc/schema/mods.ts` and `schema/owner-channel.ts` with their rows in `names/`, `grades/` and `remote-unavailable.ts`; `src/services/sessions/launch-mapping.ts` (the second value of the settings env); `src/shared/machine/address-paths.json`; their tests.

**Interfaces.** Exposes §A and the command of §E to M5. Consumes the engine's status (`src/shared/engine/status.ts`).

**Decisions.** 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 22. Beyond them:
- *Where the reservation is written.* `ownerChannelLaunch` runs before `launchOnHost` in every launch path (`setup.ts`, `resume.ts`); it writes the reservation as its last act and, when the write of a 9 fails, returns the spec with 8 and a decision that records 8. `ownerChannelLaunched` binds it. A resume's second attempt (the engine wrote nothing) reuses the reservation and binds again.
- *Where the service learns the status.* The owner service subscribes to the engine host's `session.status` beside its `session.eventBatch` subscription (`register.ts`), and at its start reads each live session's status once and reconciles: a reservation not yet bound that §A reads as the running launch's is bound then.
- *The declaration's read is asynchronous.* It is read when a launch is decided (each applicable installation) and when a status first shows plugins observed at a new time (the loaded plugin's `path`); a read in flight when a later observation arrives is dropped, and until a read answers the earlier fact stands.
- *Sequences* (each a test that holds the order open, not only the end state): the reservation is written, the host accepts, the daemon stops before the bind, a new service starts: `drawnBy` is `mod` (R2's proof; `src/services/owner/restart.test.ts`); the reservation of a 9 cannot be written: the launch's settings env carries 8; a reserved launch is refused by the host and the session's earlier launch still runs: the reservation is not read as that launch's; the next launch overwrites it; a status of an older launch arrives after a newer launch was bound (ignored: its `launchId` differs); the process exits while a declaration's read is in flight (the exit wins: `maws`); the row cannot be read while a launch is live and the mod is loaded: `mod`, and `maws` once the plugins are reported without it; an earlier build's launch follows a reservation this build left unbound: its `launch_id` is the running launch's, so the advertisement is read from that row's `desired`.
- *Authorization.* `drawnBy` is read-only state published to whoever may already read the session's owner channel. `session.ui.renderSite` is graded and offered exactly as `session.ui.render` is, and refused for a session on another machine with the same sentence; the host checks the component against its own list where the ask runs, not only where the request enters.
- *Tests the ruling names.* An installation with a higher version and no declaration advertises 8; two applicable installations of which one lacks it advertise 8.
- *The reader of the mod's file* (§M) takes a session and never a path. It builds the path from the engine session id of its own record of that session and from the configuration directory the owner service already resolves (`register.ts`), refuses an id that is no plain file name, and reads a bounded file. Whatever it cannot read is "no summaries", never an error a window shows.
- *Sequences for the summary* (each a test that holds the order open): the file is replaced, by the contract's rename, between the reader's open and its read (the answer is the old file whole or the new one whole, never a mixture); a text that does not parse, which the contract never writes, is found (`runs: null`, and the next read answers the runs); the session has no engine session id yet (an empty list); the switch is written while a launch is being decided (the launch read it once, in `ownerChannelLaunch`; the write is the next launch's); two writes of the switch arrive together (the later value stands, and `mods.list` answers it).
- *Authorization for the summary.* `ownerChannel.folds` is graded and offered exactly as `ownerChannel.state` is and refused for a session on another machine with the same sentence; the check that the caller may read the session is made on the session the path is then built from. `mods.setFoldSummaries` is graded and refused for another machine as `mods.setSessionOverride` is.
- *Two writers of one session's file.* M4 builds nothing for it: MAWS starts no process on a session a live process runs (decision 33), and what two terminals do to one file is the mod's, covered by the contract's merge (§I, item 12).

**What it does not touch.** The renderer. The switch of `contract.ts`.

**What it proves.** §A's tables case by case (the switch injected); the stored value's round trip through an earlier build's write; the wire's manifest test green with one added optional key and one added command and nothing removed or narrowed; the tolerant reader dropping `lane.drawnBy` for a schema that does not know it; an older host answering `unknown_command` to `session.ui.renderSite`. For the summary: the reader's table (no file; shape 2; shape 1 and any other shape, which read as none; a text that does not parse; a file past the limit; an entry that is not three strings; a summary of several lines, or past 120 characters, or empty; more runs than the limit); no stored id, however written, taking the path outside the folds directory; the switch's round trip through the index and through an earlier build's launch; the settings env with the switch on and off beside each advertisement; the tolerant reader dropping `summaries`; an older daemon answering `unknown_command` to both commands.

### M5 — Where the mod draws: an ask for every row, full sites, the reveal's end; the cutover

**Starts when** check 1 has answered and P's branch exists: both hold since 2026-11-16 (UTC).

**At its end.** In a session MAWS runs where the mod draws (at 9 with the declaration, or with the lane off and the plugin loaded), the messages, the record lines and the switch are the mod's; MAWS draws no record line, no switch and no message of its own, applies no projection, and folds no turn once the session holds a message. Every row the fold can hide asks a site of its own while it is mounted, so the mod's report hides it and the mod's full transcript shows it, whichever other rows are mounted. The run's row is a full site. A jump's target is revealed, and the reveal ends when its row has entered the mounted range and left it. A launch advertises 9 when §A says so.

**What it touches.** `transcript/surface/Rows.tsx` (`SurfaceGroupRow`; surface rows for `to_human`, `thinking`, `agent_list`, `browser_action` and the turn's line; the separator's probe), `adapters.ts` (their inputs; the arrival rows' asks widened), `transcript/view/rows/Row.tsx`, `Divider.tsx`, `transcript/view/Transcript.tsx` (the mod route's composition; the reveal's end), `TranscriptSlot.tsx` (`modDraws`), `stores/transcript.ts` (`modDraws` in the flag, `clearRevealed`), `engine-surface/site.ts` and `registry.ts` (the second command for `TurnDuration`; the re-ask after a reload, §E), `src/shared/engine/surface.ts` and `surface-rows.ts` (`TurnDuration`'s props; `isSummary`), `owner/view/Switch.tsx` (absent where the mod draws; the action's notice), `src/shared/owner/contract.ts` (the switch). Tests: `transcript/surface/rows.test.tsx`, `adapters.test.ts`, `arrivals.test.tsx`, `revealed.test.tsx`, `engine-surface/SurfaceRow.test.tsx` where it pins the drawing-only group; new `transcript/surface/mod-route.test.tsx` over check 1's fixtures.

**Interfaces.** Consumes §A, §B, §C, §D and check 1's fixtures. Exposes §E.

**Decisions.** 14, 15, 20, 22. Beyond them:
- *The asks do not depend on who draws the view.* Every ask is made whenever a surface is attached; a mod that is not the to-human mod can now replace a run too (the child's departure from Desktop). What depends on `modDraws` is only what MAWS adds (the mirror, the switch) and the flag's first term.
- *The cutover's order inside the milestone:* the renderer's part first, then one commit that turns the switch of `contract.ts` on. Check 1 found that the changed mod hides a run (A2), so the declaration ships with the mod's pull request; were it withdrawn, the cutover commit would still land, since with no declared installation every launch advertises 8.
- *Sequences* (tests that hold them open): the surface attaches after the mirror has drawn (the mirror goes when `modDraws` turns true, in one update with the first asks); the surface goes while a record is open in the mod's view (the mirror draws, every stretch closed); `drawnBy` turns `maws` under a live surface (a reload to an installation without the declaration: the mirror draws while the asks go on and are answered with the engine's drawing, and the reads of §M start for the tab); an ask's answer never comes (the surface row shows its placeholder as it does today; nothing else waits on it); a jump lands while its target's ask is in flight (the target is revealed and draws MAWS's own; the late answer is dropped).
- *The tests R3 names.* Each of a thought, a listing, a notice with no record of its own, a turn's line and a time separator is mounted **with no other row of its stretch mounted**, and is hidden or shown by its own ask: at the first mark's arrival (the invalidation re-asks it), at each of the mode's two switches, and across a plugin reload (the answers are forgotten and asked again).
- *The tests the rulings on points 23 and 24 name.* A turn whose answer the mod folds shows no action row and no focusable control under it; with its record opened, it shows one. A `browser_activity` row under a call is hidden while the mod hides its call's row and shown while it shows it, each with no other row of the stretch mounted, and it sends no ask of its own.
- *The tests R6 names.* A jump to a target that is not yet mounted keeps its reveal until the row has been mounted and then left; a jump to a call inside an expanded run ends when the run's row leaves, and survives the run's id changing.

**What it does not touch.** `engine-surface/SurfaceRow.tsx` and `SurfaceTree.tsx`. The mirror's rule. The mod.

**What it proves.** A15 by unit, over check 1's fixtures. And: each row of §E's table; the reload rows of §G; the action's notice; with `modDraws` true no element of `RecordFold` or `ViewSwitch` in the document; with the lane off and the mod drawing, no turn fold once a message exists. For the summary (§M): where the mod draws, a record line's words are the mod's answer, MAWS writes none of its own beside it and no read of the mod's file is made for the tab; after an invalidation the line's new label is drawn (check 1's fixture for question E2). Nothing else is built for it here: E2 found that the engine pushes one invalidation, naming the instance, for each write a drawn line had read, and §I's item 11 is dropped. `mod-route.test.tsx` also draws the lone call that becomes a run over `u2b-mod-mock-grow-9-after-fix` (one line at every step, on the group once it stands), a thought under its bare uuid over `u2b-mod-rows`, and a reload with no push (the status's `pluginsObservedAt` alone re-asks the mounted rows). And one case for an ask tool's call (reported point 21): where the mod draws, a `question` row of `mcp__sminos__AskUserQuestion` asks the `AskUserQuestion` site under the call's id and sends no `ToolUse` ask, and the row drawn is MAWS's `QuestionRow` with its receipt.

### M7 — The record line's summary where MAWS draws, and the switch

**Starts when** M4 is built (it consumes `ownerChannel.folds` and the mods row's `summaries`) **and check 1's question E4 has answered** with P's checkout: the mod replaces its file in one step and merges before it writes, and P's branch holds the shared table of cases. E4 answered yes on 2026-11-16 (UTC), and the table stands at P's `e511aad9` with seventeen cases under the sha256 `c65988f084c0978b3f79eab220ae02f3ef7175f2717ea225ff421557f9c0fa94`. The reader is not built on a mod that writes in place. It does not wait for P's merge. It is built before or after M5, never beside it (both edit `Transcript.tsx`, `TranscriptSlot.tsx` and `stores/transcript.ts`), and before M6.

**At its end.** Where MAWS draws the view of a session of this machine (a terminal session, a session at rest, a session MAWS runs at 8, a window with no surface attached), a closed record line reads the stored summary of its stretch, a stretch still growing reads its newest call in one line, and any other reads `› working record`. A summary the mod writes while the session is watched appears without a reload, within the reads' schedule. A session on another machine reads its newest call or `working record`; the desk web app reads `working record`. A session whose file a release before the mod's pull request wrote (shape 1) reads `working record`. The mods list's to-human entry has the switch `Summaries on fold lines` with its sentence.

**What it touches.** New: `src/renderer/src/owner/view/label.ts` (the label's rule, pure), `src/renderer/src/stores/folds.ts` (what a window last read, and the reads), `src/renderer/src/owner/view/fold-label-cases.json` (MAWS's copy of the shared table, taken from P's branch and never edited here). Changed: `owner/view/mirror.ts` (the optional labels; the row's `label`), `owner/view/RecordFold.tsx`, `owner/view/copy.ts` and `view.css` (one line, cut by the column; the view switch's hit area), `transcript/model/rows.ts` (`record_fold.label`; `serialize.ts` is not touched: it copies no `record_fold` row), `transcript/view/Transcript.tsx` (the labels handed to `mirrorRows`), `TranscriptSlot.tsx` and `lane-a/view/RemoteTranscriptSlot.tsx` (who supplies them), `lane-a/view/drawn-rows.ts` (it passes none), `widgets/plugins/BuiltInMods.tsx`, `builtin-mods.ts` and `widgets/copy.ts` (the switch, passed by the mods list alone), `transcript/model/cells.ts` (`mergeReads`: the member reads' ids), `transcript/surface/adapters.ts` (the two carrier lookups exported). Not changed: `customize/MineAll.tsx`. Tests: new `owner/view/label.test.ts`, `owner/view/label-cases.test.ts` and `stores/folds.test.ts`; `owner/view/kept-ids.test.ts`; cases in `owner/view/mirror.test.ts`, `lane-a/view-routes.test.ts`, `owner/view/perf.test.ts`, `widgets/plugins/BuiltInMods.test.tsx`, `customize/MineAll.test.tsx` and the cells' own suite.

**Interfaces.** Consumes §M's command and `ModRow.summaries` (M4), §B and §C, and the shared table of §I's item 13. Exposes §M's `recordLabel`, `liveLine`, `runOf`, `labelsOf` and `useStoredFolds`.

**Decisions.** 23, 24, 27 to 32. Beyond them:
- *Every clause of `label.ts` cites the function of the mod it ports* (`labelFor`, `labelOf`, `lineOf`, `lineFor`, `oneLine`, `rowKey`), at the commit of P's branch the shared table was taken from, in a comment, as §B's clauses do.
- *`runOf` is §M's one rule and nothing more.* It has no fallback of MAWS's own. A difference from the mod found while building it is a finding for the architect, not a clause added here.
- *Where the ids and a call's input come from* is §M's ("Where the ids and the inputs come from"): the ids from what each row's ask reads, a merged read's from its additive list, the input from a lookup over the turn's records handed in with the labels. MAWS's own caption of a call (`runLine`) is not used: the words are the mod's.
- *The reads belong to the session's key,* shared by every mount of the key (the pattern of `mountView`), on §M's schedule: a round at each mount, each close of a stretch and each end of a turn, one read at each return of focus; no round is cut short by what a read returned; a round armed by a mount that has since gone ends with the key's last mount.
- *Sequences* (tests that hold them open, the clock in the test's hand):
  - two reads in flight answered out of order (the later read's answer stands);
  - a read answered after the tab changed session (dropped by key);
  - a read that fails, and one that answers `runs: null`, between two that succeed (the window keeps the earlier read whole);
  - a successful read that holds fewer runs than the window had (taken whole: the lines of the runs that went read `working record`);
  - the last mount goes while a read is in flight and while a timer is armed (nothing is written, no timer fires), and the next mount starts a round of its own;
  - a stretch closes and the first read already holds a summary for it: the round still runs to its last read, and a newer summary under the same first row, written after a note or a late result joined the run, replaces the older;
  - a stretch grows by a note alone after its summary, and by a call's result that arrives after its close (each changes the run with no new last call): the round after the next close or end of a turn reads the newer summary;
  - a window is mounted while a summary is outstanding (its mount's round reads it), and remounted during a round (the remount's round runs whole);
  - a summary written after a round's last read (the line reads `working record` until the next mount, focus, close or end of a turn, then the summary);
  - the mod removed the session's file (its 30 days): a mounted window's next successful read answers no runs and its lines read `working record`; a second window and a reopened one read the same;
  - the person opens and folds the stretch while its summary arrives (the open set is untouched, and the closed line reads the summary);
  - `modDraws` turns true while reads are armed (they end; no mirror is drawn).
- *The switch's press* awaits `mods.setFoldSummaries` and then reads the list again, as `setOverride` does. A second press while the first is in flight does nothing; a failure leaves the row as it was, under the widget's own notice.
- *The switch in the row* is as §M gives it: a second line of the row, its words a `label` that holds the control, no pending line. `BuiltInMods.test.tsx` pins that a press on the words toggles it, that its accessible name is its visible words, that the entry's own switch keeps its name and has no visible words, and that no pending line is drawn for it whatever the row's `pending`.
- *The view switch's hit area* (the first pull request's `ViewSwitch`, measured in the drawing at 91 by 19.5 px): its button gets a hit area at least 24 px tall by padding in `owner/view/view.css`, with an equal negative margin so that the line's height, the text's place and the frame are as they were. The stylesheet alone; `Switch.tsx` is M5's.
- *Pitfalls seen.* `rowId.recordFold(stretch, open)` must not take the label: a line whose words change is the same row. The labels are one more input of the callers' memo of `mirrorRows`. The desk web app reaches `mirror.ts` through `Transcript` and `drawnRows`: the added argument is optional and last, and the desk's typecheck, build and test project are in this milestone's proof. A return of focus is the window's (`focus` on the window object), not a row's or a pane's.

**What it does not touch.** What folds and what stays (§B). Any service. The mod, and the shared table's content. Anything under `apps/desk-web/`. `customize/MineAll.tsx`. A cell's own id, and any reader of a cell there is today. The words `working record` and `fold to report`.

**What it proves.** The two behaviours "Acceptance" states for the summary, by unit, and its half of A15's words.
- `label-cases.test.ts`: every case of the shared table gives the label the table names, through `labelsOf` over rows built from the case; the file's digest is the one P's branch pins (a differing copy fails here and in the mod's suite). The table holds at least: a run found by both bounds; a stretch that opens with a thought; a run whose first bound is in a page not loaded, then loaded; a run whose last bound is not found; two runs in one stretch; overlapping runs; one run over two stretches; an open run with a line, with a summary and no new call, and with neither.
- `label.test.ts`: `lineOf`'s table tool by tool over invented inputs; `liveLine`'s clauses.
- `mirror.test.ts`: the label written on the closed row and on no other; a stretch regrouped and paged while its line keeps its words.
- `kept-ids.test.ts`: for every row kind, the id `keptIdsOf` gives is the id the row asks its site under (read from the row's own `…Input`); a merged read answers every member's id.
- Real events through the real derivation and the real mirror, with a stored-runs file in the mod's own form (the form of the file check 1's scratch run wrote: its structure, with invented text): a stored run whose `last` is a later read of a merged cell reads its summary; a run bounded by an arrival's carrier is placed; a bound that names nothing MAWS keeps reads `working record`.
- `BuiltInMods.test.tsx` and `MineAll.test.tsx`, one each way: the mods list draws the summary's switch; the same row under Customize draws none and is as it was.
- `view-routes.test.ts`: one recorded session with an invented folds file of shape 2 gives the same record lines from its wire and from its transcript file, and from another machine's rows, with no file, the live line alone.
- `stores/folds.test.ts`: the sequences.
- A18's lane-on case (`owner/view/perf.test.ts`) read again with five hundred stored runs (Concrete Steps 4): more than a fifth above the reading it is compared with, stop and report.

### M6 — The second pull request's scenes, the cross-build scenes, the record's second line, and its acceptance run

**Starts when** M5 and M7 are both built: it is the pull request's last milestone.

**What it touches.** `e2e/widgets.spec.ts` ("Version 8" becomes 9); `e2e/to-human.spec.ts` (the literal `'8'` becomes the name of the number a mirrored launch carries); `e2e/transcript.spec.ts` (the R3 `ToolGroup` case states the full site); its scene of the to-human view keeps U3b's assertions of Copy under the report's head; `e2e/to-human-live.spec.ts`, rewritten as the trial; `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts`, four scenes (§H; two at authoring, four at the I4 ruling); the record's second line, the dated line where the owner channel's state is defined, and the coverage table's rows, in the comment of `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts` that every later landing has extended ("UC-14's coverage for U13a … A later landing that stores something new adds its row here"); U13a's plan holds only U13a's own rows. For the summary (§M): `e2e/to-human.spec.ts` gains the scene "a record line reads its stored summary, a growing one its newest call, and working record where there is neither", whose scratch configuration directory holds an invented folds file for the recorded session, and which then replaces that file, by a rename, with one that holds one more run, and sees the line change with no reload at the mount's round or at the window's return of focus; the file is of shape 2, and a second case with the same runs at shape 1 reads `working record`; `e2e/widgets.spec.ts` gains the switch (its words, a press, the row read again); `e2e/sessions-available.spec.ts` reads the settings env of a launch made with the switch off; the trial and the cross-build scene *(to-human, to the peer)* gain the steps "Proof" names; `e2e/transcript.spec.ts`'s scene of the switch reads its button's box at least 24 px tall, and the caption and the button's text where they stood; in `docs/tech-debt-tracker.md` the row "the record line's summary in MAWS" goes and the rows of "The record" come. **And one measured variant** (the architect's ruling of 2026-11-16 (UTC) on M7's batch 0): the mirrored view's measured scene of `e2e/transcript.spec.ts` gains a variant whose scratch configuration directory holds an invented folds file in the mod's form (shape 2, the structure Step 0's scratch run recorded) for its session, so that the Mac measures the labelled case, base against landing, beside the existing unlabelled one; it runs only under the measured flag, as the existing scene does, and changes no group list or count of spec files.

**What it does not touch.** Product code, unless a scene shows a defect. `package.json`'s scripts.

**What it proves.** It writes the e2e halves of A15 and of §H; by its own run, typecheck, lint, the gate's script tests and the whole unit suite.

### P — The mod's pull request

Not a MAWS milestone: a branch in a worktree of the doperpowers repository, holding exactly Interfaces §I, with the mod's own suite green. It is prepared before Step 0 (check 1 runs against it), opened by the architect after check 1, and merged by the owner whenever they choose. Nothing in MAWS reads it but the trial.
