## Concrete Steps

`vm` stands for the build brief's helper with the pull request's branch (`vmcheck.sh <branch> -- '<command>'`): it runs its command on the build VM in a checkout of the pushed head and exits with the command's status. Never pipe it into `tail` or `head` without `set -o pipefail`. The VM is shared with a landing in build: a slow or timed-out check is read against its load first. Every command there runs under `timeout`.

1. **Base** (before each milestone and once more before each hand-back). Merge, never rebase, never force-push:

       git fetch origin master && git merge origin/master
       git grep -n "export function GroupRow" origin/master -- src/renderer/src/transcript/view/rows/Group.tsx
       git grep -n "revealed" origin/master -- src/renderer/src/transcript/model/reduce.ts
       git grep -n "PluginBand" origin/master -- src/renderer/src/composer/Composer.tsx
       git grep -n "selectWaitingByCall" origin/master -- src/renderer/src/stores/attention.ts
       git diff --stat origin/master -- src/renderer/src/transcript/index.ts src/renderer/src/transcript/index.test.ts

   The first four must answer; the last prints nothing. Then read "What is assumed of landings in flight" against what the merge brought. A row kind the merge added fails the typecheck of `mirror.ts` until it is classed (§B). Until the chain is on master the base is the chain's head merged into the branch, not master: the five commands above are read against `HEAD` in place of `origin/master` (they answer at `1ee6dca93`), and the merge of this step is of the chain's head the architect names. Before M4 also: `git grep -n "mirrorRows" HEAD -- src/renderer/src/owner/view/mirror.ts` answers (the base holds the first pull request). And `git merge-base --is-ancestor 11e183013 HEAD` exits 0: the base holds the view switch's fix of the first pull request (the branch's head `9e88e20a6` did not when this was written).

2. **Checks, per milestone**, after `git push`:

       vm 'timeout 900 pnpm exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.web.json --tsBuildInfoFile /dev/null'     # M1, M2, M5, M7
       vm 'timeout 900 pnpm exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.node.json --tsBuildInfoFile /dev/null'    # M4, and M2's and M5's shared files
       vm 'timeout 600 pnpm exec eslint <the paths changed>'
       vm 'timeout 900 pnpm exec vitest run <the folders of the milestone> test/support/tokens-guard.test.ts'

   The folders: M1 and M2, `src/renderer/src/owner src/renderer/src/stores src/renderer/src/transcript src/renderer/src/lane-a src/renderer/src/attention src/renderer/src/session src/renderer/src/composer src/renderer/src/widgets src/renderer/src/keymap src/shared/keymap apps/desk-web/src/views/project apps/desk-web/src/depth`; M4, `src/shared/owner src/shared/mods src/shared/ipc src/shared/machine src/services/owner src/services/sessions src/services/index src/engine-host`; M5, `src/renderer/src/transcript src/renderer/src/engine-surface src/renderer/src/owner src/renderer/src/stores src/shared/owner src/shared/engine`. M7, `src/renderer/src/owner src/renderer/src/stores src/renderer/src/transcript src/renderer/src/lane-a src/renderer/src/widgets apps/desk-web/src/depth`, with the desk web app's typecheck, build and test project as the static gate runs them. Expect `Test Files  N passed` and no failure. The set that fails on master too is the build brief's list.

3. **The wire's files** (M4, and again after a merge that moved `WIRE_MINOR`). Read `WIRE_MINOR` in `src/shared/ipc/wire.ts` after the merge (46 at this document's base and 51 at the merged head `1ee6dca93`: read it in the tree, never copy it from here). **The number M4 writes is a stand-in:** the next after the tree's, in one commit of its own that holds nothing but the minor, its comment, the two generated files and `routes.json`, so that at the chain merge the number this pull request finally takes replaces that one commit's content and nothing else, take the next number, and add to its comment "the UX wave, U2b: the owner channel lane's drawnBy, session.ui.renderSite, ownerChannel.folds, the mods row's summaries, mods.setFoldSummaries (N)". Make the two generated files on the VM and bring them back as a patch, by U2a's Concrete Steps 3 with this branch's name. Rewrite `src/shared/ipc/routes.json` there too, by its test's own switch (`MAWS_ROUTES_WRITE=1` on `src/shared/ipc/routes.test.ts`), and bring it back in the same patch: the three new commands fail that snapshot until it is rewritten. Expect a patch that touches those two files, changes the two `ownerChannel` entries and `mods.list`'s, adds the three commands and the minor, and removes nothing. If `wire.test.ts` says "breaking change", a schema was narrowed or a closed set gained a member: stop and report. Then, because a command's and an event's schema changed: `vm 'timeout 600 pnpm exec vitest run src/shared/machine'`.

4. **The derivation budget, before and after** (before M1's first commit and at M3's head; a relative reading):

       vm 'timeout 600 env MAWS_PERF=1 pnpm exec vitest run src/renderer/src/transcript/model/perf.test.ts'
       vm 'timeout 600 env MAWS_PERF=1 pnpm exec vitest run src/renderer/src/owner/view/perf.test.ts'     # the lane-on, mirrored case; never in one run with the other

   Write both printed lines into Surprises & Discoveries, and M1's new case's line (a lane-on session of 500 turns with a message in each, mirrored). If the head reads more than a fifth above the base, stop and report. At M7's head the lane-on case is read once more, with 500 stored runs handed to the mirror as labels, against the same case at the head M7 started from, alternately in one sitting; the same fifth applies.

5. **Check 1's fixtures** (M5). They are on this branch since 2026-11-16 (UTC), under `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/uxwave/` as `u2b-mod-*`, with the findings file `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-U2b-mod-answers.txt`; the fixtures whose name ends in `-fix` were recorded on the mod's final head, and the first round's are kept as the record of the defect. `mod-route.test.tsx` loads them by name; it invents no answer. If a recorded file is added or edited on the branch: `vm 'timeout 300 node --test .github/scripts/maws-app-scan.test.mjs'`. **The shared table of cases** (M7): copy `to-human/tests/mods/fold-label-cases.json` from P's branch, byte for byte, to `src/renderer/src/owner/view/fold-label-cases.json`; the digest `label-cases.test.ts` pins is the one P's own test pins (§I, item 13). The file is edited in P's repository only, and a change there is carried here as a new copy with its new digest. It holds invented rows; run the same scan over it.

6. **Acceptance** (M3 for the first pull request, M6 for the second). The suites of "Acceptance" for that pull request by name; then

       vm 'timeout 1200 pnpm typecheck'
       vm 'timeout 900 pnpm exec eslint <every folder the pull request changed> e2e apps/desk-web/e2e'
       vm 'timeout 600 node --test .github/scripts/*.test.mjs scripts/*.test.mjs'
       vm 'timeout 2400 pnpm test'

   and at M3 on the MacBook, each printing nothing:

       git grep -n '"To you"' -- src apps
       git grep -n "owner_record\|OwnerRecordRow\|ReportBar\|reportOpenTurns" -- src e2e ':!e2e/to-human-live.spec.ts'
       git grep -n "Working record ·" -- src/renderer e2e

   The second grep leaves out `e2e/to-human-live.spec.ts` by name: it is the second pull request's file (M6 rewrites it as the trial scene), it always skips today, and its one hit (`[data-kind="owner_record"]` in its G3 step) goes with that rewrite; M6's acceptance runs the grep without the exclusion. The desk web app keeps its own "Working record · …" label in its conversation view and the scenes that read it; they are outside these paths and stay. At M6: `git grep -n "MOD_DRAWS_FROM\|MOD_FAILED" -- src` prints nothing (no constant names a release of the mod). And `git grep -n "model\.complete\|haiku\|LABEL_SYSTEM" -- src/renderer/src/owner src/renderer/src/stores/folds.ts src/services/owner src/shared/owner` prints nothing but comments that cite the mod's functions (MAWS names no model and asks none for a summary).

7. **Stop and hand back** with: the head's sha; each command above with its result; what was not verified; the decisions made beyond this document; the scenes written and not run; any `gitleaks.toml` entry. The executor runs no `scripts/ci-gate.sh`, opens no pull request, and starts no Electron and no engine.

## Interfaces and Dependencies

§A to §E are what the milestones produce; §F to §H are what crosses builds and installations; §I is the mod's pull request; §J to §L are the other landings and the desk web app. §M is the record line's summary, which crosses all of them.

### §A. Who draws (M4; the cutover's switch in M5)

In `src/shared/owner/contract.ts`:

    export const TO_HUMAN_CONTRACT_VERSION = 9;                  // advertised where the mod draws MAWS's view
    export const TO_HUMAN_MIRROR_VERSION = 8;                    // advertised where MAWS draws it: every installation steps aside and registers its tool
    export const TO_HUMAN_MOD_ROUTE: boolean = false;            // M5's cutover turns it on; while it is off no launch advertises 9
    export const TO_HUMAN_DECLARATION = 'maws-surface.json';     // at an installation's root (§I)

In `src/shared/owner/draws.ts` (new, pure, no I/O):

    export type DrawnBy = 'mod' | 'maws';
    export type Advertisement = 9 | 8 | null;                    // null: the launch carried none (the lane off, or a style that carries no tool)
    export function declaresDrawing(file: unknown, at: number): boolean;
    export function advertisementFor(lane: boolean, installations: readonly { declares: boolean }[]): Advertisement;
    export interface Reservation { launchId: string | null; number: Advertisement; plugin: boolean; at: string; prior?: { launchId: string; number: Advertisement; plugin: boolean } }   // prior: the bound slot while this one is unbound (restated 2026-11-16)
    export type Known = { known: true; number: Advertisement; plugin: boolean | null } | { known: false };
    export function advertisementOf(row: { launchId: string | null; desired: ContractSource | null; advertised: Reservation | null } | null, live: string): Known;
    export function drawnBy(advertisement: Known, status: { launchId: string; process: EngineSessionStatus['process'] } | null, loaded: { present: boolean; declares: boolean } | null): DrawnBy;

`declaresDrawing`: the parsed file is an object whose `toHumanView.drawsFrom` is an integer not above `at`; a missing, unreadable or otherwise shaped file does not declare. `advertisementFor`: nothing when the lane carries nothing; 9 when the switch is on, the list is not empty and every installation declares; else 8. `Reservation.plugin` is whether the launch found an applicable, enabled installation of the mod whose hooks load. `row` is null when the session has no owner row or the row cannot be read.

**Who draws: the table the code and its tests are checked against** (restated 2026-11-16 (UTC), at the architect's ruling after M4's frontier review: Decision Log, "M4, who draws, closed as one table"; it replaces the prose rows this section first gave). It is read in three steps: what the row says the running launch carried (A), what the facts of the running launch say (B), and which statuses count (C). Two properties are checked over every cell of B, each against what the launch actually carried and what the installation actually does (the mod at 8, and an installation without the declaration at 9, keep the engine's drawing at every site, check 1's A1; a declaring installation at 9, and any installation with no advertisement, draw the view):

- **Never both,** except B9 under its one condition (below). A cell reads `maws` only on evidence that the mod draws no view for that launch: a carried 8 known from a bound record or an earlier build's recorded launch; plugins reported without the mod; a carried 9 with the loaded installation's declaration read and absent; or the launch at rest. A guess never reads `maws`.
- **Neither, only where named.** The cells where neither side draws a view are marked *neither* in B, each with why it is accepted and what ends it. In each the person sees the full transcript with no fold: `drawnBy` is `mod`, so MAWS draws no mirror and folds no turn once the session holds a message, and every site's answer keeps the engine's drawing, in which MAWS mounts its own row (a message drawn as a message, M2's `MessageView`; M5 pins it over check 1's `u2b-mod-8-*` fixtures).

**A. `advertisementOf(row, live)`** (the row's two slots as "Two slots" below keeps them; "recorded" is the row's `launch_id`, which this build writes at the bind and an earlier build at its own record):

| | the row | read as | why that is evidence, or why not |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | the row cannot be read, or there is none | not known | — |
| A2 | the bound slot (the bound reservation, or the unbound one's `prior`) is bound to `live` | its number and `plugin` | written before the send and bound at the host's answer for this launch |
| A3 | `live` is the recorded launch and no slot is bound to it | nothing when `desired` is `off`, else 8; `plugin` unknown | an earlier build advertises 8 or nothing; this build records a launch with no bound reservation only when the reservation's write failed, and then sent 8 or nothing |
| A4 | `live` is not the recorded launch and no slot is bound to it, the unbound reservation included | not known | an unbound reservation for an unrecorded launch is no evidence of what that launch carried: it may be the launch whose bind a daemon's stop lost, or one an earlier build, or this build after a failed write, sent after it |
| A5 | `desired` is null where A3 would read it | not known | — |

The start's reconciliation binds nothing: by A4 there is no reservation it could prove the running launch's. A launch read through A4 stays not known until its next launch, which this build decides and records.

**B. `drawnBy(advertisement, engine, loaded)`.** "The engine" is the running launch's state by C: *at rest* (a status of the running launch says `exiting` or `exited`, or a status read says the session has no live process); *not yet reported* (live, or not yet discovered: before the first status read, or a launch bound before its first status, both counted live with plugins not reported); *reported* (live, plugins reported). "The declaration" is the one read at the loaded plugin's reported path for the latest observation of plugins: *yes*, *no* (the file is missing from an installation directory that is present, or does not parse to the declaring shape), *outstanding* (the read has not answered), *failed* (the installation directory itself is gone, or an I/O error other than "no such file"). An installation's path names its version (the plugin cache keeps one directory per release), so a directory that is present holds what the engine loaded from it; one that is gone says nothing of what was loaded (restated 2026-11-16 (UTC), at M4's second review).

| | the advertisement (A) | the engine | the plugin and its declaration | drawn by | if neither: why accepted, and what ends it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | any | at rest | any | `maws` | — (no engine; the mirror) |
| B2 | 8 | not yet reported or reported | any | `maws` | — |
| B3 | 9 | not yet reported | — | `mod` | *neither* if the installation that loads does not declare or none loads: the launch was decided on declaring installations, so only a change between the decision and the load gives it; ends when the plugins are reported |
| B4 | 9 | reported | the mod loaded, declaration yes | `mod` | — |
| B5 | 9 | reported | the mod loaded, declaration outstanding or failed | `mod` | *neither* if it does not declare; ends when the read answers, or at the next observation |
| B6 | 9 | reported | the mod loaded, declaration no | `maws` | — (the declaration ships in the same commit as the gate that draws at 9, §I item 2, and the directory read is the loaded release's own: a release without it steps aside) |
| B7 | 9 | reported | the mod not loaded | `maws` | — |
| B8 | nothing | not yet reported | the launch's `plugin` true or unknown | `mod` | *neither* if no installation loads; ends when the plugins are reported |
| B9 | nothing | not yet reported | the launch's `plugin` false | `maws` | — (the launch found no installation whose hooks load). **The table's one named exception:** both can draw only if a to-human plugin is installed or enabled within the launch's own startup; it ends when the plugins report, or at the next launch (the architect, 2026-11-16 (UTC)) |
| B10 | nothing | reported | the mod loaded | `mod` | — |
| B11 | nothing | reported | the mod not loaded | `maws` | — |
| B12 | not known | not yet reported | — | `mod` | *neither* if the launch carried 8, or 9 with no declaring installation, or nothing with none loaded; ends when the plugins are reported (if the mod is not loaded) or at the next launch (decision 4's cost: a launch at 8 shows no fold) |
| B13 | not known | reported | the mod loaded, whatever its declaration | `mod` | *neither* as B12; ends at the next launch |
| B14 | not known | reported | the mod not loaded | `maws` | — |

B8 restates this section's first table, which read a launch with nothing advertised and `plugin` unknown as `maws` before the plugins report: an earlier build's lane-off launch with the plugin installed would have been drawn by both until then.

**C. Which status counts.** The running launch is the one a status of a live process names; a status is applied only for it:

| | the status | effect |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | a status of the running launch | applied (its process, its plugins, a new `pluginsObservedAt` starting a declaration read that replaces any outstanding one) |
| C2 | a status of another launch saying `exiting` or `exited` | ignored: it never puts the running launch at rest, whether or not its id was seen before |
| C3 | a status of a launch not seen before, with a live process | that launch becomes the running one (a launch another client or an earlier build made), unless the host's own start times (`launchedAt`) of both show it older than the running one; where the running launch's start time is not known (bound, no status yet), the order cannot be told and the unseen live launch becomes the running one, which A4 then reads not known: never `maws` on a guess. The daemon's own clock (a bind's time) orders nothing (restated 2026-11-16 (UTC), at M4's second review) |
| C4 | a snapshot (the start's read, a reconnect's) of a session for which a newer event has been applied | not applied over it |

**The tests of the table.** Every cell has at least one case, and every case's name begins with the cell ids it pins: `src/shared/owner/draws.test.ts` (the pure functions, cell by cell), `src/services/owner/drawn-by.test.ts` (the tracker, held in order), `restart.test.ts`, `store.test.ts`, `launch.test.ts`, `register.test.ts`, `service.test.ts` and `declaration.test.ts` under `src/services/owner/`. A5 cannot occur in a row either build writes (`desired` is written with every `launch_id`) and is pinned by the pure function alone. B9's reading and its end at the plugins' report: `drawn-by.test.ts`, "B8, B9, B10 · …".

`OwnerChannelStateSchema.lane` gains `drawnBy: z.enum(['mod', 'maws']).optional()`. This build always sends it; absent reads as `maws`. `ResolvedStyle` gains `toHuman: { paths: string[]; hooksLoad: boolean }`, the install paths `pluginInstallPaths` accepts for the `to-human` plugin and whether no setting keeps its hooks from loading (it already computes both for `messageTool`). `OwnerChannelFlags` carries the advertisement in place of `laneOn`. It also carries `summariesOff: boolean` (§M, "The switch"). The store gains `reserveLaunch(appSessionId, reservation)`, written before the send, and `recordLaunch` binds the reservation's `launchId`; both write one added column:

    { table: 'owner_channel_sessions', column: 'advertised', definition: 'TEXT' }   // ADDED_COLUMNS, src/services/index/migrations.ts; the Reservation as JSON

A value that does not parse is no reservation.

**Two slots** (restated 2026-11-16 (UTC), at the architect's ruling on M4's gap; Decision Log, "M4, the reservation's two slots"). The column holds the reservation last bound to a launch, and at most one reservation not yet bound. A new reservation replaces only the unbound one and never the bound one; binding moves the unbound one into the bound slot. So the bound reservation of the running launch survives any number of refused relaunches in a row. In the stored form the unbound reservation carries the bound slot as its `prior` (a replacement of an unbound reservation carries its `prior` forward); a bound reservation has no `prior`. A rollback on the host's refusal is rejected: a daemon that ends before the rollback loses it.

**What a window draws under each published value.** `maws`: the mirror while the lane is on and the session holds a message. `mod`, with the session's surface attached in this window: no mirror and no switch; no turn fold once the session holds a message; every ask as §E gives it. `mod` with no surface attached in this window (a session at rest is never published `mod`; a session on another machine publishes nothing here): the mirror. Under decision 4's reading of an advertisement that is not known, a launch that in fact carried 8 shows no fold until the row can be read again or the session's next launch: every row in sight, each message drawn as a message.

### §B. The mirror's rule (M1)

Every clause carries, in a comment, the function of the mod it ports and the release it was read at (0.8.2; lines of `to-human/hooks/mods/to-human.tsx`). The mod's row kinds are `user`, `marked`, `mixed` and `record` (`RowKind`, line 319); here they are `stays`, `stays`, `mixed` and `folds`.

| MAWS's row | class | ports |
|---|---|---|
| `user` (a prompt, a steer, one still waiting in the queue's tail) | stays | `promptRow` (400 to 417): an origin that is not in `DELIVERED` (329) is the person's |
| `bash` (the person's own `!` command) | stays | the person's input; the mod is never asked. *Decided here* |
| `assistant_text` whose words are all inside messages | stays | `AssistantMessage`, `marked`: `spanRows` (1411) |
| `assistant_text` with a message and words outside it | mixed | `mixed`: the messages stay, its own words open with the stretch after it (`runStart`, 430) |
| `assistant_text` with no message (commentary, an unmarked answer, a reply an interrupt cut) | folds | `record` |
| `to_human` (a message-tool call, waiting for its result or sent) | stays | `isRead` (605) for the message tool; drawn by `spanRows` |
| a message-tool call that sent nothing (refused, failed, interrupted, empty): it is a `cell` | folds | `isUnsent` (623) |
| `group`, `cell` | folds | `recordRow` (1187) at `ToolGroup`, `ToolUse` and `ToolResult` |
| `thinking` | folds | the engine draws a thought's text through `AssistantMessage` with `isSummary` ("Today"), where the mod reads a text with no message: `record`. *Decided here* for a thought MAWS alone draws (card 5) |
| `subagent` | stays | `isSpawn` (598): `Agent` and `Workflow` |
| `question`, `ask`, `plan`, `request_slot`, waiting or answered | stays | `isRead` for `AskUserQuestion` and the ask tools; UC-3 for a waiting form |
| `agent_message` (to or from a subagent, between sessions, one the agent sent) | folds | `promptRow`: a row with `from` is `record`; a sent message is a tool call |
| `notice`, class `answer`, where no `question` or `ask` row among the rows with its `toolUseId` carries its receipt (a `question` with answers, an `ask` answered or cancelled) | stays | `ASK_DELIVERY` (378): the person's answer is `user` |
| `notice`, class `answer`, where such a row carries the receipt | folds | R13: the mod leaves the question's call in view (`isRead`, 605), so row and delivery would state one answer twice |
| `notice`, class `task`, outcome `failed`, whose `toolUseId` is the call of a `subagent` row among the rows | stays | `promptRow`'s first clause: a failed task of a spawn the transcript shows is `marked` |
| any other `notice` (a task's end, a scheduled trigger, the bridge's wake) | folds | `promptRow`: a row with `task` is `record`; `DELIVERED` |
| `error`, at every severity | stays | `isApiError` (616): an API error is `marked` and ends a run; a retry line and a plugin's notice have no site, and a row the mod is never asked about stays. *Decided here* (card 3) |
| `boundary`, `gap`, `local_command`, `artifact`, `delivery`, `side_thread` | stays | the child's table: rows MAWS draws that the mod is never asked about |
| `agent_list`, `browser_action` | folds | tool calls: `recordRow` at `ToolUse`. *Decided here* (card 3) |
| `browser_activity` (no call of this session behind it) | stays | never asked |
| `activity` (U3b: the line of a compaction under way) | stays | MAWS's own line; in the app it is placed after the mirror and never reaches it |
| `browser_activity` under a call of this session | folds | that call's work: `recordRow` (M1's decision (h)) |
| `time`, `divider` | member of no stretch | decision 11 |
| `receipt` | draws nothing today | |
| `record_fold` | the mirror's own row | |

The class is one exhaustive `switch` over `Row['kind']` with no default branch.

**In force,** where MAWS draws: the store's `noTurnFold` is set (decision 8), which is: the lane is on and the rows, those handed in from outside the store included, hold a row classed `stays` or `mixed` that is an `assistant_text` or a `to_human`, a live text block with an open mark included (`isFolding`, 1512; `hasSeenMark` set at 1713 to 1718 and 1801 to 1804). **A stretch** is a maximal run of consecutive `folds` rows, the rows of no stretch between them included, headed by the `mixed` row directly before it if there is one; a `mixed` row with nothing folding after it is a stretch of its own (`runStart`, `runEnd`, 430 to 470). **Its key** is decision 9's. **Closed,** it draws its head with only its messages in sight, then one `record_fold` row reading `› working record` in the place of its first folding row. **Open,** it draws its head whole and every row as the plain transcript draws it, then one `record_fold` row reading `‹ fold to report` after its last row. All stretches start closed. In the full transcript nothing is folded.

### §C. The mirror's function and state (M1)

In `src/renderer/src/owner/view/mirror.ts`:

    export type ViewClass = 'stays' | 'folds' | 'mixed' | 'none';
    export const reportViewOf: (ui: Pick<TranscriptUi, 'ownerLane' | 'view'> | undefined) => boolean;        // moved, unchanged
    export function liveMarkedRowIds(state: TranscriptState | undefined): string;                              // moved, unchanged
    export function holdsMessage(rows: readonly Row[], liveMarked?: string): boolean;
    export function stretchKey(row: Row): string;
    export interface Stretch { key: string; head: Row | null; rows: readonly Row[]; turns: ReadonlySet<TurnId> }
    export function stretchesOf(rows: readonly Row[], liveMarked?: string, open?: ReadonlySet<string>): Stretch[];
    export function stretchHolding(rows: readonly Row[], id: RowId | CellId, liveMarked?: string, open?: ReadonlySet<string>): string | null;
    export function mirrorRows(rows: readonly Row[], ui: Pick<TranscriptUi, 'openStretches'>, liveMarked?: string): Row[];

As built, the file also exports `liveMarksOf`, `mirrorsView`, `lostKey`, `carriedKeys`, `keysOfStretch` and `openKeysOf` (the Decision Log's entries of M1 and of the whole-branch review). `mirrorRows` is pure and is memoised by its rendering callers (`Transcript` and `useDrawnRowCount`; `drawnRows` calls it at each read) on the rows' identity, the open set and the live marks. Its signature is as above at `1ee6dca93`, three arguments, so M7's added argument is its fourth. From M7 it takes a last, optional argument, the labels (§M), which joins its callers' memo. Its callers apply it only while `ui.noTurnFold` is set, the view is the report and `ui.modDraws` is not set; it scans for no message itself.

Rows (`transcript/model/rows.ts`): `{ kind: 'record_fold'; stretch: string; open: boolean }` replaces `owner_record`, its id `rowId.recordFold(stretch, open)`; `assistant_text` gains `reportOnly?: true`, set by the mirror alone on a closed stretch's head. From M7 a closed `record_fold` row carries `label?: string` (§M); its id does not take it.

View state (`TranscriptUi`): `openStretches: Set<string>` replaces `reportOpenTurns`; `noTurnFold?: boolean`; `tailMessage?: boolean` (what `Transcript` reports of the rows handed in from outside); `modDraws?: boolean` (declared since M1, and already read by `mirrorsView` and by the switch; set, and joined to the flag, in M5: nothing sets it today). `ownerLane`, `view` and `revealed` stay. Store actions: `toggleStretch(key, stretch)`, which answers the keys the other line can carry, replaces `toggleReportRecord`; `setToHumanView(key, { lane, tailMessage, modDraws? })` replaces `setOwnerLane` and recomputes `noTurnFold` in the same update; `setView` stays; `clearRevealed(key, id)` (M5). `drawnRows(key, tail, atRest)` and `useDrawnRowCount(key, atRest)` keep their names and signatures.

### §D. The drawing and the words (M1 for the record's two lines, M2 for the rest)

`src/renderer/src/owner/view/copy.ts`, the only place these strings are written:

| what | text |
|---|---|
| a message's label | `● message`; a later message of the same text block, `message`; while it streams, the label and ` …` |
| the record line | `› ` and its label: the stretch's newest call or its stored summary (§M, from M7), else `› working record`; in the first pull request always `› working record` |
| the line under an opened record | `‹ fold to report` |
| the switch, in the report | `to-human view · full transcript ›` |
| the switch, in the full transcript | `to-human view · report only ›` |
| kept for the desk web app's bar | `Session view`, `Report`, `Transcript` |
| kept for the mods list | the style-mismatch sentence, as it reads today |
| the palette's command (M2) | `Mark all read`: the one string of this table written outside `copy.ts`, as the title of its row in `src/shared/keymap/actions.ts` (§K), where every action's title is written and which shared code cannot import from the renderer |
| the notice when "Toggle report and transcript" is used where the mod draws (M5) | `The to-human mod draws this session’s view. Use its switch above the composer.` |

They are `STYLE`, `UNFOLD`, `FOLD` (555 to 564), `'fold to report'` (1218) and the band's caption and labels (1957 to 1963) of the mod. Components: `MessageView` (`owner/view/Message.tsx`: the label in `var(--accent)` at weight 600, the body under it indented two columns, in the normal text colour, as Markdown); `RecordFold` (`owner/view/RecordFold.tsx`: one button, muted, full contrast on hover, at the transcript's ordinary row spacing with no extra row above it and no bullet before it (ruling 3, items ii and iii); `aria-expanded`; the lower line indented two columns; from M7 the closed line is one line, a label longer than the column cut with an ellipsis by the stylesheet, the button's accessible name being its whole text); `ViewSwitch` (`owner/view/Switch.tsx`: the caption muted, the label a button, in a frame of its own (`section[data-view-switch]`) drawn as `PluginBand` draws its frame; its exported `toggleView` is the handler of `transcript.toggleReport`).

### §E. Where the mod draws (M5; the command in M4)

`modDraws` is true for a tab when the lane's published `drawnBy` is `mod` and the session's surface is attached in this window (`useSurfaceAttached`). A session on another machine never has it: its site asks do not cross the machine link.

**The rule (R3).** Whether a row is in sight where the mod draws comes from an ask that row holds while it is mounted. An invalidation re-asks every mounted ask, so a first mark, a press of the mod's switch and a reload each reach every mounted row by itself; a row that is not mounted is asked when it mounts.

| MAWS's row | the site it asks | with | new here |
|---|---|---|---|
| a run (`group`) | `ToolGroup`, as a full site | as today; `groupInput` unchanged (a run that is a jump's target, holds one, or holds a waiting call asks nothing and is drawn by MAWS) | `drawingOnly` goes: an answer with no engine slot replaces the run, one that keeps it draws MAWS's `GroupRow` there |
| a message-tool call (`to_human`) | `ToolUse` | the call's own props, as a sent message's are built (`sentInput`); MAWS's `MessageView` is its own drawing. No `ToolResult` is asked | yes |
| a marked text, a prompt, a single call, a command's output, a question | as today | `MessageView` as a marked text's own drawing | |
| a thought (`thinking`; outside a run it has text or is still streaming, and one with no text yet asks nothing and is drawn) | `AssistantMessage` | `{ text, isFirstOfReply: false, isSummary: true }`, the engine's own props for a thought; **the id is the thought row's own uuid, bare** (check 1's C2: under it the thought folds with its run, opens with it and is the engine's drawing in the full transcript; under a uuid qualified by a block it is not placed) | yes |
| an agent listing, a browser action | `ToolUse` | the call's props | yes |
| an arrival or a notice for which U3a's `engineUserProps` gives props | `UserMessage` | as today | |
| an arrival or a notice for which it gives none (a scheduled trigger, the bridge's wake, a task's end the host made, a message line with no envelope) | `UserMessage` | the row's own line as `text`, and the engine's origin for its class: a task's end `task-notification` with `task` from the row's `taskId`, `outcome` and `toolUseId`; a scheduled trigger `scheduled-trigger`; the wake `plugin` (named `sminos`); a message between sessions or from a subagent `peer` with `from` | yes: U3a's ask widened |
| an answer's notice whose question's row carries the receipt (§B) | `UserMessage` | its line as `text`, origin `plugin`: a delivery, which the mod folds | yes (R13). One whose question's row carries no receipt asks nothing and stays |
| a closed turn's line | `TurnDuration`, through `session.ui.renderSite` | `{ word, durationMs, isExpanded: true }`, the engine's props for its closing line; no chevron | yes |
| the running turn's line | nothing new: its `Spinner` ask for its words stays as today | MAWS draws it: it is what shows that the session works | |
| a time separator | none of its own: it keeps alive the ask of the row after it (a probe at that row's own address, so one ask) | drawn while that answer leaves anything in sight: MAWS's drawing, or a drawing of the mod's with ink | yes |
| a `browser_activity` row under a call | none of its own: it follows its call. As a time separator does, it keeps alive the ask of the row that holds its call (a probe at that row's own address, so one ask), and is drawn exactly when that row is shown; so it folds with the call, and decision 14 holds | — | yes (reported point 24) |
| an error, a boundary, a gap, a subagent's row, `ask`, `plan`, `request_slot`, an artifact's, a delivered file's and a side thread's row, the person's `!` command, standalone browser activity | nothing | they stay on both routes | |

**An ask's id where the transcript holds no record of the row.** The mod places a row by finding its id in the transcript file. A turn's line and a notice the host made have none (a headless session writes no `turn_duration` row). Their id is `<anchor>~<tag>`: `anchor` is the id under which the nearest row before it asks and which the file holds (a message's uuid, a call's id); `tag` is `turn` for a turn's line and the row's own id otherwise. **For a turn's line "the nearest row before it" is the row that opens the turn,** and for one reason only: MAWS draws a turn's line at the head of the turn's work (the prompt, the line, then the turn's rows). The mod reads the turn forward from the line: the line shows while a run that begins at or after its place, and before the next row of kind `user`, is unfolded (§I, item 5; recorded in C1 with the line anchored at the turn's prompt). A line anchored after the turn's last message is not an ask MAWS sends, and the mod's hook says so where it states the contract. The mod places an anchored ask at its anchor's place plus a small fraction (§I, "As built"), so it stands before whatever followed the anchor. The mod's pull request places such an ask directly after its anchor (§I). An id is at most 256 characters on MAWS's wire.

**The command.** `session.ui.renderSite` has `session.ui.render`'s input and output, but for its `component`, a string of 1 to 64 characters that the engine host checks against its own list (`TurnDuration` at this landing). It exists because `session.ui.render` names its sites in a closed set on the wire, and an added member there is a wire major. An engine host of an earlier build answers `unknown_command`; the row then draws MAWS's own, always.

**An answer's action row where the mod draws** (reported point 23, as ruled). U3b's action row (Copy, Fork, the time; `AnswerActions`, mounted in `view/rows/Row.tsx`) stands only under a row with a drawn body. A row whose site answer draws nothing has no action row: it is not laid out, so nothing of it can be hovered or reached by the keyboard. Nothing is placed anew for the mod's route: the placement is U3b's own (the last drawn answer block of the turn, or a stretch's head text where MAWS mirrors), and a turn that draws neither has no action row until its record is opened. Copy still takes the turn's whole answer from the turn's projection, never from what is drawn.

**Which ids the mod places** (check 1's C2). The mod finds a row by `rowKey`: a 36-character uuid by its first 24 characters, any other id whole. So a row asks under its own uuid, or under an id of another shape that the transcript holds whole (a call's). A uuid qualified by a block (`<uuid>:thinking:0`, `<uuid>:text:0`) has no place: the mod guesses it past every row, where, folded, it draws a record line of its own and stays folded when its run opens. Not known: whether the engine's own derived form of a later block's id (the uuid's first 24 characters, then the block's index in twelve digits) is placed when a client sends it. The probe's one case of it was confounded by the seed's ids, was removed, and proves nothing either way; the mod's table of cases holds the form as the mod's own reading ("a row drawn under its block's derived id"), which is not a recording of the engine. **A text asks under its bare uuid on every recorded engine:** each `assistant` frame in the recordings carries one block under its own uuid (reported point 22, with the count), so the block is its message's first and `assistantInput` asks under the uuid alone. The qualified form `<uuid>:text:<n>` is reached only by a frame that carries two text blocks under one uuid, which no recording shows; it is the unplaced case, and a tracker row keeps it until a recording holds one.

**A lone call that becomes a run** (check 1, found beside the table; the architect's ruling: fixed in the mod, MAWS's asks unchanged). This section's pattern stands as written: a call alone asks `ToolUse` under its own id, and from its second call the run asks `ToolGroup` under `group:<its first call>`, the lone row no longer mounted. As 0.8.2 and the mod's branch first stood, the lone row kept the run's place and its button, the group answered an empty Box, and a live stretch lost its line at its second call. The mod now makes the lone row one row with the group that holds its call (`joinGroup`; §I, item 14), and invalidates once when it does. Recorded with this pattern at 9 and with no advertisement: the group answers the newest call's line at each step and then the summary, and from the switch on the engine's pushes name the group's instance (`u2b-mod-mock-grow-9-after-fix`, `u2b-mod-mock-grow-none-after-fix`, `u2b-mod-summary-fix`). A lone `ToolUse` still mounted after the switch answers an empty Box (recorded), and the engine's row if its run is unfolded (the mod's code, not recorded). No `ToolResult` ask for a call inside a group is handled by the mod, and none is needed: MAWS asks none (the table above).

**A call's ask never changes kind over its row's life** (restated 2026-11-16 (UTC), at the architect's ruling on M5's finding; Decision Log, "M5, a call's ask by its row's live identity"). The paragraph above holds for the terminal's order of asks, not for MAWS's. MAWS draws a live run of one as a run row (`group:cell:<call>`, U2a), so in MAWS a live call that the derivation draws as a run asks `ToolGroup` from its first call; when its turn closes, U2a's derivation makes a single call a `cell` row, which asked `ToolUse`, and the mod (its branch at `e511aad9`, the `ToolUse` hook) answered that as a member of a group no mounted row asks any longer, so a closed stretch of one call lost its record line. The rule, in a session the mod draws: a call that the live derivation draws as a run (of one, or more) asks `ToolGroup` under the id its live run asks under (`group:<call>`, the id check 1 recorded; the run's row id is `group:cell:<call>`, under which its open state is read) with its calls when its turn is closed too; a call that never joins a run asks `ToolUse`, as today. Which of the two a closed single call is, is decided from the cell alone, by the same test the live derivation uses, never from a memory of an earlier ask; and by the part of that test that does not move over the call's life: the tool's own row of the classification table (`tableRowOf(tool).groupable`, `transcript/model/classify.ts`), not what a result later makes of it (an image result, a refusal: each turns a call standalone once it arrives, after the call was asked as a run; restated 2026-11-16 (UTC), at M5's second review): a window opened later, or a session read from its file, asks exactly what the live window asked. It is the recorded pattern (check 1's E3, `-group`: a group of one keeps its line), and the mod's own kit holds it at the mod's branch `e79f701fe72e488155e6b43d4e9f7cd0bcd9ffc0` (tests only, each at 9 and with no advertisement): a group of one asked while the turn runs and again after its end keeps the stretch's line and opens; asked with no earlier state for an ended turn it draws the line (`working record`, the new tracker holding no run for it) and opens; a second call joining under the same id moves the line to it; and the sequence MAWS must not send, a group of one then `ToolUse` for the same call, answers an empty member row with nothing that opens. What MAWS draws where it draws by itself (the mirror, the plain transcript) does not change: this is the ask's identity on the mod's route only.

**After a reload MAWS re-asks by itself** (check 1's C4). `reload_plugins` pushed no invalidation in the four runs that asked one row alone, and one where the installation changed; so a push at a reload cannot be relied on. MAWS re-asks a session's mounted sites only at an engine invalidation today (`engine-surface/site.ts`, `connectEngineSurface`). M5 adds the other trigger there: a status of the session whose `pluginsObservedAt` differs from the last one seen invalidates the session's mounted sites, exactly as an invalidation does.

**Check 1 answered yes** for every site of questions C (a thought under its bare uuid), so no row takes the ruling's second means, and it is not built. It stays written as what a later failure of a site would take: **if a site answers no,** that row takes the ruling's second means, and only that row: while mounted it keeps alive, by probes at their own addresses, the asks of the first asked row of each stretch it depends on (a turn's line: of each stretch of its turn), and is drawn while one of them shows MAWS's drawing; and, for a row whose stretch has no asked row, one ask mounted once per transcript reads the mod's mode from the band's answer, where the mod's pull request then marks it with a keyed node (`to-human-view:report` or `to-human-view:full`). If neither can be built for a row, that comes back to the architect as a card for the owner before M5: no tracker row narrows "the full transcript folds nothing".

**The reveal's end (R6).** `Transcript.tsx` keeps, for the current jump, whether its containing row has been in the list's mounted range since that jump; the containing row is found by locating the revealed id among the drawn rows at each change of them (a cell's is the run that holds it). When it has been in the range and no longer is, `clearRevealed` is called. A jump whose row has not yet mounted keeps its reveal.

**Known differences between the mod's view and the mirror,** each a tracker row and a line of the trial's reading: fold states are not carried when a session starts or stops running; a jump opens the record where MAWS draws and reveals the row where the mod does; the mod looks for a session's first message only since the last compaction at its start; the seconds between a stretch's close and its summary, when the mod's line still reads its last call and the mirror's reads `working record` (§M); a summary in a session on another machine and in the desk web app (decided for this landing); in a session whose engine host is of an earlier build, a turn's line is always drawn; a mark written after a backslash (`\<message>`) is literal text in MAWS and a mark in the mod (`parseMarked`), a difference the mod's pull request (P) decides (the architect, 2026-11-15). A mark left open in a completed text, a stray closing tag and crossing marks are text in MAWS, as the shared grammar reads them for main's stored marks, previews and channel, and the mod's `parseMarked` reads the first two as messages and the third as two runs; MAWS does not follow at this landing (ruled 2026-11-16 (UTC); Decision Log): the shared grammar stays as main's stored marks, previews and channel read it, and a later change of it decides, with its wire minor. Now that the mod draws the view, a person can see it: for a text with an open mark, a stray closing tag or crossing marks, the view (the mod's reading) and a preview or the channel (MAWS's reading) can differ.

### §F. What is stored and sent, against both earlier builds

`git show f1852de3a:<path>` and `git show 2c7044231:<path>` give the same text, and the same as this document's base, for the three files this table rests on: `src/shared/owner/state.ts`, `src/services/owner/store.ts` and `src/shared/ipc/tolerant.ts`. The summary's rows rest on two more: `src/shared/mods/registry.ts` (the same text at both) and `src/services/index/migrations.ts`, where `index_meta` is at the installed build and not at F. All of it is the second pull request's; the first stores and sends nothing new.

| what | an earlier build with this build's value | this build with an earlier build's | this build, then an earlier one, then this build |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lane.drawnBy`, on `ownerChannel.state` and `ownerChannel.changed` (the wire; an optional key) | its main reads the supervisor's output with the tolerant reader, drops the key and keeps the state; its renderer draws its own report | absent: `maws` | nothing kept |
| the command `session.ui.renderSite` (the wire; a new command, in the same minor) | never sends it; as a host, answers `unknown_command` | this build's window on an earlier host: a turn's line draws MAWS's own | nothing kept |
| `owner_channel_sessions.advertised` (the index; an added nullable column, `user_version` unmoved) | opens the index; reads the row with `SELECT *` by column name and ignores it; its `recordLaunch` updates the columns it names and leaves it | null: the advertisement is read from the row's `desired` (§A) | the earlier build's launch changed `launch_id`; a reservation bound to another launch, or unbound while `launch_id` is the running launch's, is not read as that launch's |
| `MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN=9` in a launch's settings env (the engine's process, not MAWS's storage) | a session launched at 9 keeps it until it ends; an earlier window draws its own report beside whatever the mod draws (§H, row 5) | its launches carry 8, or nothing with the lane off | this build's next launch decides again |
| the mods list's version (`mods.list`, an integer) | reads 9 where it read 8 | reads 8 | |
| the command `ownerChannel.folds` (the wire; a new command, in the same minor) | never sends it; as a daemon, answers `unknown_command` | this build's window on an earlier daemon reads no stored summary: its lines read their newest call or `working record` | nothing kept |
| `summaries` on the to-human row of `mods.list` (the wire; an optional key, in the same minor) and the command `mods.setFoldSummaries` (the same minor) | its main drops the key with the tolerant reader and its mods list draws no switch; as a daemon it sends no key and answers `unknown_command` | absent: the switch is not drawn, since that daemon cannot keep it | nothing kept |
| `index_meta`, the key `toHuman.foldSummaries` with the value `off` (the index; absent reads as on; no column is added and `user_version` is unmoved) | F's own schema has no such table and F reads none of it; the installed build reads that table's keys by name and never this one; neither writes it. Their launches advertise 8 and carry no summaries value, and at 8 every release of the mod makes no summary: while an earlier build runs, the switch is not asked and nothing is made | absent: on | the key is as this build left it |
| `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off` in a launch's settings env (the engine's process, and the launch's own record of its arguments, as the advertisement is) | a session launched with it keeps it until it ends; a release of the mod before the mod's pull request ignores it, and makes no summary under MAWS anyway | its launches carry none: where the changed mod draws at 9, it summarises | this build's next launch decides again |
| the mod's file `to-human/folds/<session id>.json` in the engine's configuration directory (the mod's storage, not MAWS's) | never reads it | reads it at the contract's shape, 2; never writes, moves or removes it; any other `shape`, the shape 1 of releases before the mod's pull request among them, reads as no summaries | untouched by every build of MAWS |
| either settings file, any layout key | untouched | | |

`ui.view`, the open stretches and the revealed row are the tab's, in memory: nothing of the view is stored. The stored runs a window has read are in memory too, per session, and are read again at a mount.

**Two cross-build runs are owed by the second pull request,** one with each earlier build as the exact peer (`--peer-exact f1852de3a`, `--peer-exact 2c7044231`), over §H's four scenes (its two unit-mawsd scenes on macOS only). The row added to the coverage table (begun by U13a's M5, and kept since in the comment of `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts`):

| stored | the peer reads this build's value, and writes on | this build reads the peer's value |
|---|---|---|
| `owner_channel_sessions.advertised` | *(to-human, to the peer)*: the peer opens the index, lists the session, launches it and records its launch | *(to-human, from the peer)*; the return of *(to-human, to the peer)*: the reservation no longer counts |
| `index_meta`, `toHuman.foldSummaries` | *(to-human, to the peer)*: this build turns the switch off before it quits; the peer opens the index, lists the session and launches it | the return of *(to-human, to the peer)*: the mods list's row still reads `summaries: false`, and this build's next launch carries the value |

### §G. The matrix: no mod, a mod without the declaration, a mod with it; at nothing, at 8 and at 9

One table an executor turns into cases (`src/shared/owner/draws.test.ts`, `src/services/owner/launch.test.ts`, `transcript/surface/mod-route.test.tsx`). "Older" is an installation without the declaration, 0.8.2 and every release before the mod's pull request among them; "newer" is one with it. A version plays no part: a higher version without the declaration is older.

| the lane | the mod, as the launch finds it | the launch advertises | the mod's view | its message tool | `drawnBy` | what the session shows |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| off, or a style that carries no tool | no applicable installation, or its hooks do not load | nothing | — | — | `maws` | MAWS's transcript as today; a recorded message reads `● message` |
| off, or a style that carries no tool | an applicable installation whose hooks load, older or newer | nothing | draws, as in a terminal, once the session holds a message | not registered (a headless launch with no advertisement) | `mod` | the mod's view; MAWS's turn fold stands aside; no mirror, no switch |
| on | older | 8 | steps aside | registers | `maws` | the mirror |
| on | newer | 9 | draws | registers | `mod` | the mod's view |
| on | two installations apply, one older | 8 | steps aside | registers | `maws` | the mirror |

Under a launch that has advertised, after a reload:

| the launch advertised | the installation now loaded | the mod's view | `drawnBy` | the view |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | any, or none | steps aside | `maws` | the mirror |
| 9 | newer | draws | `mod` | the mod's |
| 9 | older | steps aside (any non-empty value) | `maws` | the mirror |
| 9 | none | — | `maws` | the mirror |
| nothing | older or newer | draws | `mod` | the mod's |
| nothing | none | — | `maws` | MAWS's transcript |

Not a session MAWS runs: a terminal session, a session on another machine and a session at rest are the mirror in every row where the lane is on.

**The summary in each row** (§M). Where the session shows the mirror, a record line reads what the mod's file holds for it (a run summarised in a terminal, or under an earlier launch at 9), or its newest call, or `working record`; no row of these tables makes MAWS ask a model. Where it shows the mod's view at 9, the mod's line reads a summary when the launch carried no `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off` (an installation at 9 carries the declaration, which ships with §I's items 9 and 10). Where the mod draws with no advertisement, its line reads its newest call and then `working record`: a headless session with no advertisement asks for no summary (decision 25).

### §H. The cutover

| | the situation | what is drawn | proved by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | a session running across the update: launched by an earlier build at 8, its engine still running, this build's window and daemon on it | the mirror. The row holds no reservation and its `launch_id` is the running launch's, so the advertisement is read from `desired`: 8, `maws` | *(to-human, from the peer)*, with each peer |
| 2 | a session resumed from a transcript an earlier build's session wrote | at rest, the mirror, from the file's rows (the file is the engine's and does not change with MAWS's build; the older marks and both message tools read as messages). Its resume is this build's launch and is decided by §A | `lane-a/view-routes.test.ts` over a recorded file; `owner/view/mirror.test.ts`'s cases for the older marks and `mcp__maws__message` |
| 3 | this build's window on an earlier build's daemon | the mirror: `drawnBy` is absent, and that daemon's lane-on launches advertise 8 | *(to-human, on the peer’s daemon)*, a unit-mawsd scene |
| 4 | an earlier build's window on this build's daemon, a session launched at 8 | that build's own report, once: the key is dropped, the mod steps aside | *(to-human, the peer on this daemon)*, a unit-mawsd scene: nothing refused, the key dropped (the line the peer's tolerant reader writes to its main output), the peer's bar and record row drawn |
| 5 | the same, a session this build launched at 9 with a declaring installation (or: going back while such a session runs) | both drawings, until that session's next launch, which the earlier build makes at 8 (point 6, card 2) | cannot be staged with a replayed engine: one look by the architect on the trial's Mac |

The four scenes of `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts` (the second pull request; two at authoring, four at the architect's I4 ruling of 2026-11-16 (UTC), Decision Log), each skipped only by the group's own conditions (a skipped scene is not evidence): *(to-human, from the peer)*, in which the peer keeps a lane-on session whose recorded transcript holds a message and work, this build attaches, and the transcript shows the record line and the switch, no bar, and no refusal in either log; *(to-human, to the peer)*, in which this build launches such a session with a scratch config home whose style and installation are written by `test/support/to-human-plugin.ts`, the installation carrying the declaration (so the launch's spawn row carries 9 and the index holds its bound reservation), quits, the peer opens the profile, draws its bar and its record row, launches the session again and keeps that launch running on the stub engine; this build reads `drawnBy` `maws` while that launch runs (the reservation bound to the earlier launch, under a live launch at 8), stops it, resumes, and asserts the reservation bound to the new launch. *(to-human, on the peer’s daemon)* (row 3): this build's window on the peer's unit mawsd reads a lane state with no `drawnBy`, a launch advertising 8, and draws the mirror, nothing refused. *(to-human, the peer on this daemon)* (row 4): the peer's window on this build's unit mawsd, a session launched at 8, drops `drawnBy` and draws its own report once, nothing refused. The two unit-mawsd scenes run where a unit daemon can, under the group's `UNIT_SKIP` (macOS; CI refuses), as "(update, render)" does; no peer skips them on its own source.

### §I. The mod's pull request (doperpowers; read at the installed 0.8.2)

**Built** on the branch `to-human-maws-surface` of the doperpowers repository, final head `e511aad9`, to-human 0.9.0; not opened. The items below are the design as it was handed over; where the branch departs from them, "As built", after item 14, says how, and each departure is accepted by the architect.

By ruling R1 it holds more than the one gate. Exactly this, and nothing else.

1. **The gate.** (The code below is built otherwise: see As built.) `to-human/hooks/mods/to-human.tsx`, `native()` (lines 1450 to 1455). Today it is true when the value is non-empty and the surface is not the terminal. After, it is true when the value is non-empty **and is not a number of 9 or more**, and the surface is not the terminal:

       const MAWS_DRAWS_FROM = 9
       let maws: Promise<boolean> | undefined
       const native = async ($: EngineInterface, e: object) =>
         (await (maws ??= $.env.get('MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN').then(
           (value) => value !== undefined && value !== '' && !(Number(value) >= MAWS_DRAWS_FROM),
         ))) &&
         (!('surface' in e) || e.surface !== 'terminal')

   So unset or empty draws, as today; a number below 9 steps aside; 9 or more draws; anything that is no number steps aside. Its eleven callers are unchanged in place: `command.run` for `/to-human-dump` (1616); `ui.render` for `UserMessage` (1643), `TurnDuration` (1675), `AssistantMessage` (1701), `ToolUse` (1782), `ToolGroup` (1840), `ToolProgress` (1888), `ToolResult` (1921) and `AbovePrompt` (1942); `ui.press` for `to-human-toggle` (1982) and `to-human-mode` (1998).
2. **The declaration.** A file at the plugin's root, `to-human/maws-surface.json`, read by MAWS without running the plugin:

       { "toHumanView": { "drawsFrom": 9 } }

   It says: this installation's view draws on a client's surface when the client advertises this number or more. It ships in the same commit as the gate and only with an implementation check 1 found able to hide a run. A test of the mod reads the file and the gate's constant and fails when they differ (built otherwise: see As built).
3. **Nothing in MAWS reads the version.** It moves as the repository's own rule says (`to-human/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and the to-human entry of `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`: the next after the main branch's).
4. **A place for an ask with no record.** Where the view finds a row's place (`slotOf`, `see`): a request id of the form `<anchor>~<rest>` is placed directly after the row `<anchor>`, before whatever followed it. Nothing else about such a row changes: a `UserMessage` under such an id is classed by `promptRow` as any other.
5. **MAWS's turn line.** In the `TurnDuration` hook, for a request id that ends `~turn`: when the view is not folding, `next(e)`; when it folds, `next(e)` while a run that begins at or after its place and before the next row of kind `user` is unfolded (the run it stands in included), else an empty `Box`. An ask under the transcript's own row id keeps today's rule.
6. **A thought needs no change,** and gets a test: the `AssistantMessage` hook reads a summary block (`isSummary: true`) as a text with no message, a `record` row, so it folds and unfolds with its run and is kept in the full transcript. MAWS asks it under the thought row's own uuid, bare (§E; check 1's C2).
7. **`to-human/CLAUDE.md`:** the clause that says the view reads "any non-empty value" is rewritten (the notifier and the stream hook read any non-empty value; the view steps aside for a non-empty value that is not a number of 9 or more and draws from 9); one sentence states that rule where the file describes the view; one names the declaration and what a client reads from it.
8. **Tests.** `to-human/tests/mods/to-human-native.test.ts` keeps its case (every gated hook passes through off the terminal at `'1'`, the terminal still draws, one read of the setting, the inventory of ungated hooks) and gains: at `'8'` and at `'native'`, the same pass-through on `desktop`, `vscode`, `mobile` and no surface; at `'9'` and `'10'`, on each surface that is not the terminal, a marked `AssistantMessage` draws the report and not `next`'s result, a record row folds, `AbovePrompt` draws the band after a mark, and the two presses act. New cases for items 2, 4, 5 and 6: an ask under `<anchor>~x` folds with the run its anchor is in or begins; a `~turn` ask is hidden in a folded report, shown with a record of its turn open, shown in the full transcript; each of them asked alone, with no other row of its run asked. `to-human/tests/mods/live-maws-check.py` gains a second run at `MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN=9`, with the same five expectations.
9. **The tracker asks where the mod draws MAWS's view.** `to-human/hooks/mods/folds.tsx`, `readSettings` (579) and `isAsking` (566). Today a run's summary is asked for only in an interactive session whose `MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN` is unset or empty. After: `isQuiet` is true when `SMNS_INTERNAL` is set, when the advertisement is one the view steps aside for (non-empty and not a number of 9 or more: item 1's predicate, one exported function that both read), or when item 10's value is `off`; and a session counts as read when it is interactive **or its advertisement is a number of 9 or more** (a client that advertises 9 reads the view's drawing). So:

   | the session | `MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN` | a due run's summary is asked for |
   |---|---|---|
   | interactive | unset or empty | yes, as today |
   | interactive or headless | a number of 9 or more | yes |
   | interactive or headless | non-empty, and below 9 or no number | no, as today |
   | headless | unset or empty | no, as today: nothing says a client reads it |
   | any, with `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off`, or with `SMNS_INTERNAL` set | any | no |

   Nothing else of the tracker changes: a mark seen or kept first, one attempt per run, the digest, `LABEL_SYSTEM`, the model and the effort, the file and its 30 days.
10. **A value that turns summaries off.** `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES`, read once with the others in `readSettings` through `$.env.get`: exactly `off` makes `isQuiet` true; unset, empty or anything else leaves summaries on. A person sets it for terminal sessions in the engine's own settings env; MAWS writes it into the settings env of the launches it decides while its switch is off (§M). The value stops the asking and nothing else: summaries already made are still read. Card 11 is answered "Only new folds": `foldLabel` (`to-human.tsx`, 1164) is not changed by this item.
11. **A label that changes reaches a client's surface.** Dropped for good. Check 1's E2 found that the engine pushes one invalidation, naming the instance, for each write of `fold` or `folds` that a drawn line had read; nothing is added to `publish`.
12. **The file is a contract a client reads.** In these words, which §M repeats: **the mod writes the file by atomic replacement (a temporary file in the same directory, then a rename over the file), and before it writes it reads the file again and keeps every stored run its own tracker does not hold (its own runs win where both hold one, by the run's first id).** In `folds.tsx` that is `keepFile` (661) and `stored` (442):
    - *The replacement.* `$.fs` has no rename, as it has no delete (`prune` already runs `find` through `$.process.run`); the temporary is written with `$.fs.write` and moved over the file with one `mv -f` in the same directory. The temporary's name is the file's with a suffix the 30-day removal also takes, so a leftover of a write that died goes with the rest. No reader ever finds a partial `<session id>.json`.
    - *The merge.* Before each write the file is read again, and every run in it whose first id the tracker's own runs do not hold is kept, in its place among them by the order it had. So two processes on one session id (two terminals resuming one conversation) no longer erase each other's summaries. Two writes that cross between the read and the rename can still leave out the earlier writer's newest run; that writer's next write puts it back, since its tracker still holds it.
    - *The shape.* A file kept this way carries `shape: 2`; the runs are laid out as before. `storedRuns` (450) reads shape 1 and shape 2, so a session's older file is taken up and written back as 2 at the next summary. A client reads shape 2 only (§M): what a release before this one wrote, whole and in place from one process's memory, is not a coherent snapshot for anyone but the process that wrote it.
    - *Said and typed.* `types/to-human.d.ts` gains the stored shape (`StoredFolds`: `{ shape: 2, runs: [first, last, summary][] }`, the ids as `FoldRun` gives them) with the contract's sentence. `to-human/CLAUDE.md`'s paragraph on `folds.tsx` states the contract, says where summaries are asked for after item 9, names item 10's value, and names the file as one MAWS reads.
    - *Tests* (`to-human/tests/mods/folds.test.ts`): **two trackers on one session's file, writing in each order,** each holding a run the other does not, and both holding one run under the same first id: after either order the file holds both foreign runs and, for the shared one, the last writer's own; a reader that reads between the temporary's write and the rename gets the earlier file whole; a shape 1 file taken up and written back as 2; item 9's table row by row over the existing gates' cases; `off` and its near misses (`OFF`, `0`, empty) for item 10; `stored()`'s output read back through the declared type. `to-human/tests/mods/live-maws-check.py`'s run at 9 gains: a run that closes at a message reads a summary within the call's limit, the session's folds file holds it at shape 2, and with `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off` in the settings env none is asked for.
13. **One rule from a line's row to its run, and its table of cases.** `labelOf` (`folds.tsx`, 513) gains the stretch's last row and §M's clause 4: when no run holds the place of the row that draws the line, the row takes the run that begins after it and at or before the place of the last row of its stretch (`runEnd`, `to-human.tsx`), the nearest such. So a stretch that a thought opens, which MAWS's surface asks about at 9 and the tracker never follows, reads its summary and not `working record`. Clauses 1 to 3 stay as they are: the place of the row that draws the line, both bounds of a closed run required, the newest run first. The rule is written once, in the MAWS document's §M ("Which run a line reads"), and `to-human/CLAUDE.md` points at the cases, not at a second wording. **The cases are one file,** `to-human/tests/mods/fold-label-cases.json`, in the shape §M gives, holding at least the cases M7 lists; `folds.test.ts` runs every case through `labelOf` and `labelFor` and pins the file's digest (built otherwise: see As built), and MAWS's test reads a byte-for-byte copy under the same digest. A change of the rule is a change of that file first.

14. **A lone call that joins a group** (added after check 1's first round, by the architect's ruling that the defect is the mod's to fix). `joinGroup`, called in the `ToolGroup` hook: each call the group holds that stands in the view's order as a record row of its own, because a client asked it alone as `ToolUse` before its run had a second call, becomes one row with the group. The group takes that row's slot with what it carried (its run's unfolding, its place in the window, the end its label was read to, the controls drawn under it), and the hook invalidates once when a row joined. A call the group later lets go is its own row again. A call the person reads (a message, a spawn, a question) keeps its own row. A terminal's group, drawn from its first call, draws as it did.

**As built** (the builder's report in the branch's worktree, both rounds; 17 files, +1,371 −112 against 0.8.2).

- *Items 2 and 13 are checked by scripts, not by kit tests:* `to-human/tests/mods/maws-surface.ts` and `to-human/tests/mods/fold-label-cases.ts`, run by bun from `to-human/tests/mods/run.sh` after the kit. The plugin test kit loads only code files and refuses a data file outright: "cannot import "./data.json" (from tests/p.test.ts): … is not named like code and was not loaded: a hooks module and the files it imports end in .ts, .tsx, .jsx, .js, .mjs, .cjs, .mts or .cts". `folds.test.ts` keeps the unit cases of clause 4.
- *The case table* is `to-human/tests/mods/fold-label-cases.json`, seventeen cases, sha256 `c65988f084c0978b3f79eab220ae02f3ef7175f2717ea225ff421557f9c0fa94`, pinned in `fold-label-cases.ts`. MAWS's copy is pinned to the same digest (M7).
- *The backslash keeps the mod's reading* (the mod's decision): a mark after a backslash is a mark in the mod and literal text in MAWS, and §E's known difference stands.
- *Item 1's shape.* `native` is a function at the top of the file that takes a setting kept for each registration, and the eleven callers pass it; the plugin loader refuses `$` handed to a closure. Its behaviour is the item's: one read of the value for a registration.
- *Item 4's place.* An anchored ask stands at its anchor's place plus a small fraction (1/2000, half the step between a live row's calls), so before anything that followed the anchor, in the file and live alike. It counts as kept exactly when its anchor is placed.
- *Item 5's boundary, and its contract.* "Before the next row of kind `user`" sees only prompts that have drawn, so a turn's line whose next prompt was never asked also shows while an unfolded run of the next turn is open. Known, a tracker row; check 1 could not provoke it. The hook's comment states the contract of §E: the line is anchored at the row that opens its turn and stands for that turn.
- *Item 12's file.* The temporary is `<session id>.json.<token>.tmp`, the token made once for a registration so that two processes do not share one; the 30-day removal takes those names too; a move that fails is logged and leaves the file as it was; a file of a shape the mod does not know is taken as holding no runs and is written over.
- *Item 13's refresh.* A folded line whose label came from clause 4 is asked again when a row drawn later moves its stretch's end (`View.endOf`).
- *The live check* (`live-maws-check.py`) is edited to three launches, at 8, at 9, and at 9 with `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off`, and was not run: check 1 stands in for it.
- *Beside the items:* the mod's own design record for summaries is amended in place where four of its statements became false, and its catalogue names the value that turns summaries off.

**What it must not change: a terminal session.** Check 1's second round drew one interactive terminal session with each installation: the record line read the newest call and then the summary in both, and nothing in the view differed between them (`u2b-mod-terminal-before.json`, `u2b-mod-terminal-after.json`).

Changed beyond the view, and said in the body: `folds.tsx`, by items 9 to 11 alone. At 9 its live line shows in MAWS as it does in a terminal, whatever item 10's value, since the tracker publishes it in every session (reported point 18). Untouched, and said in the body: `message.tsx` (`getsTool`, the tool from 8 in a headless session), `notify.tsx`, `reminder.tsx`, `edits.tsx` (the terminal only, by its own test), the doperpowers plugin's `hooks/to-human-stream.sh`, the sminos ask mod, and `hooks/hooks.json`, whose description of the tool ("not under MAWS") has been stale since 8.

Before it opens: `to-human/tests/mods/run.sh` passes, and check 1 has recorded the answers. Both hold at `e511aad9` (the kit's 346 cases and the three scripts; "Checks handed to the architect", "The answers"). After it merges, an earlier MAWS build is unchanged (both advertise 8), and so is a MAWS at the first pull request.

### §J. Consumed from siblings, by name

### §L. The desk web app

Its session view mounts the renderer's `Transcript` with the lane on and counts rows through `drawnRows` (`apps/desk-web/src/depth/view/TranscriptPane.tsx`). So from its next deploy on, whoever makes it, it draws the mirror's rows and the message's new look. **Re-pointed:** the two imports of `ReportToggle.tsx`, from `owner/report/copy` and `owner/report/report.css` to `owner/view/copy` and `owner/view/view.css`, which keep the bar's three words and its classes (`owner-bar`, `owner-view-toggle`) for it. **Unchanged:** every other file under `apps/desk-web/src/`; its own bar, which still switches `ui.view`; its own "Working record · …" label in the conversation view; everything under its deploy. Nothing is deployed by this landing.

**The summary.** The desk hands `Transcript` no labels, so its record lines read `› working record` as they do after the first pull request, with no live line (decision 28). None of its scenes changes for the second pull request, and M7 edits nothing under `apps/desk-web/`.

Its e2e scenes whose expectations change (group `e2e:desk-web`), all for the old record row or the label, none for the bar:
