### §M. The record line's summary (M4: the reader, the command, the switch; M7: the mirror and the control; P: the mod)

**The law, extended.** Beside "one side draws":

- **One maker.** For any stretch, one program at most asks a model for its summary: the mod's tracker, in the engine process that runs the session. MAWS asks no model. One maker is not always one call: by the mod's own rule a run summarised at a turn's end is summarised again when it closes after it grew, and the newer line replaces the older under the same first row. A process that resumes a session never asks again for a run the file already holds (`takeUp`: such a run is not held whole). MAWS never makes a second maker: it starts no process on a session a live process runs (decision 33).
- **One text, for as long as the mod keeps its file.** A stretch that has settled reads one text: the same where the mod draws it and where MAWS draws it, before and after a reload, and in two windows. In motion a line moves forward: its newest call while it grows, then `working record` or its summary; a summary gives way to a newer summary of the same stretch. It goes back to `working record` in one case, the mod's own expiry: the mod removes a session's file 30 days after its last write (`prune`, 630, run when any new process loads), and the line of a session untouched that long reads `working record` again, in every window alike.
- **Nothing unknown is a summary.** A file that cannot be read, a `shape` MAWS does not know, a run whose bounds MAWS cannot place: each reads `working record`. A read that fails changes nothing a window shows (decision 29).

**Who makes it, where it is kept, what the line reads.**

| the session | who makes the summary | where it is kept | who puts it on the line | with none, the line reads |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MAWS runs it and the mod draws at 9 (`drawnBy` is `mod`, the surface attached in this window) | the mod's tracker in that engine process, from the mod's pull request on (§I, item 9), unless the launch carried `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off` | the process's `$.state`, and the session's folds file | the mod, in its own answer for the stretch's first row | its newest call while it grows, then `working record` (the mod's own fallback) |
| MAWS runs it and the mod draws with no advertisement (the lane off, the plugin loaded) | nobody: a headless session with no advertisement asks for none (decision 25) | — | the mod | the same |
| MAWS runs it and MAWS draws (the advertisement 8; or a window with no surface attached) | nobody at 8. What an earlier launch at 9, or a terminal, summarised is in the file | the session's folds file | the mirror, from `ownerChannel.folds` | its newest call while it grows, then `working record` |
| a terminal session seen in MAWS, on this machine | the mod in the terminal's own process, by its own rule and its own setting | the same file, written by that process | the mirror, from `ownerChannel.folds`, read again as the session moves. A file a release before the mod's pull request wrote (shape 1) is not read | the same |
| a session at rest, read from its file, on this machine | nobody; what was made while it ran | the same file | the mirror, read at the mount | `working record` |
| a session on another machine | that machine's process, as above | that machine's file, which this landing does not read: `ownerChannel.folds` is refused there with the owner channel's sentence | — | its newest call while it grows, then `working record` (decided for this landing) |
| the desk web app | — | — | — | `working record`: it hands the mirror no labels (§L; decided for this landing) |

MAWS reads the mod's file because it is where the mod keeps what it made, the only copy that outlives the process, and what the mod itself reads back at a resume: reading it gives the session at rest the words the running session showed. Nothing of a summary is in the transcript file ("Today").

**The file: a contract, and its reader** (`src/shared/owner/folds.ts`, pure; `src/services/owner/folds.ts`, the one place that opens the file; M4). The contract is §I's item 12, in these words: *the mod writes the file by atomic replacement (a temporary file in the same directory, then a rename over the file), and before it writes it reads the file again and keeps every stored run its own tracker does not hold (its own runs win where both hold one, by the run's first id).* So a read that succeeds is one whole file some process wrote, and two processes on one session id do not erase each other's summaries. A file kept under the contract says so by its `shape`, 2; the runs inside are laid out as in shape 1. MAWS reads shape 2 and nothing else. **This is the one place that is decided:** the releases of the mod before its pull request write shape 1, whole and in place, each process from its own memory, and their files read as no summaries.

    export const FOLDS_SHAPE = 2;                                                                 // the contract's shape (§I item 12)
    export const FOLD_SUMMARIES_ENV = 'TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES';                                  // §I item 10; the one value MAWS writes is 'off'
    export interface StoredRun { first: string; last: string; summary: string }
    export function storedRunsOf(text: string): StoredRun[] | null;                              // null: not a file of the contract's shape
    export function foldsFileOf(configHome: string, engineSessionId: string): string | null;    // null: an id that is no plain file name

`storedRunsOf` follows the mod's `storedRuns` (`folds.tsx`, 450) at the contract's shape: the text parses as JSON to an object whose `shape` is 2 and whose `runs` is an array; an entry that is not three strings is skipped. Beyond that, because the file is another program's: an id is kept only at 1 to 256 characters; a summary is trimmed, read to its first line, cut at 120 characters between code points with `…` (the mod's `oneLine`, 140, which the mod already applied) and skipped when empty; of more than 5,000 runs the last 5,000 are kept. `foldsFileOf` answers null for an id that holds anything but letters, digits, `-` and `_`, or is longer than 128 characters. The service reads the file of the session's engine session id, as its own index record gives it, under the configuration directory the owner service already resolves (`register.ts`), in one read of the whole file; a file past 4 MiB answers null, and no file answers an empty list. It opens that one name and no temporary beside it.

    'ownerChannel.folds': { input: { appSessionId }, output: { runs: StoredRun[] | null } }       // owned by `machine`, graded `view`

`runs: null` says that a file is there and is not a file of the contract's shape, or could not be read: the caller keeps what it had. A session with no engine session id yet answers an empty list.

**Which run a line reads: one rule, the mod's and MAWS's alike.** It is written here once. The mod implements it in `labelOf` (§I, item 13) and MAWS in `runOf`; neither adds to it. A *place* is a row's position in the conversation: the mod's `View.place`, and for MAWS the row's index among the rows it holds. A row neither side knows has no place. Ids are compared as the mod's `rowKey` compares them (`message.tsx`, 194): an id shaped as a row's uuid by its first 24 characters, any other whole. The inputs are the row that draws the line (the stretch's first row: its head, where a mixed message heads it), the stretch's last row, the closed runs, and the open run where one is published.

1. `at` is the place of the row that draws the line. With no place, no run.
2. The open run, where one is published, if its `first` has a place and `at` is at or after it.
3. Else, of the closed runs whose `first` and `last` both have a place, the newest with `first <= at <= last`.
4. Else, of the runs whose `first` has a place after `at` and at or before the place of the stretch's last row (the open run, and the closed runs with both bounds placed), the one that begins nearest. This is the one change, made on both sides: a row that opens a stretch before the run's first tracked step (a thought: the tracker follows no thinking) takes the run that begins inside that stretch, so a stretch's line reads its summary whichever row opens it.
5. Else no run: the line reads `working record`.

Clauses 1 to 3 are `labelOf` as it stands (`folds.tsx`, 513 to 524); clause 4 is new in both programs. The open run is published in `$.state`, which only the mod reads, so for MAWS clause 2 never applies: the file holds an open run's summary as a run bounded by the last row that summary covered (`stored`, 442), and clause 3 or 4 finds it. There is no fallback on one bound: while a run's `first` is in a page MAWS has not loaded the line reads `working record`, and its summary when the page arrives. One run can hold two stretches where MAWS keeps in sight a row the mod's run passes over; both then read its summary, as clause 3 gives it.

**That the two sides give equal text is proved over one table of cases:** `to-human/tests/mods/fold-label-cases.json` in the mod's repository, written with §I's item 13, and its byte-for-byte copy `src/renderer/src/owner/view/fold-label-cases.json` here. Each case gives the rows in order (each with its id, its class as the view sorts it, whether it has a place, and for a call its line and whether its result has come), the closed runs, the open run if any (its first row, its summary if it has one, the last row that summary covered), whether the turn is running, and for each stretch's first row the label expected, or none. Both repositories' tests read their copy and pin the same digest of it. M7's own tests over invented files do not carry acceptance A15's words; this table does, with the trial.

**The label's rule** (`src/renderer/src/owner/view/label.ts`, pure; M7).

    export interface StretchLabels { stored: readonly StoredRun[]; running: boolean }            // running: the session's turn is running
    export function keptIdsOf(row: Row, turn: TurnState | undefined): string[];
    export function runOf(stretch: Stretch, rows: readonly Row[], runs: readonly StoredRun[]): StoredRun | undefined;
    export function liveLine(stretch: Stretch, covered: StoredRun | undefined, last: boolean, running: boolean): string | undefined;
    export function recordLabel(stretch: Stretch, rows: readonly Row[], labels: StretchLabels, last: boolean): string | undefined;   // undefined: `working record`
    export function labelsOf(rows: readonly Row[], labels: StretchLabels | undefined): ((stretch: Stretch, last: boolean) => string | undefined) | undefined;

- `keptIdsOf`: the ids the transcript keeps a row under, which are the ids the row asks its site under (§E): a call's tool-use id (for a run, each member's; for a merged read, each member read's), a text's and a thought's message uuid, an arrival's carrier's. "Where the ids and the inputs come from", below, says how.
- `runOf` is the one rule above, clauses 1 and 3 to 5, over the stored runs in the file's order (oldest first).
- `liveLine` ports the live half of `labelFor` (502) and `lineOf` (188, with `lineFor`, 148, and `oneLine`, 140), tool by tool and cut for cut. To MAWS a stretch is live while one of its calls has no result, or while it is the rows' last stretch and the session's turn is running (the reading by which decision 11 draws the running turn's line). Its line is that of its newest call after the row the stored run's `last` names, or of its newest call when no run is stored; with no such call it has none, and the stored summary reads on (the mod's `run.line ?? run.summary`).
- `recordLabel`: the live line, if the stretch is live and has one; else the stored run's summary; else nothing. **Card 11's answer is built here and nowhere else** ("Only new folds"): a stored summary is read whatever the switch says.
- The mod holds a run live for the seconds between its close and its summary's arrival, and reads its last call meanwhile. MAWS cannot see that state, and reads `working record` for those seconds: a known difference (§E).

**Where the ids and the inputs come from** (reported point 25, as ruled; the rule above is unchanged).

- *One derivation of "the id a row asks its site under".* `keptIdsOf` does not work the id out a second time: for each kind it reads what the row's own ask reads. For an arrival (`notice`, `agent_message`) that is the carrier the ask finds in the turn's events (`messageCarrier` and `noticeCarrier` in `transcript/surface/adapters.ts`, exported for it, not copied), which is why the function takes the row's turn. A test pins, for every row kind, that the id `keptIdsOf` gives is the id the row asks under.
- *A merged read keeps every member's id.* `mergeReads` (`transcript/model/cells.ts`) adds each member read's tool-use id to a list on the merged cell. The list is additive: the cell's own id and `toolUseId`, and every reader of the cell today, are unchanged.
- *A thought needs nothing added.* A stored run's bounds name a note (a text), a call or a delivery, never a thought: the mod's tracker sets a run's `first` and `last` only from the steps it follows (`follow`, `newRun(step.id)` and `run.last = step.id`, lines 406 and 407 of `folds.tsx` at the mod's `e511aad9`), and thinking is among the rows that make no step (`stepsOf`, the comment at 110). So a thought taken into a run keeps no uuid, and a thought before a run is placed by its row's index alone (clause 4).
- *A call's raw input, for `liveLine`,* is a lookup by tool-use id over the turn's records, handed in with the labels where the mirror is called. Cells gain no input.

`mirrorRows` takes a last, optional argument, `label?: (stretch: Stretch, last: boolean) => string | undefined`, and writes its answer on the closed `record_fold` row as `label?: string`. `RecordFold` draws `› ` and the label, or `› working record` without one; the opened line is unchanged.

**The reads** (`src/renderer/src/stores/folds.ts`; M7). `useStoredFolds(appSessionId, enabled)` answers what this window last read of the session's file. `enabled` is: the session is this machine's, the mirror is in force for the tab (`mirrorsView`), and the daemon has not answered `unknown_command` or a refusal for the key.

- *A round* is four reads: 3, 8, 20 and 45 seconds after it starts. The mod's own call is limited to 30 seconds, so the read at 45 is past any summary that was being made when the round started. (The summaries check 1 recorded settled about a second and a half after their stretch closed: the first read of a round will usually hold them.)
- *A round starts* at each mount of a view of the session (with one read at once: the catch-up for a summary made, or outstanding, before the window looked), at each close of a stretch and at each end of a turn, the two moments at which the mod asks. A remount starts a round again. The window reads once when it regains focus.
- *No read of a round is cancelled by what an earlier read returned.* A stored summary is no evidence that the mod's current attempt has finished: a note or a late result changes a run with no new last row. Rounds overlap freely; reads that fall due while one is in flight are sent as one read when it answers, and none is dropped. A round ends early only with the key's last mount.
- *What a read answers replaces what the window held, whole,* when it succeeds; `runs: null` and a failed command change nothing. Answers are taken in the order the reads were sent (a counter per key), and an answer for a key with no mount left is dropped.
- *What is left, said plainly.* A summary written after a round's last read (a call slower than the schedule allows for, a write delayed past it) is not shown until the next mount, return of focus, close of a stretch or end of a turn. No file watcher and no change subscription is built in this landing; a tracker row names them as the stronger form.

**The switch** (M4: where it is kept and how it is carried; M7: its control). One fact of this machine, kept by the daemon: the key `toHuman.foldSummaries` of the index's own facts (`index_meta`), read and written by `foldSummaries(): boolean` and `setFoldSummaries(on: boolean)` in `src/services/owner/store.ts`. It holds `off` when off and is absent otherwise, so summaries are on by default. **This is the one place the switch's home is decided** (card 10, answered "A switch in MAWS"; reported point 19, accepted). `mods.list` answers it on the to-human row as `summaries: z.boolean().optional()`, which this build always sends; `mods.setFoldSummaries` (input `{ on: boolean }`; owned by `machine`) writes it. `ownerChannelLaunch` reads it once when it decides a launch, and `withOwnerChannel` writes `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off` into the settings env of every launch decided while it is off, whatever the lane, and otherwise removes a caller's value of that name, as it does the advertisement's. It reaches a session at its next launch; a running session keeps what it was launched with.

In the mods list alone (`BuiltInMods` in `widgets/plugins/BuiltInMods.tsx`), which is the picture the owner answered (frame 11), and only when the row carries `summaries`. `BuiltInModRow` takes the switch as something its caller passes, and only the mods list passes it: the same row under Customize's "My items", All (`customize/MineAll.tsx`) is unchanged (reported point 26, as ruled).

| what | text (in `widgets/copy.ts`, the mods list's own table; typographic marks, as that table's other copy has them) |
|---|---|
| the switch | `Summaries on fold lines` |
| its sentence | `Each summary is one small model call by the to-human mod, on the session’s own account. Off: sessions MAWS starts make no new summaries from their next start, and summaries already made stay. A session started in a terminal follows the mod’s own setting.` |

- *The clause `and summaries already made stay`* is card 11's answer, confirmed by the owner, and stands. The sentences of card 10's other options ("The drawn example", frame 11) are not built.
- *The words are the switch's label.* `Summaries on fold lines` is a visible label and the switch's accessible name: the words and the control stand in one `label` element, so a press on the words toggles the switch, and the control's name is the same words. The entry's own switch stays as it is built: it has no visible words, and `To-human in every session` is its accessible name alone. The two differ on purpose; the existing one is not changed here.
- *Where it stands.* A second line of the To-human row: the words, and the sentence under them, start at the row's middle column (where the name and the description start); the switch stands at the right, under the entry's own switch.
- *Its hit area* is its whole line: the label spans from the words to the control.
- *No pending line under it.* The row's existing pending line (`Applies the next time this session starts`) is `pendingOf` (`src/services/owner/mods.ts`), computed from the stored facts of the session's last decided launch (`desired`, `enabledAt`, `applied`, `offUnavailable` of `owner_channel_sessions`). They say what instructions that launch carried and nothing of its summaries value, and the host cannot say what a running launch was given ("Today"). To show the line while an open session's launch differs from the switch would take a new stored form (one more field of §A's reservation, or a column). By the architect's condition there is therefore none, and the sentence carries it: `from their next start`.

**Recorded** (check 1's E1 to E5, on the mod's final head). The file is as this section gives it: shape 2, one run for each summarised stretch, replaced in one step (a temporary beside it, moved over it), merged across two processes on one session id, its ids those MAWS reads, its name the session's engine session id before and after a resume. The column "where the mod draws" of the table below was recorded with MAWS's own ask pattern, a lone call and then its run (§E).

**What a person sees.**

| the moment | where the mod draws | where MAWS draws |
|---|---|---|
| the stretch is open and growing | `› ` and its newest call | the same |
| it has closed and its summary is being made | its last call, for the seconds the call takes | `› working record` |
| the summary has arrived | `› ` and the summary | the same words, at the round's next read: 3, 8, 20 or 45 seconds after the close |
| the summary was written after the round's last read | `› ` and the summary | `› working record` until the next mount, return of focus, close or end of a turn; then the summary |
| the call failed, timed out or was not made (the switch off; a release before the mod's pull request; a headless session with no advertisement) | `› working record` | `› working record` |
| the stretch grows again after a summary (a turn ended, and a later turn went on with no message between) | its new call, then the newer summary | the same |
| the stretch opens with a thought | its summary (clause 4) | the same |
| the run's first row is in a page not yet loaded | — | `› working record`, then the summary when the page arrives |
| the switch is turned off | nothing changes in a running session, which goes on making summaries until it is started again; from its next launch new stretches read `working record`, and stretches already summarised keep their summaries (card 11). No line of the mods list marks the difference meanwhile | the same |
| the session is reopened at rest, or shown in a second window | — | the stored summaries, in the same words |
| a stretch closes in a session launched with the switch off | `› working record` | `› working record`. Nothing on screen says that summaries are off for that session, so the line looks like a summary that was not made. Accepted (the architect's ruling on the drawing) |
| the session was not touched for 30 days and the mod removed its file | `› working record` | `› working record`, in every window |

## Checks handed to the architect

No engine runs on the MacBook. Each check is a step with what it runs, where, with what, what it records, and what each outcome changes.

### Check 1 · what the mod answers on MAWS's surface (Step 0; before the mod's pull request opens and before M5)

**Run on 2026-11-16 (UTC), in two rounds; Step 0 is done.** The table below is the check as it was asked; "The answers", under it, is what was recorded.

- **What it runs.** A probe scenario under `spikes/protocol-probe/scenarios/` built on the existing mod-surface scenarios (`mods-surface-transcript-common.mjs`, `mods-surface-sitting.mjs`, `owner-mod-source.mjs`): it launches the engine as MAWS does (headless, stream-json both ways, the settings env carrying `MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN` or not), attaches as MAWS's surface (`desktop`, the client id MAWS uses), and sends the engine's own `ui_render` with the props MAWS would send. Two parts. *Resumed, no model call:* over a seeded transcript of invented text that holds a prompt, a thought with text, a tool run of three calls, an unmarked text, a message-tool call, a text with a message and words beside it, a subagent's message and a task notification, an asynchronous question's call with its delivered answer, and a final marked text. *Live, a few model calls on the smallest model:* one turn per advertisement, to see the tool registered in `system/init` and a real message call's row answered. For questions E the live turn at 9 after is two stretches of two tool calls each, each closed by a message; each closed stretch then costs one more call, the mod's summary, on the model the mod names.
- **With which installations.** *Before:* a copy of the installed 0.8.2. *After:* P's checkout. Each installed into a scratch config home with the doperpowers plugin (for the style) and the sminos plugin (the mod's dependency), never the owner's.
- **Where.** A cloud VM or the Mac mini with engine `2.1.322` and model access; no Electron.
- **What it records.** Fixtures under `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/uxwave/`, named `u2b-mod-<none|8|9>-<before|after>.jsonl`, `u2b-mod-rows.jsonl` (questions C), `u2b-mod-reload.jsonl` and `u2b-mod-summary.jsonl` (questions E, with the folds file the run left, its text invented by the scenario's own prompts), each holding the asks and the answers' trees; a findings file `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-U2b-mod-answers.txt`; their rows in `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures.test.mjs`. The secrets scan (`node --test .github/scripts/maws-app-scan.test.mjs`) runs over them.

### Check 5 · rows the mod hides (A18; on the trial)

- **What it runs.** The trial scene's second case: a long seeded session (two hundred turns, a message in each; invented) resumed in a session MAWS runs with P's checkout installed, with no prompt sent, so that no model call is spent while the surface is attached. It scrolls from the end to the top and back and reads the count of mounted rows and of render asks at each stop.
- **Where and with what.** Electron on the Mac mini under the load rule, the trial's scratch config home.
- **What it records.** The two counts per stop, in this document's Surprises & Discoveries; no fixture.
- **Outcomes.** At or under the anchoring scene's bound of 40 mounted rows: nothing changes. Over it: nothing is built by an executor. The child's remedy (not mounting a hidden stretch's rows) would make a row's presence rest on a row that may not be mounted, which R3 forbids; the reading comes back to the architect with its numbers and one candidate, which needs the mod: the band's answer carrying the keys of the unfolded runs, read by one ask alive per transcript.
- **Added for the summary.** A third reading, live, in the trial's first turn: the count of render asks MAWS sends while one stretch of five tool calls runs with the mod drawing at 9, where each call writes the live line (reported point 18; check 1's E2). Check 1's E2 found one push for each write a drawn line had read, each naming one instance, and MAWS re-asks every mounted site at any of them. The count decides whether the mod's pull request must limit its invalidations (how often a changed live line reaches a client's surface): the architect reads it beside the two counts above, and a limit, if one is needed, is an item of §I added before the mod's pull request opens. Nothing changes in MAWS for it.

### Check 7 · U3's rows and the mod (O-30)

- **By reading, done here.** U3a's arrival rows ask `UserMessage` with the engine's props: `origin` (`peer` or `task-notification`), `from` for a message from a session or a subagent, `task` (`id`, `status`, `type`, `toolUseId`, `durationMs`) for a task notification (`transcript/surface/adapters.ts`, `arrivalInput` and `engineUserProps`); its sent-message row asks `ToolUse` (`sentInput`). Those are the fields `promptRow` reads. One gap, by the engine's own design: a scheduled trigger's and a wake's notice, and a task's end the host made, have no carrier the engine draws through `UserMessage`, so they ask nothing today; §E gives them the ask (R3).
- **Live.** Check 1's questions A2 and C3; the trial shows it in the window. No separate run.

## Cost on a long transcript

| what | where | can this landing move it |
|---|---|---|
| Derivation of a 500-turn session, and the median delta batch | `transcript/model/perf.test.ts` (the lane off) and `owner/view/perf.test.ts` (the lane on, mirrored), by hand, `MAWS_PERF=1`, one file at a time | yes, for a lane-on session only: with no turn fold every turn's rows are in the flat list, and the mirror is one pass over them per change of the rows. Concrete Steps 4 reads the base, the head, and the new lane-on case |
| No derivation of a turn during token batches | `assistant-ink.test.tsx`, `stores/transcript.test.ts`, `derive.test.ts` | it must not, except once: the flip at a session's first message derives every turn (decision 8) |
| Anchoring while 500 turns stream: at most 40 mounted rows | `e2e/transcript.spec.ts`, "anchoring", lane off | no: lane off, nothing of this landing is in its path but one flag read. Its measured half is read base against landing in one sitting on the Mac mini (the gate-offload plan's Decision Log 162) |
| The same bound in the mirrored view | the new scene of M3 | new: a folded stretch is one row where a folded turn was two or three, so it is expected under today's |
| Mounted rows and site asks where the mod draws | check 5 | the open question of A18; what an over-the-bound reading comes back with is named |
| Model calls for summaries | made by the mod, never by MAWS: one for each stretch that closes at a row the person reads, and one for the open stretch at a turn's end if rows joined it since its last; one attempt each | not by MAWS's code. The switch (§M) stops them in the sessions MAWS launches |
| What one call holds, from the mod's source | a system prompt of 963 characters; one user message, the stretch's digest, of at most 60,000 characters (a note cut at 1,500, a command at 600, a result to its first 600 and last 400); `maxTokens` 4,000 at effort `high`, sent by the engine as `max_tokens` 6048; the reply read to one line of at most 120 characters | no |
| What one call costs, measured (check 1's E6) | on the smallest model, for a stretch of two short calls: 386 to 388 tokens in (about 330 of them the system prompt and the wrapper, the rest the digest) and 23 to 40 tokens out, in about a second and a half, with no tools, no thinking and no prompt cache. A longer stretch adds its digest, up to the cap. Not measured: a long stretch, another model than the one a session's `haiku` resolved to in the probe, the price on a person's route, and whether a plan counts the calls against a limit | no |
| Where a person sees that cost | **nowhere in MAWS.** No `result` frame counts a summary call in its cost or its usage, and no request record of it is written to the transcript, so the turn's summary popover, the usage figures and every other cost MAWS shows leave these calls out; only the engine's debug log names each one. The owner has been told (the architect, 2026-11-16 (UTC)), and nothing is planned to display them: a tracker row | no |
| How many a long session makes | about one a stretch: the lane-on budget case's 500 turns with a message in each would be about 500 calls | no |
| The mirror's labels | `owner/view/label.ts`: one index of the rows' kept ids and one lookup for each stored run, at each change of the rows or of the stored runs | new, M7: the lane-on case is read again with 500 stored runs |
| Reads of the mod's file | five at each mount of a session's view (one at once, then the round), four after each close of a stretch and each end of a turn, one at each return of focus; none is cut short, and reads due together are sent as one. Each is one file of this machine, read whole by the daemon | new, M7; no measured bound. A session of 500 stretches watched from start to end makes about 2,000 reads over its whole length |

A lane-off session, and any session before its first message, is not moved at all.

## Proof that an executor does not run

Each is a step for the architect, with what it needs. A list per pull request (R7).

**The first pull request.** No two-build run is owed: it edits no two-build scene and stores and sends nothing.

1. **The Electron e2e groups the change crosses,** on a Mac under the load rule, each edited or new scene first alone, by its name in `package.json`: `e2e:transcript` (the rewritten report scene, the two new scenes, the R3 and M3 cases read again), `e2e:to-human` (the Needs-you jump), `e2e:tools` and `e2e:design-systems` (each pressed the bar), `e2e:library` (its conditional press), `e2e:composer` and `e2e:c7` (the switch is mounted in the composer), `e2e:lane-a` and `e2e:remote` (a terminal session's and another machine's pane now carry the view), `e2e:side` (it reads a row for raw message tags), `e2e:settings` (the keymap's table gains a row), and `e2e:desk-web` on the pool for §L's eight scenes.
2. **The measured anchoring scene,** `MAWS_TRANSCRIPT_PERF=1`, base and landing in one sitting on the Mac mini. About ten minutes of an idle Mac twice.
3. **The look on the built window,** light theme, against the drawn example: the message, the record line closed and open, the switch in its frame, the turn's line with no chevron, a running turn, a waiting permission, the switch above the holder notice; "Mark all read" from the palette.
4. **The owner's sitting.** Scenes: a session MAWS runs, a terminal session and a session on another machine, each drawn by the mirror; a relaunch in the middle of a session; the full transcript and back; a jump from Needs you into a folded record.

**The second pull request.**

1. **The Electron e2e groups:** `e2e:transcript` (the R3 `ToolGroup` case; the reveal), `e2e:to-human` (with the summary's scene), `e2e:widgets` (the mods list's version and the summaries' switch), `e2e:available` (it reads a launch's settings env, the summaries' value included). The default group collects `e2e/to-human-live.spec.ts`, which skips there.
2. **The trial** (A15, check 5, check 7's live half): `e2e/to-human-live.spec.ts` under its switch, on a Mac where Electron may run, with the harness build, an engine binary, a scratch config home holding the doperpowers, sminos and to-human plugins from P's checkout with the to-human style selected, and model access for three short turns under the spec's spend cap (tools then a final message; a message mid-turn then tools; a background task's notification and a peer's message). It must show: the messages, the record lines and the switch drawn by the mod and none by MAWS; nothing drawn twice; a thought, a listing, a notice and a turn's line hidden in the report and all in sight in the mod's full transcript, also after scrolling so that their neighbours unmount; a jump revealing its target; the same session with the lane switched off, drawn by the mod with no turn fold; the same session after a quit and a reopen at rest drawn by the mirror with the same rows, fold states aside; one artifact mention and one path link inside a mod-drawn message, with what each opens. A paid run: once, on the final head (UC-10). For the summary: the turns close at least two stretches. The trial must show each closed record line reading a summary where the mod draws; the same words on the same lines after the quit and the reopen at rest (the mirror, from the mod's file); a second window on the running session reading them too; and, after the switch is turned off and the session is started again, a new stretch reading `working record` while the earlier lines keep their summaries. One of the stretches opens with a thought, and reads its summary on both routes (§M, clause 4). Each closed stretch is one more model call, on the mod's model, under the same spend cap.
3. **Check 5,** in the same sitting, and its reading against 40.
4. **The two cross-build runs,** `e2e:cross-build` with `--peer-exact f1852de3a` and with `--peer-exact 2c7044231`, over §H's four scenes on a Mac (the two unit-mawsd ones skip off macOS), on the final head or the last head that changed what §F names. The pull request names both.
5. **Three looks.** With the changed mod installed in a scratch config home: a session build F runs, and one the installed build runs, each looking as it did (A16); and an earlier build's window on a session this build launched at 9, to see §H's row 5 as it is before the owner is asked card 2.
6. **The owner's sitting.** Scenes: a session MAWS runs drawn by the mod, before and after a relaunch; the mod's switch to the full transcript and back; the lane switched off with the plugin on. For the summary: the record lines of a session MAWS runs and of a terminal session; the switch off and on.
7. **The look of a long summary,** light theme: a record line whose label is 120 characters, in a main column of 480 px (`MAIN_MIN_WIDTH` in `src/renderer/src/app/regions.ts`), cut with an ellipsis; the mods list's second line as frame 11 draws it; and the view switch's button measured at least 24 px tall with its look as before. Against frames 11 and 12 of the drawn example as redrawn.

| M3 | 15 | +600 −300 | scenes, the desk's eight, the record's first line |
| **the first pull request** | **about 80** | **about 5,300** | |
| M4 | 46 | +1,950 −80 | the decision's pure functions, the declaration's reader, the reservation, the status's subscription, one command through the host, one wire minor; for the summary, the reader of the mod's file, two commands, the switch's key and its value in a launch |
| M5 | 24 | +1,250 −100 | the surface rows and their inputs, the reveal, the cutover's switch |
| M7 | 30 | +1,200 −30 | the label's rule and the one lookup rule, the shared table's copy and its test, what a window last read and its rounds, the row's label, the mods list's switch |
| M6 | 12 | +900 −60 | scenes (the trial, four cross-build since the I4 ruling, the summary's and the switch's), the record's second line |
| **the second pull request** | **about 110** | **about 5,600** | |
| P (doperpowers), as built at `e511aad9` | 17 | +1,371 −112 | the gate, the declaration, the placement, the turn line's rule, the tracker's gate, the value that turns summaries off, the file's contract (replacement, merge, shape 2), the lookup's one change and its table of cases, their tests, the check script, the sentences |

**Each pull request's branch is reviewed by the xhigh panel,** by size and by stakes. The first's lenses: (1) *the port*: §B clause by clause against the mod's source at 0.8.2; (2) *the flag*: no pass projects withheld rows, by the three routes, with resets and interruptions; (3) *cost*: the flip, the flat list with no turn fold, the mounted rows; (4) *UC-8 and UC-3*: the insertions of §K, U1b's rows. The second's: (1) *one side draws*: §A, §G and §H as state machines, the reservation's window, a daemon's restart, a reload, the unreadable row; (2) *across builds*: §F against both earlier builds, the tolerant reader, the added column, the new command on an older host; (3) *an ask per row*: §E against check 1's fixtures, each dependent row mounted alone, the full site, the reveal; (4) *the lane off*: R4's rows. (5) *the summary*: §M against the mod's source at 0.8.2, clause by clause; no path on which MAWS asks a model; a window that holds the last successful read whole; rounds that are never cut short; `runOf` against §M's one rule and the shared table; a reader that takes no path from a caller; the switch against both earlier builds. Each milestone's own review at its boundary is a single reviewer, at the high rung for M1, M4 and M5 and the medium rung for the others. P is reviewed in its own repository.

## The pull requests

Each body names: its class 1a run (`scripts/ci-gate.sh "$PWD" --gates static,unit,probes --label u2b` and `u2b-2`); the panel's verdict with what was dismissed or deferred; the e2e groups run and where; its residue (its tracker rows). It carries its dated line of "The record" and ends with the trailer the build brief gives. The first also says: every launch still advertises 8; the desk web app draws the new rows from its next deploy on and keeps its bar; the second follows. The second also names: the two cross-build runs by their peers; the trial's reading and check 5's; check 1's findings file; the mod's pull request beside it, and that nothing here waits for its merge. For the summary it names §M, cards 10 and 11 as answered, check 1's answers to questions E, and that MAWS asks no model.

## Surprises & Discoveries

- Observation: the installed mod is 0.8.2, two minor lines past the 0.7.3 the first landing's document read, and 0.8.0, the number the design gave to the release that draws at 9, is in use. `native()` is unchanged through all of them; an eleventh caller and a fold-summary tracker were added.
  Evidence: the install record's `version`; the cache's directories 0.7.2 to 0.8.2; `to-human.tsx` lines 1450 to 1455 and 1616; `folds.tsx` line 592.
- Observation: the lane's state is on the wire and its objects are strict, so the published field is a wire minor; and a launch's id is the host's, so nothing about a launch is durable before it runs unless it is reserved first.
  Evidence: `wire-manifest.json`, `commands.ownerChannel.state` and `events.ownerChannel.changed`; `src/services/sessions/resume.ts`, the loop around `launchOnHost`; `src/services/owner/store.ts`, `recordLaunch`.
- Observation: the engine draws a thought's text through `AssistantMessage` with `isSummary: true`, and its closing line through `TurnDuration`, a site a remote surface may ask. So two of the rows this document first called site-less have a site of the engine's own.
  Evidence: `cli.pretty.js` of `2.1.322`, lines 923794, 929014 and 1127722.
- Observation: MAWS's render command names its sites in a closed set on the wire; an added name is a wire major.
  Evidence: `wire-manifest.json`, `commands.session.ui.render`, `component`'s `enum`; the rule at the head of `src/shared/ipc/wire.ts`.
- Observation: with no advertisement the mod draws on MAWS's surface once it has seen a mark, whatever the lane and the style.
  Evidence: `native()` (1452 to 1455): an unset value is not native; the style is read by `message.tsx` alone.
- Observation: for a session on another machine the lane cannot be read, so today's report never applies there.
  Evidence: `src/shared/ipc/remote-unavailable.ts`, `'ownerChannel.state': notYet('the owner channel')`.
- Observation: the mod's fold is in force by time, not by place, and it leaves a question's call in view.
  Evidence: `isFolding = () => hasSeenMark && !isFullTranscript` (line 1512); `isRead` (605).
- Observation: a terminal session no port drives has no composer, so a switch mounted only there is missing on the route the mirror exists for.
  Evidence: `session/SessionPane.tsx`, `noticed`.
- Observation: `e2e/to-human-live.spec.ts` skips on both branches of its own condition.
  Evidence: its `skipReason` returns a reason with and without `MAWS_C_LIVE=1`.
- Observation (the revision for the summary): two gates keep the mod's tracker from asking under MAWS, and this document named one. `isAsking` requires an interactive session as well as a quiet-free one, and the engine raises a headless start as not interactive.
  Evidence: `folds.tsx`, `isAsking` (566) and `isQuiet` (592), at 0.8.2 and on the mod's main branch (the two texts are the same); `cli.pretty.js` of `2.1.322`, line 611529 (`surface: null, isInteractive: !1`) against line 1205481 (the terminal: `surface: "terminal", isInteractive: !0`).
- Observation (the same revision): the live line is not gated, so from 9 the mod's record line in MAWS reads its newest call while a run grows, whatever is decided about summaries; and each call writes a `$.state` value the drawn buttons read. MAWS drops the `instances` of an invalidation and re-asks every mounted site of the session.
  Evidence: `folds.tsx`, `follow` (382: a call sets `run.line` and `isLive` with no reference to `isAsking`) and `publish` (645); `to-human.tsx`, `foldLabel` (1164); `src/engine-host/normalizer-system.ts`, the case `ui_invalidate`; `engine-surface/registry.ts`, `invalidateSession`. No fixture under `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/` holds a `ui_invalidate` with `instances`, or a state write seen from a surface.
- Observation (the same revision): a summary the mod made is in a file of the mod's, one per session, and nowhere in the transcript; the mod itself reads that file back at a resume. So a session at rest can read the words the running session showed, from the one copy there is.
  Evidence: `folds.tsx`, `stored` (442), `keepFile` (661), `load` (725).
- Observation (the same revision): MAWS has no route to a model of its own, and the wave's rule for an owner's switch (a layout key) names a store the daemon does not read.
  Evidence: no API client in `package.json` or the workspace packages'; `src/shared/net/egress.ts`, `EGRESS_RULES`; `git grep` for the layout's reads under `src/services` and `src/mawsd` answers nothing; `git show f1852de3a:src/services/index/migrations.ts` holds no `index_meta` and `git show 2c7044231:…` does.

- Observation (check 1): with the asks exactly as §E gives them, a live stretch lost its record line when its second call arrived, in 0.8.2 and on the mod's branch alike. The lone `ToolUse` row, which MAWS no longer mounts once the run asks as a group, kept the run's place and its button; the group answered an empty Box; the engine went on naming the unmounted instance. Neither §E nor §I had the case. Fixed in the mod (`joinGroup`), and recorded again with the same asks.
  Evidence: the findings file, "Found beside the table", 1, and "Second round"; `u2b-mod-summary`, `u2b-mod-mock-grow-*` against their `-fix` recordings.
- Observation (check 1): the mod places a row by its uuid's first 24 characters or by another id whole, so a uuid qualified by a block has no place. §E's id for a thought was wrong, and MAWS's existing ask of a text that is a later block uses the same form (reported point 22).
  Evidence: the findings file, C2; `u2b-mod-rows`; the mod's `rowKey`.
- Observation (check 1): a plugin reload pushed no invalidation when the installation had not changed (four runs), and one when it had. MAWS re-asks only at an invalidation today.
  Evidence: the findings file, C4 and A3; `engine-surface/site.ts`, `connectEngineSurface`.
- Observation (check 1): a summary call is in no `result` frame and in no transcript record; the request the engine sends asks for more tokens than the mod names (6048 against 4000).
  Evidence: the findings file, E6; `u2b-mod-summary`, `u2b-mod-summary-fix`.
- Observation (check 1): the sminos ask mod's drawing is not gated by the advertisement; only its tool is. At 8 it answered a seeded question's row and the band (reported point 21).
  Evidence: the findings file, A1; `u2b-mod-8-before`; the sminos plugin's `registerAsk`.
- Observation (the mod's branch): the plugin test kit cannot read a data file, so the shared table of cases is read by a script beside the kit.
  Evidence: the builder's report, departure 1, with the kit's refusal line (§I, "As built").
- Observation (the drawing of frames 11 and 12): the first pull request's view switch drew `to-human view ·full transcript ›`, with no space after the dot: its caption and its button are two items of a flex line, and a flex item drops its trailing space. Fixed on `ux-wave/U2b` at `11e183013` by the architect. Nothing of it is M4's to M7's to build; the second pull request's base holds that commit.

Its words (`To-human in every session`) are its accessible name alone (`aria-label` on a `role="switch"` button, 28 by 16 px), so the first text of frame 11, which listed them as a visible line, was wrong; and the row's pending line is computed from what the last launch carried of the lane's instructions, nothing else.

- Observation (the rulings on the review of the summary): as the mod's `labelOf` stands, a row placed before a run's first tracked step reads no run, and the tracker follows no thinking. At 9 MAWS asks a thought's site, so a thought that opens a stretch is the row that draws the line: under the mod's rule as installed, such a stretch would read `working record` where the mod draws, with its summary made and paid for.
  Evidence: `folds.tsx`, `labelOf` (513 to 524: `first <= at <= last`) and `stepsOf` (thinking is among the rows that do nothing); §E's row for a thought.
- Observation (the same rulings): `keepFile` writes the whole file from one process's memory, and the engine's plugin interface has no rename; MAWS itself cannot be a second writer's cause, since it refuses to start a process on a session a live process holds.
  Evidence: `folds.tsx`, `keepFile` (661) and `stored` (442); the plugin calls listed at `cli.pretty.js` line 859745 (`fs.read`, `fs.write`, `fs.list`, `fs.exists`, `fs.stat`, `fs.ancestors`); `src/services/sessions/holders.ts` (`holderAuthority`), `start-guards.ts` (`held`), `resume.ts` (the second check), `pause-take-over.ts` (the stopped CLI, verified and then killed).

- Observation (M1): the derivation budget, base against head (Concrete Steps 4). Base, alone at `02eed5eb3` (22:57 PST, the branch's VM): `perf: events=43919 rows=1574 deriveMs=215.1 deltaBatchMedianMs=0.090 deltaBatchMaxMs=5.808`. Head `9c368ae54`, alone: `perf: events=43919 rows=1574 deriveMs=253.5 deltaBatchMedianMs=0.106 deltaBatchMaxMs=6.029`. The VM was loaded and single readings moved by a third between runs of the same code, so base and head were then read alternately in one sitting (base from a scratch worktree at `02eed5eb3` on the VM): base `deriveMs` 320.9, 240.2, 315.6; head 308.1, 271.2, 316.3; median delta batch 0.08 to 0.14 ms on both. No difference beyond the noise; the lane-off path gains one flag read per turn derivation. The new lane-on case (`owner/view/perf.test.ts`, 500 turns with a message in each, no turn folded, mirrored): `perf (lane on, mirrored): events=43919 rows=3720 drawn=3198 deriveMs=286.4 deltaBatchMedianMs=0.097 deltaBatchMaxMs=25.894` (in the sitting: 301.8, 274.1, 307.0, 369.6). Under that load the existing case's own bound of 300 ms failed once at the head and the base read above it too: that bound is a wall-time bound on a shared VM.
  Evidence: the four `vmcheck.sh ux-wave/U2b` runs of `MAWS_PERF=1 pnpm exec vitest run` named in M1's report.
- Observation (M1): the node project lists `src/renderer/src/transcript/model/*.ts` and is composite, so a suite of the model's directory cannot import `owner/view/mirror.ts` (an unlisted file). The lane-on case of the budget therefore lives beside the mirror, in `owner/view/perf.test.ts`; Concrete Steps 4's command at M3's head names both files.
  Evidence: `tsconfig.node.json`, the include's comment above `src/renderer/src/transcript/model/*.ts`.
- Observation (M1): no recorded pair of a journal and a transcript under `fixtures/uxwave/` holds a `<message>` or a message-tool call; the recorded session with messages on all three routes is P4-R7's `p4/p4-r7-tool-stages.jsonl` (its stream frames, and its transcript in a `disk` meta row), which `lane-a/view-routes.test.ts` draws. The wire alone carries the process's own error line at its end, which the comparison leaves out (every error stays in sight on every route).
  Evidence: a count of marked texts and message calls over `fixtures/uxwave/*.transcript.jsonl`; the failed first run of `view-routes.test.ts` (`expected [ …(9) ] to deeply equal [ …(10) ]`, the extra row `error`).
- Observation (M1): standalone browser activity is placed by the view (`withBrowserActivityRows`), never by the store, and it stays in sight, so a standalone browser row the view places inside a stretch splits it there and not in the store's rows. M1 first left the store's jump reading the stretches of its own rows, so a jump to a row after such an activity opened the first half's key and left its target folded; M1's review found it and the fix wave reversed that: the jump reads the rows each mounted view composes (`mountView`, decision below).
  Evidence: `Transcript.tsx`, `withBrowserActivityRows`; the review's reproduction; `owner/view/jump.test.tsx`.

- Observation (M2): MAWS's own `AssistantMessage` input gives `isFirstOfReply` to the first text block of an API message only (`transcript/surface/adapters.ts`, `assistantInput`: `row.blockIndex === firstIndex`), where the engine gives it to every text row of its conversation (ruling 4's entry below). A mod asked through MAWS's surface therefore draws a later text block of one API message without its bullet where the engine's own list draws one. M2 leaves the adapter alone (the surface is the second pull request's); M5 decides whether the ask follows the engine.
  Evidence: `adapters.ts` line 46; the Decision Log's ruling 4 entry; no recorded fixture holds two text blocks in one API message with a mark in the later one (a scan of `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/**/*.jsonl`), so no test pins either reading on that case.
- Observation (M2): Streamdown caches its unified processor by its plugins' function names (`generateCacheKey` in its dist reads each plugin's `.name`), so two decorations that are both anonymous functions share whichever processor was built first. M2's four decorations of a text (completed or streaming, whole or a closed head's messages alone) each carry a name of their own (`owner/to-human/message-ink.ts`, `named`); a later decoration passed to `Markdown` must too.
  Evidence: `node_modules/streamdown/dist/chunk-*.js`, the key loop over `f.name`; the first VM run of `message.test.tsx`, whose closed head drew its narration (the whole text's processor) until the names were given.
- Observation (M2): an open message while its text streams is drawn as a message (its label and ` …`, §D), on every route that draws a live row through `AssistantTextRow`, the desk web app's streamed tail included. §L's row for `depth-info.spec.ts` keeps the scene "a streaming message stays authored text until closed…"; its streaming assertions (literal `<message>` and no `.owner-message-ink` while open) now read the label and the body, so M3 rewrites that scene's streaming half, not only its stylesheet read. The unit cases that pinned the literal text are rewritten (`lane-a/view/streaming-rows.test.ts`, `owner/report/report-view.test.tsx`).
  Evidence: `apps/desk-web/e2e/depth-info.spec.ts`, its streaming assertions; `transcript/view/rows/AssistantText.tsx`, the live path.
- Observation (M2): the question machinery of `owner/report/answering.ts` (the card's send, its report to main, its Cancel, Record again, and their stores) had no caller once the cards went: only the request card's Mark read reads the file. M2 removed it with the cards, kept `markRead`, and added the bar's "mark every item read, or say why not"; `test/lane-b/renderer-guards.test.ts`'s entry for the file now names `ownerChannel.markRead` alone, and `test/one-predicate.test.ts` drops `QuestionCard.tsx`'s exception. Main's `ownerChannel.answered` and `ownerChannel.cancel` stay on the wire, now with no caller in the renderer.
  Evidence: `git grep` for the removed exports over `src`, `apps` and `test`; the two guards' tables.

- Observation (M2, fix wave): Streamdown's root component is memoised on its text, its mode and a few display props, not on its plugins, so a `Markdown` handed another decoration over the same text keeps drawing with the old one until the text or the mode changes. So a drawing that changes over the same text must change component or key: the presenter draws a closed head as its own component (`HeadMessages` in `owner/to-human/Presenter.tsx`), so a head that opens or folds mounts afresh.
  Evidence: the root `memo`'s comparator in streamdown's dist (`children`, `mode`, `isAnimating`, `className` … and no `rehypePlugins`); `owner/report/report-view.test.tsx`'s full-transcript case, which drew a head's messages-only drawing after the view switched until the drawing remounted.

- Observation (M3): the derivation budget at M3's head (Concrete Steps 4), beside M1's base. Alone at `9fa8f8bce` (load 13 to 17): `perf (lane on, mirrored): events=43919 rows=3720 drawn=3198 deriveMs=463.6 deltaBatchMedianMs=0.095 deltaBatchMaxMs=42.701`; the lane-off case read 402.8 and failed its own 300 ms wall bound. Base (`02eed5eb3`, a scratch VM worktree) and head read alternately in one sitting, each side's `transcript/model/perf.test.ts` alone (load 9 to 13): base `deriveMs` 309.0, 259.0, 588.3, 989.9; head 347.5, 278.6, 582.9, 1019.6, pair by pair 1.12, 1.08, 0.99 and 1.03 of the base, so within a fifth; median delta batch 0.10 to 0.18 ms (base), 0.08 to 0.30 ms (head). The lane-on case after them: 770.3 and 1206.3, the load rising. A first alternated sitting ran the head's two files together, which vitest runs in parallel workers, and read the head 1.31 of the base by its median (705.5, 396.7, 538.6 against 381.3, 411.7, 485.2): that was the lane-on case competing for the cores, so a head's two files are read one at a time.
  Evidence: the `vmcheck.sh ux-wave/U2b` runs of `MAWS_PERF=1 pnpm exec vitest run` named in M3's report.
- Observation (M3): gate-plan's walk of raw time bounds (`.github/scripts/gate-plan.test.mjs`, `rawTimeBounds`) reads a bare name's declaration line, so a count measured by a page function that takes its window as `durationMs` reads as a time. The mirrored anchoring scene's bound of 40 mounted rows asserts a property of the window's result (`streamWindow.domRows`), as the existing anchoring scene's `window10s.domRows` does.
  Evidence: the first `node --test .github/scripts/*.test.mjs` at `5a5305151` listed `expect(mounted, 'DOM rows').toBeLessThanOrEqual(40);`; passes at `9fa8f8bce`.

## Decision Log

- Decision: the verification call. This document gets an adversarial review before any executor starts (the architect dispatches it). Each pull request's branch gets `doperpowers:review-code` at the xhigh panel, with the lenses of "Size and review"; each milestone a single reviewer at its boundary. Class 1a on GitHub Actions is the gate of record; the Electron groups, the two cross-build runs, the trial and the measured scene are the architect's.
  Rationale: about 150 files in the surface the owner reads most, a wire minor, a stored value, and a cutover between two programs that deploy separately.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15, this document's author.
- Decision: revised at the architect's rulings R1 to R13 on the adversarial review (seven findings, verdict "needs attention") and on this document's reported points. What changed, by ruling. **R1:** no constant names a release of the mod; an installation's declaration decides, read at the launch and at the loaded plugin's path; the failed-release list of the child's file is not built (a release that cannot hide a run does not carry the declaration); §A, §G, §I. **R2:** the advertisement is reserved before the send and bound after; an advertisement that cannot be known is published `mod` while the mod may be loaded; the host cannot answer for a running launch, so the reservation is the record; decisions 3 and 4, §A, §F, M4. **R3:** the lead rule and its registry are withdrawn; every row the fold can hide asks a site of its own (the first means, taken whole: a thought and a turn's line at the engine's own sites, the latter through one new command; a listing and a browser action as calls; a notice as an arrival; a separator by a probe mounted with it); error rows now stay, having no site; check 5's remedy is withdrawn; §B, §E, §I, check 1's questions C. **R4:** a launch's advertisement has three values, and with none the mod draws when its plugin is loaded; §A, §G, card 8. **R5:** the mirror is applied only while the stored flag is set, and `Transcript` writes the outside rows' part of the flag before paint; M1. **R6:** a reveal ends after its containing row has entered the mounted range and left it; §E. **R7:** two pull requests, six milestones; Progress, "Proof" and "Size and review" are per pull request. **R8:** the wire minor (now an optional key and a command), the stored column and the second mount are in §F and §K as permitted. **R9:** the toggle says why where the mod draws; "Mark all read" is a palette command, one new action id; §D, §K. **R11:** eight cards, each with the place its recommended answer is built. **R12, R13:** U1b's document read at `e5d34d52b`; the table and the shared files corrected; an asynchronous answer is stated once outside the record (decision 12, §B, M1's test).
  Rationale: the law at the head of this document; the review's evidence was read against the code and holds (the launch's order in `resume.ts` and `launch.ts`; the registry's retained sites; the desk's tail). Rejected at the revision: adding `TurnDuration` to the existing command's set (a wire major); asking a site for a retry line or a plugin's notice (none exists: they stay); keeping any rule by which one row follows another's remembered report.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15, this document's author, at the architect's rulings.

- Decision: the owner's answers to the cards, 2026-11-15 (UTC), recorded verbatim in "Questions for the owner". "Unnamed rows" (card 3): "! command and errors stay (Recommended)". "Lone thought" (card 5): "It folds (Recommended)". "Mod layout" (card 9): "Evened out for MAWS (Recommended)". "Switch frame" (card 6): "Bordered box (Recommended)". "Other machine" (card 4): "This app's switch (Recommended)". "Lane off" (card 8): "Let the mod draw (Recommended)". "Fold line" (card 1), typed: "we actually recently made changes to how '> working record' shows in terinal -- in short, it no longer shows '> working record', but shows '> <summaries>' instead. and i think that's how we should do it. mods already shipped it and is active already in terminal. it uses haiku call instead of forked call, and is cheap enough to use. But let user be able to disable it if they want to cut that token cost, and in that case it'll just be working record."
  Rationale: each chosen answer is the default this document builds. The typed answer to card 1 is read by the architect's ruling of the same date (below): the summaries are ruled into the second pull request; the first's record line reads `working record`. The sentences of "Left", reported point 3 and card 1 that called the summaries out of this landing's scope are corrected to say so, and "The record" gains the tracker row "the record line's summary in MAWS", owned by the second pull request.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the owner, recorded by the executor of the first pull request.
- Decision: the architect's rulings after the revision, 2026-11-15 (UTC). **1.** The author's seven departures from the architect's rulings are accepted as this document states them; those concerning M4 to M6 belong to the second pull request. **2.** Card 2 is decided: going back to an earlier build while the mod draws a session is accepted (second pull request; a tracker row there). Card 7 is decided: the desk web app keeps its Report · Transcript bar and the architect registers the desk ticket; §L is built as written (M2, M3). **3.** The mirror's look is "evened out", each item at one named place so the other answer is a small change: (i) a later message's label stands in a fixed two-column bullet slot, so `message` under `● message` keeps the indent (M2, `owner/view/Message.tsx`); (ii) a record line has the transcript's ordinary row spacing, with no extra row above it (M1, `owner/view/RecordFold.tsx` with its rule in `view.css`); (iii) no dim bullet stands before a record line (M1, `RecordFold.tsx`); (iv) no empty row stands above the switch inside its box (M2, `owner/view/Switch.tsx` with its rule in `view.css`); (v) the switch's caption and its button are one text size (M2, `Switch.tsx`/`view.css`); (vi) a message's body has the transcript's line spacing (M2, `Message.tsx`/`view.css`); (vii) a turn's line keeps the indent its chevron gave when the chevron is absent (M1, `transcript/view/rows/Divider.tsx`). The executor of each item names its one place in its report and in this entry's follow-up. **4.** "A reply's first text" (which message carries the bullet, M2's "the label follows `spanRows`") is defined as the engine defines it: M2 reads the rule in the engine bundle the repository pins, states it in this document, and tests it on a recorded fixture under `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/`; it is not inferred. **5.** A turn that is running shows, in the folded view, only its line (`Working for …`) after a closed record, as frame 5 of the drawn example has it (M1; decision 11). **6.** The frame with a permission waiting follows U1b only once U1b is on master; it is not, so M1 to M3 build against master as it is (the docked card and its pointer row stay) and build nothing around U1b; "What is assumed of landings in flight" says what each side may rely on.
  Rationale: the architect's dispatch of the first pull request, after the owner's look at the drawn example.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the architect, recorded by the executor of the first pull request.

- Decision (M1): what M1 left open, decided for the milestones after it. **(a)** `reportOnly` is set by the mirror on a closed stretch's head from M1; drawing only its messages there is M2's `MessageView` (until then the head draws whole, as a marked text does today). **(b)** A live text with an open mark is classed `stays` while it streams, never `mixed`: a native live row's markdown is its first delta, so its words outside the mark cannot be read without its buffer, and the native and the outside routes must agree; it is classed by its words once it completes. **(c)** The live marks a caller passes are the store's (`liveMarkedRowIds`) joined with those of the live rows handed in from outside (`liveMarksOf(stored, outside)`, exported beside them), and a live row is marked exactly when its id is among them; `mirrorsView(ui)` (exported) is the one test of "the flag is set, the view is the report, MAWS draws it", read by `Transcript`, `drawnRows` and the store's jump. **(d)** `Transcript`'s outside rows are its `tail` and its `placed` rows. A mount writes `tailMessage` when its own reading changes or the key started over, never merely because the stored value differs, so two mounts of one key cannot undo each other. **(e)** The flag is recomputed in `put` for every new state (`withViewFlag`), so events, a reset, a staged install and the lane all reach it in their own update; `setToHumanView` first guesses it with the lane as it will be, from the turns' rows and, when the lane turns on, from their completed texts' marks (rows the lane-off derivation folded hide a marked commentary), so turning the lane on over a history that holds a message derives every turn once; `put` settles a wrong guess. **(f)** `openStretches` is optional in `TranscriptUi`, as `reportOpenTurns` was; absent reads as empty. **(g)** The rows of no stretch: a running turn's line counts as covered when it lies inside a closed stretch or when the next row of a class is a closed stretch's head or first row, and is then drawn directly after that record line (ruling 5); a closed turn's line drawn because another record of its turn is open, whose own place lies inside a closed stretch, is drawn after that stretch's record line; a separator is drawn when the row after it is. **(h)** `browser_activity` under a call folds with it (`recordRow`); only a standalone one stays. **(i)** A stretch's key is its head's id when a mixed text heads it, else `stretchKey` of its first folding row, so a text completing into a mixed head after its run started does not rekey the run. **(j)** The record line hands keyboard focus to its other line when that line is drawn (the two stand at the two ends of the stretch). **(k)** With no fold, a turn's line is `TurnLine` in `Divider.tsx`, the label in a span set as the disclosure was and the chevron's slot an empty `transcript-chevron` span (ruling 3, item vii).
  Rationale: each follows the nearest decision of this document (8, 9, 11; R5; ruling 5) or the mod's own function it ports; (e) keeps the lane flip's cost at today's single derivation.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15, the executor of M1.
- Decision (M1): the places of ruling 3's M1 items. **(ii)** the record line's spacing: `owner/view/view.css`, the rule `.owner-record-fold { padding: 2px 0 8px; }` (no margin above; the mod's own line stands one row lower); **(iii)** no bullet before it: `owner/view/RecordFold.tsx`, whose button holds only `RECORD.closed` or `RECORD.open`; **(vii)** a turn's line keeps its chevron's indent: `transcript/view/rows/Divider.tsx`, `TurnLine`'s empty `<span className="transcript-chevron" aria-hidden="true" />`, with `.transcript-turn-line` in `transcript/view/transcript.css`.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15, the executor of M1.

- Decision (M1, fix wave after M1's review): three of M1's choices amended and two added. **(d), amended:** the outside rows' term is any mounted view's part: each `Transcript` keeps its own reading of its `tail` and `placed` rows (`outsideMessages` in `Transcript.tsx`, by mount) and writes the term (any part true) through `setToHumanView`, whose signature is unchanged; a mount's part goes when it unmounts, and the term is written again then. The rule "a mount writes only when its own reading changes" is withdrawn: it let the last writer win, so a second mount with no message cleared the first's. **(j), amended:** the hand-off is the transcript's own and lapses: a press asks focus for its other line in the mount it was pressed in (scoped by the mount's id prefix, `owner/view/handoff.ts`); the request is taken once and lapses at the next animation frame, so a line the list did not draw in the press's own update, or a line of another pane, is not handed focus. **(l), added:** the store's jump resolves the stretch over the rows each mounted view composes before the mirror (its resting and placed rows, browser activity, queue tail, with its live marks), handed to the store by `mountView(key, compose)` (additive; released on unmount) and read when the jump is asked; with no view mounted, over the key's own rows; mounted views that compose alike name one stretch, and the jump opens the stretch holding its target in each. Item (h)'s standalone browser row stays in sight, so it splits the stretch, and the jump now agrees. **(m), added:** when `noTurnFold` takes effect, a turn fold the reader started and still running is settled at once, before paint (`WorkMotion.settleFolds`): its turn is folded in the store as asked, its close motion skipped, so no row of the view (a record line included) waits on it, and the turn is folded when the view leaves force.
  Rationale: the review's three Important findings and two Minor ones, each reproduced; (d) and (j) as first written did not hold with two mounts of one key; (l) is M1's jump contract, which exempts no row the view places.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15, the executor of M1.

- Decision (M2, ruling 4): "a reply's first text", the message that carries the bullet, as the engine defines it at both versions the repository reads (the pin `TESTED_ENGINE_VERSION = '2.1.312'`, `src/engine-host/engine-compat.ts` line 16, and 2.1.322). **The rule: every assistant text row of the engine's conversation list is drawn with `isFirstOfReply: true`,** so the first message of every text block reads `● message`, a text block after a tool call in the same reply included; only a later message of the same block reads `message` (the mod's `spanRows`, `i === 0 ? bullet : '  '`). The engine's chain, in `~/claude-code-bundle/<version>/cli.pretty.js`: the `AssistantMessage` site's props are `{ text, isFirstOfReply: c }` with `c` the row's `shouldShowDot` (2.1.322 line 923485; 2.1.312 line 734530); the list's row passes `shouldShowDot: Ut` with `Ut = !_ && !A`, not following a speaker label and not continuing a labelled reply (2.1.322 lines 679065 and 679068; 2.1.312 `yt = !T && !C`, lines 1170921 and 1170924); a continuation is `_L = !jR && Vc.has(vn)` (2.1.322 line 681063), and `Vc` is filled only when `Zn = he && po !== null && Et !== Bc` (line 680797), where `po` is `null` at module scope (line 680459; 2.1.312 line 1172311) and is never assigned in the list's code, so no row follows a speaker label or continues one. The other `shouldShowDot: !1` sites (the transcript mode's and a few tool rows') are not the conversation list MAWS's view stands for. The mod draws its bullet from `e.props.isFirstOfReply` (`to-human.tsx` line 1724, at 0.8.2) and always for its tool call (1809). Tested on recorded fixtures (`owner/view/message.test.tsx`): `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/p4/p4-r7-loss-short.jsonl` (two text blocks of one reply with a tool call between them, each a message: both `● message`) and `pass3/p2-to-human-converge-s13-new.jsonl` (one text block whose `<to-human>` holds three older marks: `● message`, then `message` three times).
  Rationale: the architect's ruling 4; read in the bundle, not inferred.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15, the executor of M2.
- Decision (M2): the places of ruling 3's M2 items. **(i)** a later message's `message` in the bullet's fixed two-column slot: `owner/view/Message.tsx`, `MessageLabel` and `messageLabelNode` (the bullet's `span.owner-message-bullet` drawn empty after the first), with `.owner-message-bullet { display: inline-block; width: 2ch; }` and the label's `margin-inline-start: -2ch` in `owner/view/view.css`; **(iv)** no empty row above the switch inside its box: `owner/view/Switch.tsx` (the band's frame, with nothing before the line), with `.owner-view-switch` in `view.css` carrying no margin or top padding; **(v)** the caption and the button one text size: `.owner-view-switch { font-size: var(--text-ui) }` and `.owner-view-switch > button { font-size: inherit }` in `view.css`; **(vi)** a message's body at the transcript's line spacing: `owner/view/Message.tsx` (the body is the text's own Markdown) and `view.css`, which sets no line height and spaces the blocks inside a message as the answer's own (`space-y-4`'s step), the label taking none of it.

- Decision (M2): what M2 left open, decided for the milestones after it. **(a)** A message is what the mod reads as one (`parseMarked`): every older mark (`to-human`, `essential`, `need-input`, `insight`) is drawn as `<message>`, and each run of text under one mark is its own message, so a mark nested in another and the outer mark's text after it are each a message (A12; "Words used here"). In the full drawing a nested mark inside one line keeps its paragraph, one wrapper, since the text keeps one Markdown tree; a closed stretch's head draws the mod's message spans themselves instead (the architect's direction of 2026-11-15, below). **(b)** The streaming label: a live text whose mark is still open at its end draws that message with ` …` (the shared grammar's `parseLive` leniency, `hasMessages(text, true)`), through `AssistantTextRow`'s live path, since `transcript/surface/Rows.tsx` asks the presenter for completed marks only; a completed text never repairs a mark left open. (Kept by the third fix wave, below: that reading reaches main's stored marks, so every reader keeps it; the difference from the mod is the second pull request's.) **(c)** A closed stretch's head (`reportOnly`) draws only its messages: `Row.tsx` wraps the assistant row in `ReportOnly` (a context in `Message.tsx`), which the presenter reads to choose its decoration, so the surface's own drawing path is unchanged. **(d)** The lane function is `viewLaneOf(owner, elsewhere, appSwitch)` with `useViewLane` beside it in `TranscriptSlot.tsx`; another machine's terminal session (`lane-a/view/RemoteTranscriptSlot.tsx`) takes the same lane, its pane now carrying the view as card 4 decided. **(e)** The title "Mark all read" is written once, in the action's row of `src/shared/keymap/actions.ts` (the palette reads action titles there; `src/shared` cannot import the renderer's `copy.ts`); the failure's toast keeps `REPORT.notMarkedAll` and `REPORT.retry` in `owner/report/copy.ts`. The action is not rebindable (build F does not know its id), and the keymap's table test pins it so. **(f)** "Mark all read" counts the unread items as the bar did (`unreadOf`, an essential unread or a question unanswered) and asks nothing with none. **(g)** The switch also stands above a compact pane's composer (the split's card), since it is mounted in the composer.
  Rationale: (a) to (c) follow §D and A12 and keep the surface's ask untouched for M5; (d) is card 4's answer on both of that machine's routes; (e) is UC-6's single place for an action's title.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15, the executor of M2.

Built the plain way: `parseMarked` is ported (`owner/view/marked.ts`, `markedSpans`; its glued reading through `gluedCodeRegions`, one export added to `src/shared/owner/to-human-parser.ts`), and the head draws each span's text as its own Markdown under its own label (`owner/to-human/Presenter.tsx`, `HeadMessages`), the first with the bullet, as `spanRows` draws a folded mixed row (which spans there are is amended by the one reading, below). One reading of MAWS's own is kept: a tag after a backslash is no tag, as MAWS's full drawing shows it literally (the mod would read it). The tree-cutting code is removed from `message-ink.ts`. The port's own file (`owner/view/marked.ts`) is replaced by the one reading below.
