- Decision: one reading of a text's marks, the architect's direction on M2's second re-review (2026-11-15, UTC), binding, as corrected the same day: the law of the wave is one reading of each fact in the whole app. Which words are a message and which are the record's comes from one function for the presenter's gate, the full drawing, a closed stretch's head and the mirror (`marksOfText`, so `holdsMessage`, `liveMarksOf`, `liveMarkedRowIds`, §B's `stays`/`mixed`/`folds`, `derive.ts`'s `forHuman` and the store's flag): `readMarked` in `src/shared/owner/marked.ts`. The modes differ only in how a message is drawn. Its tags are the shared grammar's (`markTagsOf`, added to `to-human-parser.ts`: what `parse` pairs, or `parseLive` while a block streams, under the strict-then-glued code regions), so the readers outside the renderer, which `parse` serves, read the same tags; its runs are `parseMarked`'s. **Sized first (option 3 of the correction):** the shared grammar is read outside the renderer by `marks.ts` (`marksOf`: the questions and essentials main stores, by mark id and span ordinal, with `grammar: TO_HUMAN_GRAMMAR_VERSION`, `services/owner/projection.ts` and `service.ts`, sent in `ownerChannel.state`, counted as unread items, raised as Needs-you items and notifications), `preview.ts` (`previewLine`, `messageLine`, `markFreeText`: the digest's preview, recent turns, the search index's stored text, attention items, the notifier, the engine host's summaries, side threads) and `to-human-items.ts` (`toHumanOfText`, `finishedTextOf`, `messageOfCall`: lane B's channel, the projection). Reading a mark left open in a completed text as a message would change stored marks and their ids, stored previews and index text, and what the channel sends, which an older build reads; so MAWS keeps the shared grammar's answer there: such a mark, a stray closer and crossing marks are text in every reader, as on master, and the difference from the mod is the second pull request's item (§E). A closing fence glued to the closing tag is read through the glued recovery in every reader (the full drawing takes the glued closer out of the fence's code). A tag after a backslash stays literal in the renderer's readers, the one difference from `parse` (accepted above). `marksOfText`'s `parts` stay the shared grammar's: the desk web app counts a landing's marked spans by them (`apps/desk-web/src/views/project/landed-message.ts`), and the desk draws through the same `Transcript`, presenter and mirror, so the one reading reaches it too. Pinned by `owner/view/agreement.test.tsx`: over every recorded string under `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/` that holds a mark tag and over invented texts (the review's two reproductions among them), completed and streaming, the shared grammar's parts, `marksOfText`, `holdsMessage` or `liveMarksOf`, the gate, the closed head and the full drawing yield the same message and record.
  Rationale: two readers had drifted (the review's reproductions: a mark left open drawn as a message by the head alone; a glued fence's message refused by the gate); folding them makes the partition one by construction, and the renderer cannot read a mark main does not know of.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the architect; recorded by the executor of M2.

- Decision (the whole-branch review's fix wave, finding 1): a reply an interrupt cut (`assistant_text` with `cut`) is drawn through the presenter's `MarkedText` inside its cut frame, under its note: its messages as every message is drawn, and where it heads a closed stretch (`reportOnly`) only its messages, as any head. `Row.tsx` wraps the cut row in `ReportOnly` as it wraps the others; `CutTextRow` keeps its note, its muted frame and no `transcript-answer` class (never drawn as the reply), and draws plain Markdown when the text holds no message. A message inside a cut reply takes the message's own colours (`owner-message-ink`), not the cut's muted one.
  Rationale: the mirror classes a cut text by its words like any text (§B), so it can head a stretch; the drawing must follow the one reading there too.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (finding 2): `unglued` breaks an opening fence glued to its tags as it broke a glued closing fence: where the one reading takes the first tag, the tags go on a line of their own above the fence, under the same indent; only when that fence then closes (a later closing line of its kind, a glued one counting only where its own tag is taken) before any tag the reading takes, so the code CommonMark draws holds none of the reading's tags; a backtick fence whose info string holds a backtick opens nothing, as in the shared grammar.
  Rationale: the reading read the glued opener as a fence; CommonMark read the line as a paragraph and opened a runaway block at the closing line. Splitting where the fence would not close would swallow a taken closer, the disagreement the fix removes.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (finding 3): a question's options pair only within one run of the one reading, between two tags it takes and never across one; an option whose tags stand on either side of a mark's tag is literal text. Taken over popping by kind, because the shared grammar lifts options from one part's text (`liftChoices`) and a closed head draws each span alone: popping by kind would still hide the two tags of a crossing option, which both of those leave literal, so the full drawing would again disagree with the reading. Options in the record (outside every mark) pair within their run as before.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (finding 4; decision 9 and M1's (i) amended): **an open key names a row, not a place.** A stretch is open while it holds a row that answers to an open key: a row answers to its own key (`stretchKey`), and a run also to the key each of its members had as a row of its own (each call's tool-use id, each thought's row id). Open, a stretch is keyed by the first open key it holds (so its `‹ fold to report` names an open key, and the jump finds it open); closed, by decision 9's key of its first row, which a press writes. Folding takes every open key the stretch holds, over the rows each mounted view composes (`openKeysOf`, the store's `toggleStretch`), so two opened stretches that since met fold in one press. `ui.openStretches` stores what it stored, a set of keys written by presses and jumps; `stretchesOf` and `stretchHolding` take the open keys as an optional last argument. The rule holds for a thought taken into the run its first call starts, a run of one call unwrapped at completion, and older rows paged in above, at every step of the stream (`owner/view/mirror.test.ts`, "a stretch stays the one the reader opened", twelve timelines). A key that cannot move was rejected: no row of a stretch survives both ends changing (paging moves its first row, streaming its last, the derivation renames its first). The mod's run is the same run while it starts at the same row of the whole transcript (`runStart`, 430), which the mod reads whole, and its unfolding moves with a row whose id changes form (`adopt`, 1087); MAWS reads by pages and regroups, so the row named carries the opening. **Left:** a thought with no text that the derivation drops when its run of one call unwraps (`groups.ts`, `thoughtRow`) takes its key with it; a stretch opened while that thought was its only row folds when the call completes. Covering it needs the key carried at the regrouping in the store; not built.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (findings 2 and 3, the contract): `owner/view/agreement.test.tsx`, "the drawn row says what the one reading says": a table of marked texts (the review's two cases, the glued closer, a mark left open, a stray closer, crossing marks) drawn as their row draws them (whole, as a reply an interrupt cut, and as a closed stretch's head, cut or not) yields the one reading's message texts inside message wrappers and its record outside them; for the last three, option 3's literal reading.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (the architect's runs of the first pull request's scenes, part 2): each failure classed by the document. **(a), the scene corrected:** `e2e/to-human.spec.ts`'s "Mark read" in the pane was the bar's (`ReportBar`, one unread item): decision 21 removes the bar, decision 20 keeps "Mark all read" as the palette's; the scene now pins that the pane has none. The three U2b scenes in `e2e/transcript.spec.ts` counted `.transcript-divider button[aria-expanded]`, which also matches the turn summary's trigger (`Turn summary`, a popover trigger) that a turn's line keeps (`TurnLine`: its words, its counts and its summary, with no chevron; ruling 3 item vii); they now count the turn's disclosure, `.transcript-divider .transcript-disclosure`. The desk's G1 ("a send stays at the tail…") read a message between agents as a row in the report, where §B folds `agent_message`; it now reads the full transcript ("In the full transcript nothing is folded", §B). **Not classed:** "the to-human view folds work by stretch, draws each message as the mod does…": the document says the scene is right (decision 11: a closed turn's line is drawn while a stretch holding a row of its turn is open), and the renderer draws that line over the same recorded replay (`owner/report/report-view.test.tsx`, "over the report scene's recorded replay…"), so the app's live route differs in a way no unit route here shows. **(c), handed to the architect:** the desk's paging scenes (`depth.spec.ts` "older rows page in when the reader reaches the top…", `session-view.spec.ts` "older rows page in at the top of the session view until the first row…" and the three "a page of older rows answered…"): the pager asks while what is drawn does not fill the pane (`lane-a/view/older-pages.ts`, `scrollHeight - clientHeight <= TOP`) and until the start; the scenes' premise is that the snapshot's newest rows fill the view, so nothing pages before the reader scrolls, and the fold by stretch draws each copy of the recorded session in a handful of rows, so the snapshot no longer fills it: the pager asks at once (held by the scene, or answered to the first row).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.

- Decision: the architect's rulings on what the fix wave stopped on, binding. **1.** The desk's paging scenes (`depth.spec.ts` "older rows page in when the reader reaches the top…", `session-view.spec.ts` "older rows page in at the top of the session view until the first row…", "a session that ended while its view was open…" and the three "a page of older rows answered…") are paging-geometry scenes: they read the desk's full transcript through its Transcript button, where nothing folds. M3's raise of the notification scene's `limit` from 60 to 120 is reverted by the same means. One desk scene in the folded view, the default, shows the pager asking until the pane is filled or the first row is reached and the reader's row staying where it was; `lane-a/view/older-pages.ts` carries a unit case where a page folds to fewer rows than the pane holds. Rejected: a longer recording; a smaller viewport. **2.** The report scene's turn line (`e2e/transcript.spec.ts`, "the to-human view folds work by stretch…"): the scene attaches the pane's rows, as the list and the DOM hold them, when its count fails; the hypothesis that the line is in the drawn list but outside the list's mounted window is checked in the code. **3.** (i) The textless thought's key is fixed in this wave only if a person can hold it open and the fix stays inside the one rule; (ii) focus after folding a stretch whose shape changed is a row of `docs/tech-debt-tracker.md`; (iii) a message inside a cut reply keeps the message's own colours: the cut's frame and its note already say the reply was cut, and a message to the person is still theirs to read at full strength.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the architect; recorded by the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (ruling 1, built): the scenes choose the full transcript before the snapshot lands (the player holds the subscription, the Transcript button is pressed, the snapshot is released: `inFullTranscript` in `session-view.spec.ts`, the same steps around the reload in `depth.spec.ts`), so no folded render pages first. The folded scene is `session-view.spec.ts`, "in the to-human view, older rows page in until the pane is filled, and a page the reader asks for leaves their row where it was" (the long history, `limit` 40). The unit case is `older-pages.test.ts`, "in the to-human view, a page that folds to fewer rows than the pane holds is followed by the next until the pane is filled".
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (ruling 2, found): the line is in the drawn list and outside the mounted window, by the code; no code fault. Evidence: over the scene's recorded replay the renderer's list holds the turn's line once the call's record is open and holds the same three record lines the scene counts (`report-view.test.tsx`), the live route feeds the same rows; the list mounts the visible rows and `OVERSCAN` (6) more on each side (`Transcript.tsx`), and in follow mode keeps its end in view as rows go in, so the two rows the open record adds above the end move the window's start past the line, which stands above the record's rows. The scene now brings the opened record into view before it counts (`opened.scrollIntoViewIfNeeded()`), and attaches the pane's rows if the count still fails (`attachPaneRows`), which the next run reads.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (ruling 3 (i), left; superseded by "the second look, 2" below): a person can hold the textless thought's key open (its live row heads a stretch with a record line while it streams), but no fix stays inside the one rule: once the thought finishes with no text the derivation drops its row (`groups.ts`, `thoughtRow`), alone or when its run of one call unwraps, so no row answers to its key any more; carrying the key needs the derivation to keep the thought's id on the unwrapped call or the store to rewrite the key at the regrouping, a second mechanism. Left, as recorded under finding 4.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision: ruling 3(ii) withdrawn after the reviewer's case, which shows the hand-off failing with the whole stretch in view, against a requirement the document already binds; the fix is in this wave. The tracker row the fixer had written for it is removed.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the architect; recorded by the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (the second look, 1, a cut reply kept partway): the ellipsis of a partway cut is drawn beside the reply's words (`transcript-cut-partway`, `aria-hidden`), not prepended to them, so the string that is classed and projected is the reply's own text and a message in it is drawn as on a whole reply (`CutTextRow`, `AssistantText.tsx`). Proof: the agreement table's "cut partway" and "cut partway head" variants, in both modes.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review.
- Decision (the second look, 2, the textless thought): the opening is carried to a surviving identity at the regrouping, inside the one rule: when an update takes away the row an open key named, the keys of the stretch it stood in that a new row still answers to are opened with it (its own row's other keys, and the rows it folded with), and when none survives, the keys of the row the update brought in its place (the first new row after the last row before it that survives) (`carriedKeys`, `mirror.ts`, applied in the store's `put` in the same update). `ui.openStretches` stores keys as before, the carry only adds keys; the vanished key stays and opens its stretch again if a row comes to answer to it. Found while building it: a finished textless thought with no recorded time travels in no cell of its call's run (`derive.ts` drops it before the group machine), so at the call no row shares its key and the run stands in its place. Proof: `mirror.test.ts`'s thirteenth timeline (the newest window, the commentary above it), "a thought with no text that finishes before its call comes" (the update with no row in its place, where the commentary carries it) and the store's case, each red first. Left: a thought alone in the newest window that finishes in an update of its own before its call comes, with nothing it folded with in that window, has neither; its record line goes with it, and the run that follows is a new stretch, closed. This supersedes "ruling 3 (i), left" and the textless part of finding 4.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review, on the architect's ruling.
- Decision (the second look, 3, focus after a reshaped stretch): the press resolves the other line from the stretch it toggled, after the toggle: `toggleStretch` returns the keys the other line can carry (the key just opened; on folding, every key the stretch's rows answer to, over each mounted view's composition), and the hand-off names those lines (`handFocus(scope, lines)`), still scoped to its mount and one-shot, lapsing at the next frame. Proof: `focus.test.tsx` (after the run of one call unwraps; after a page above), red first, and the hand-off's several-lines case.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review, on the architect's ruling.
- Decision (the re-review, the carry over the composition): the carry reads the rows each mounted view composes, before and after the update (the composition `mountView` hands the store, which the jump reads), and the key's own rows only when no view is mounted, since what a view places among the key's rows (a standalone browser activity) splits a stretch there; over the store's rows the carry opened the earlier, closed stretch and left the reader's call closed. It composes only when the key's own rows lost a row an open key named (`lostKey`). Proof: `jump.test.tsx`, "an opened thought with no text that its call replaces stays open as the call, beside a closed stretch a standalone browser activity splits off", a real `Transcript` mount, red first.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the fixer of the whole-branch review, on the re-review's finding.
- Decision (R13, found by the architect after the merge): decision 12 was not met in the app. The mirror folds an answer's notice when the notice names a call whose row carries the receipt, but the derivation built every answer notice with `toolUseId: null` (`noticeOf`, `transcript/model/derive.ts`), and the R13 cases passed only on notices made by hand. The fix is where the notice is built: an answer notice names the call the arrival names (`arrival.call`) when the session holds that call's `tool.use` (the same lookup that reads its questions), else none, so a notice whose question is not loaded stays as the one statement. The join, from the code and the recorded shapes: the sminos bridge's arrival names the question's tool-use id (`u3-sminos-r1-terminal`, "async AskUserQuestion (call toolu_…)"); MAWS's ask names its askId, which the engine host sets to the call's tool-use id whenever the engine's request carries `claudecode/toolUseId` (`normalizer-controls.ts`, `toolUseId ?? requestId`; `pass3/p1-maws-ask-async`, askId equal to the tool-use id). Left: an ask whose request carried no tool-use id has the request id as its askId, so its notice names no call and stays beside the receipt; no recorded engine omits it. The row's shape is unchanged (`toolUseId` was already a nullable field of every notice; rows cross no process). Proof: `mirror.test.ts`, "through the derivation", red first.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the architect (the finding); the fixer of the whole-branch review (the fix).

- Decision: the record line's summary, designed into the second pull request (board #708) on the owner's typed answer to card 1 and the architect's ruling on it: the line carries the summary in MAWS too, and a setting turns summaries off, the line then reading `working record`. Added: decisions 23 to 30; Interfaces §M; rows of §F, §G, §K and §L, and §I's items 9 to 12; M7, and work in M4, M5, M6 and P; check 1's questions E and check 5's third reading; cards 10 to 13; reported points 17 to 20. **The design, fork by fork, with what lost.**
  1. *Who makes the summary:* the mod's tracker, in the engine process that runs the session, and nobody else. Lost: MAWS making it, by a control request on the session's engine or a one-shot engine process. That is a second maker wherever a terminal also runs the session, it needs a route to a model that MAWS does not have, and it gives one stretch two texts.
  2. *Where it is kept:* the mod's own file for the session, which MAWS reads and never writes. Lost: a copy in MAWS's index (a second store, and one more value against both earlier builds); a row in the transcript (it would reach every route with no reader of MAWS's, but nothing recorded shows a plugin writing a row the model does not read and an earlier build does not draw, and it changes the mod's own store).
  3. *How it reaches the mirror:* one command, read by the window on a short schedule after each close. Lost: a watcher in the daemon with an event (a lifecycle for every watched session and one more event on the wire, for a file that changes seconds after a moment the window already sees); the runs carried in the owner channel's published state (the whole list sent at every change of that state).
  4. *Where the mod summarises under MAWS:* at 9, where it draws. Lost: at 8 too, with the mirror reading the file. It would buy summaries only for an installation that could draw and is kept from it, and would pay model calls in sessions where the declared contract says MAWS draws.
  5. *The switch's home:* a fact of the daemon's, in the index's own facts, carried to the mod in the launch's settings env. Lost: a key of the machine settings file (UC-14 freezes its schema); a layout key, as UC-14 words it (no service reads the layout file, and a launch is decided in the daemon: reported point 19); a file of the mod's that MAWS writes (one switch for the machine, acting at once, but MAWS's first write into the engine's configuration directory, and of no effect until the changed mod is installed: card 10); the mod's value alone, with no control in MAWS (card 10).
  6. *What off means:* no new summaries; those already made stay. Lost: hiding them while it is off (card 11).
  7. *The line while a stretch grows, where MAWS draws:* its newest call, as the mod reads it. Lost: `working record` until the summary (card 12): the two routes would then differ in the sessions the mod draws.
  8. *Where it is built:* the daemon's half and the wire in M4, beside the minor and the owner service's files M4 already edits; the renderer's half in a milestone of its own, M7, whose state (the mod's file as a window has read it) and whose proof (a clock in the test's hand) are neither M4's nor M5's; one test in M5, the scenes in M6, four items in P. Lost: all of it inside M4 and M5 (each past what one reviewer reads in a pass, and M5 waits for check 1 where this does not); renumbering M6 (earlier entries of this log name it).
  Rationale: the law of this document, extended in §M. Read for it, none assumed: the mod's source at 0.8.2 and on its main branch (the same text), the engine's bundle at the lines "Today" cites, MAWS's tree at `d1c60b51b`, and both earlier builds for §F's rows. What no line of source and no recorded fixture shows is a question of check 1 and waits for the probe: what the engine pushes to a surface when a plugin's state is written (E2); whether a headless launch at 9 with the changed mod summarises and stores as a terminal does (E1, E5); whether the file's ids are the ids MAWS reads, and how the file is written (E4); what a call costs as the engine counts it (E6). Not verified at this revision: the sizes of M7 and of M4's addition (counted from the files named, with nothing built); the mod's design document's estimate of a call's cost (its author's); who owns `lane-a/view/RemoteTranscriptSlot.tsx` under UC-8 (§K has it read before the edit); that an index F opens ignores a table F's schema does not name (the installed build has kept `index_meta` on the same profiles F is kept for, and the cross-build run with F as the peer is what shows it).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the author of the revision for the summary, at the architect's dispatch.

- Decision: the rulings on the review of the summary. The adversarial review of this document at `03e23a042` returned "needs attention": one P1 and five P2, the architecture kept; it found the cross-build rows, the authorization boundary, the 24-character key rule and the switch's owner sound. The architect read each cited place of the mod's `folds.tsx` at 0.8.2, held all six, and ruled; this entry records where each ruling stands.
  - *The four reported points (17 to 20)* are accepted as written: the two gates in the mod; §I corrected about the live line at 9, with check 5's count deciding whether the mod's pull request must limit its invalidations; the switch's key in `index_meta`, because the daemon decides launches, with its coverage row; law 3 amended for this one switch. "Reported points", check 5.
  - *Findings 1 (P1) and 5, one ruling:* the file is a contract. The mod writes it by atomic replacement (a temporary file in the same directory, then a rename over the file), and before it writes it reads the file again and keeps every stored run its own tracker does not hold (its own runs win where both hold one, by the run's first id). §I, item 12, with its test of two trackers writing in each order; §M, "The file"; check 1's E4, which verifies the replacement and accepts no write in place; M7's "Starts when". From MAWS's code: MAWS starts no process on a session a live process runs (decision 33), so the collision that remains is two terminals', the mod's own, and M4 builds nothing for it.
  - *Finding 2:* no read of the schedule is cancelled by what an earlier read returned; a round runs to its end after every close of a stretch and every end of a turn. Decision 32; §M, "The reads"; M7's sequences (growth by a note alone, a late result).
  - *Finding 3:* a mount starts the same round, a remount starts it again, and the window reads once when it regains focus. What is left is stated: a summary written after the last read shows at the next mount, focus, close or end of a turn. No watcher and no subscription; a tracker row names them. Decision 32; §M, "The reads" and "What a person sees"; "The record".
  - *Finding 4:* one rule from the line's row to its run, written once (§M, "Which run a line reads") and implemented by both sides: the mod's `labelOf`, with one change made in the mod and in MAWS alike, a row that opens a stretch before the run's first tracked step taking the run that begins inside that stretch. No fallback on one bound in MAWS. Equal text is proved over one table of cases whose copy both repositories' tests read under one digest; M7's tests over invented files do not carry A15's words alone. Decision 31; §I, item 13; M7; "Acceptance", A15; Concrete Steps 5.
  - *Finding 6:* the mod's 30-day removal is accepted as the mod's; a window holds the last successful read whole, and a failed or unreadable read changes nothing. The law's "one text" holds for as long as the mod keeps its file. Decision 29; the header's law and §M's; "Acceptance"; M7's sequence for a removed file, a reopened window and a second window.
  - *The questions.* Card 12 (the line while a stretch grows) is decided by the owner's own answer to card 1: the newest call, then the summary. Card 13 (another machine and the desk web app) is decided by the architect: accepted for this landing, a tracker row and a desk ticket. Cards 10 and 11 go to the owner, rewritten to stand alone, each with its drawn example (frames 11 and 12). "Questions for the owner"; "The drawn example".
  - *Settled by the author under ruling 1, at one place; accepted (the architect):* how MAWS tells a file kept under the contract from one a release before the mod's pull request wrote. The file says it: the contract's files carry `shape: 2`, and MAWS reads shape 2 alone (decision 30; §M, "The file"; §I, item 12). The consequence is that a machine whose installed mod is 0.8.2 or before shows no summaries in MAWS, terminal sessions included, until the changed mod is installed and has written. The other reading, MAWS also reading shape 1, would let a read that succeeds be a file one of two processes had half replaced, against ruling 6's "a successful read is the truth".
  Rationale: the review's evidence, read in the mod's source by the architect and again at this revision; the law of §M.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-15 (UTC), the architect (the rulings); recorded by the author of the revision for the summary.

- Decision: check 1's answers and the mod's branch as built, recorded in one pass. Check 1 was run by the architect in two rounds on 2026-11-16 (UTC) (engine 2.1.322; the fixtures `u2b-mod-*` and the findings file are on this branch); the mod's branch `to-human-maws-surface` stands at `e511aad9`, to-human 0.9.0. This pass records them where the document states each thing, and corrects what they contradicted. It is not the re-check of the document against the chain, which comes when the build's base exists.
  1. *The table.* Every question's recorded answer with its fixtures is under check 1's table ("The answers"). Step 0 is done; M5 and M7 wait for nothing of it.
  2. *C2.* A thought is asked under its row's bare uuid; a uuid qualified by a block is not placed by the mod. §E's row and item 6 of §I are corrected. The probe's case of the engine's derived id form was confounded and proves nothing either way, and §E says so.
  3. *The defect and its fix* (the architect's ruling: fixed in the mod, MAWS's asks unchanged). A call asked alone as `ToolUse` and then held by a `ToolGroup` is one row with that group. §E's pattern stands; §E says what a lone `ToolUse` still mounted answers, and that no `ToolResult` ask for a call inside a group is handled or needed. §I, item 14.
  4. *E2 and C4.* The engine pushes one invalidation, naming the instance, for each write a drawn line had read: §I's item 11 is dropped for good. A reload may push nothing, so MAWS re-asks its mounted sites at a reload by itself; that is not built today and is M5's, in `engine-surface/site.ts`, from the status's `pluginsObservedAt`.
  5. *E6.* A summary call is counted in no `result` frame and leaves no record in the transcript: 386 to 388 tokens in and 23 to 40 out were measured, with `max_tokens` 6048 in the request. "Cost on a long transcript" holds the measured numbers in place of the estimate and says that MAWS's cost figures do not show these calls; the owner has been told, nothing is planned to display them, and a tracker row holds it.
  6. *§I as built.* The departures, each accepted by the architect, are under "As built": the two scripts beside the kit with the kit's refusal line, the table's digest, the backslash, `native`'s shape, an anchored ask's place, the file's temporary and the widened removal, the re-ask of a line read by clause 4, the live check's three launches.
  7. *The turn's line.* MAWS draws it at the head of the turn's work, so its anchor is the row that opens the turn, and the mod reads the turn forward from it: §E says so outright. A line anchored after the turn's last message is not an ask MAWS sends. Item 5's known limit is a tracker row.
  8. *The sminos ask mod.* "Today" is corrected to what was recorded at 8. Whether its drawing in a session MAWS runs is right, stale or hides an answer could not be settled by reading and is reported point 21, open.
  9. *A terminal session* draws the same before and after: §I, "What it must not change".
  Found at the pass and not ruled: reported point 22 (MAWS's existing ask of a text that is a later block uses the id form the mod does not place).
  Rationale: the recordings, and the mod's diff from 0.8.2 to `e511aad9` read beside them. No word a person reads was changed by this pass.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), the architect (the rulings of items 3, 4, 5 and 6); recorded by the author of the revision for the summary.

- Decision: the architect's rulings on points 21 and 22. Both are closed for this landing; each was confirmed in the code at this pass before it was written.
  - *Point 21, the sminos ask mod.* MAWS sends no `ToolUse` ask for an ask tool's call: such a call is a `question` row (`ASK_MOD_TOOLS` in `transcript/model/classify.ts`; the derivation; `askModAnswers`), drawn by `SurfaceQuestionRow`, which asks the `AskUserQuestion` site under the call's id and draws MAWS's own `QuestionRow` around the answered props (read at the chain's head, `origin/ux-wave/U1c` at `b62710d8b`: no presentation but `cell` makes a cell, so no run holds such a call and no `ToolUse` or `ToolResult` ask is made for one). The installed sminos ask mod (1.47.0) hooks `ui.render` at `ToolUse`, `ToolResult` and `AbovePrompt` only. So the tree check 1 recorded for the question's row answered the probe's own ask; in MAWS the row is MAWS's, with its receipt, and R13's condition matches what is drawn. M5 gains one case of `mod-route.test.tsx`. Left, as a tracker row and not as work of M4 to M7: the band in a session MAWS resumed from a terminal's transcript.
  - *Point 22, a text that is a later block.* No change to `assistantInput`. No recording holds an `assistant` frame with more than one block (counted on this branch: 810 files and 3,581 frames, through every wrapper, none; the architect's first count, 678 files and 2,856 frames with none, was taken on an older tree and is not the one of record), so every recorded text asks under its bare uuid, which the mod places. §E says so; a tracker row keeps the unplaced form until a recording holds a frame with two blocks. M5's condition on this point is removed.
  - *The contradictions of the last pass.* §I's items 1, 2 and 13 stay as the design handed over, each with a pointer to "As built", which governs. The cards' shared opening stays, as the record of what was asked. The mods list's words "on the session’s own account" stay: they are true, and the tracker row on the cost figures covers what MAWS does not show.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), the architect (the rulings); confirmed and recorded by the author of the revision for the summary.

- Decision: the pre-build pass. The branch `ux-wave/U2b-2` at `1ee6dca93` holds the chain through U3b's merge of U1c (wire minor 51), the base of the second pull request. Every statement about the code that M4, M5, M6 and M7 rely on was read on it with git and the tree's files, nothing run: about 80 for M4, about 140 for M5, about 75 for M6 and M7. Each statement that no longer held is restated in place, one line here for each:
  1. *"Today":* of the lane's command and event, only the command has an owner in the wire manifest; the event is among `MACHINE_EVENTS`.
  2. *"Today":* the status's plugins are filled in `src/engine-host/session-status.ts`, not in `observed-facts.ts`.
  3. *"Today":* `WIRE_MINOR` was 46 at the document's base and is 51 at the merged head.
  4. *"What is assumed of landings in flight":* a waiting row asks no site of its own while its form stands (`useWaitingForm`); a waiting question's form asks `AskUserQuestion` under the call's id.
  5. *Decision 9:* a closed stretch's key is its first row's; an open stretch is named by an open key and its opening is carried (the amendment of the whole-branch review, now in the decision's own text).
  6. *M4, "Starts when":* its base holds the first pull request; the merged branch does.
  7. *M4, the tests that pin 8 by its digit:* `launch-mapping.test.ts` is one of them.
  8. *M4 and Concrete Steps 3:* `src/shared/ipc/routes.json` is rewritten with the wire's files.
  9. *Concrete Steps 1:* until the chain is on master, the base's greps are read against `HEAD`.
  10. *Concrete Steps 3:* the minor is 51 at the merged head, and the number M4 writes is a stand-in in one commit of its own.
  11. *Concrete Steps 4, "Cost", M7:* the lane-on case of the derivation budget is `owner/view/perf.test.ts`, read alone.
  12. *§C:* `stretchesOf` and `stretchHolding` take the open keys as a last optional argument, and the file exports six more names.
  13. *§C:* `mirrorRows` is memoised by its rendering callers; it has three arguments today.
  14. *§C:* `toggleStretch` answers the keys the other line can carry.
  15. *§C and M5:* `modDraws` is declared and read since M1; M5 sets it and joins it to the flag.
  16. *§B:* two rows the table did not name, `activity` (stays) and `browser_activity` under a call (folds).
  17. *§D:* the switch stands in a frame of its own, drawn as the band's.

- Decision: the architect's rulings on points 23 to 26, each written where it belongs; M5 and M7 wait for no ruling.

  - *24 (M5), a `browser_activity` row under a call.* It follows its call: no ask of its own, shown exactly when its call's row is shown. Written in the form a time separator already has in §E (it keeps the call's row's ask alive by a probe at that row's address), which is how "an ask alive while the row is mounted" still holds for a row mounted without its call's row. §E's table; M5's test.
  - *25 (M7), the ids a row is kept under.* The rule of §M stands. One derivation of the id a row asks under, which `keptIdsOf` reads from what each row's ask reads (the carrier lookups of `adapters.ts`, exported); a merged read keeps every member's tool-use id in an additive list; a thought needs nothing, since a stored run's bounds never name one (the mod's `follow` and `stepsOf`); a call's raw input is looked up in the turn's records, handed in with the labels. Tests run real events through the real derivation and the real mirror. §M, "Where the ids and the inputs come from"; M7's files and proofs; §K's row for `adapters.ts`.
  - *26 (M7), where the summary's switch is drawn.* The mods list only, as frame 11 showed the owner; `BuiltInModRow` takes the switch from its caller, and the row under Customize is unchanged. §M, "The switch"; M7's two tests.

- Decision: M4, the reservation's two slots (2026-11-16 (UTC)). The sequence that showed the gap: this build launches at 9 (L1, its reservation bound to L1); a relaunch reserves again, which overwrote the column with an unbound reservation; the host refuses it and L1 still runs. `advertisementOf(row, L1)` then found no reservation bound to L1, `L1` equal to the row's `launch_id`, and read `desired` as 8: `drawnBy` `maws` while the mod draws at 9, both sides drawing once the route is on. §A's third row took "the row's own launch with no reservation bound to it" for an earlier build's launch only; this build's own refused relaunch lands there too. Restated in §A ("Two slots", the `prior` of `Reservation`, a row of `advertisementOf`'s table): the bound slot survives any number of refused relaunches; binding moves the unbound reservation into it. Cases pinned red first: two refused relaunches in a row, then the running launch reads its own bound 9 and `drawnBy` is `mod`; the daemon ends between a reservation and its bind and starts again, the running launch still reads its bound reservation and the leftover unbound one is overwritten by the next; a relaunch that succeeds reads its own, and nothing reads the old launch's. The earlier-build sequence (an earlier build launching after a reservation this build left unbound reads `desired`) stays as pinned. Rejected: a rollback on refusal (a daemon that ends before it loses it).
  Also from M4's build, safe under decision 4 and left as they are: during a resume's retry `drawnBy` reads "not known" (`mod` while the plugin is loaded) until the retry's id is bound; after a failed write of a 9 a leftover unbound reservation stays until the next reservation overwrites it.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), found by M4's executor, ruled by the architect; recorded by the controller of the second pull request.

- Decision: M4's means, as built (`eab0ea0ac`..`9d8ab8f7d`). (1) `advertisementFor` takes the route as an optional third argument defaulting to `TO_HUMAN_MOD_ROUTE`, so §G's table runs with the route on in tests while the constant stays off; production callers pass none. (2) `LaunchRecord.reserved?: boolean`: `recordLaunch` binds the stored reservation only when the decision it records wrote it, so a launch sent at 8 after a failed write, or one with no reservation, never binds another launch's. (3) Before the owner service's first read of the live sessions' status, a session with no status yet takes its row's newest launch as live and its plugins as not yet reported (an unbound reservation read as the running launch's), which is what makes the restart case read `mod` before the plugins report. (4) The tracker (`WhoDraws`, `src/services/owner/drawn-by.ts`) orders launch ids by when it first saw them and ignores a status of an older launch. (5) The reader of the mod's file answers `[]` where there is nothing to read (no record, no engine session id, no file) and `null` where the file cannot be trusted (an id that is no plain name, an unreadable index, a read error, past 4 MiB, a shape not 2); the read goes through the opened handle. (6) `mods.setFoldSummaries` for another machine is refused with `mods.list`'s sentence, as its namespace's siblings are. (7) The host's list of components `session.ui.renderSite` accepts is `RENDER_SITE_COMPONENTS` in `src/engine-host/ipc.ts`, the host's own. (8) `resolve-style` computes `toHuman` for every style, since the launch reads every applicable installation's declaration; `messageTool` reads from it. (9) `test/support` 's `installToHumanPlugin` gains `declares` and `version`. (10) The wire minor's stand-in is the one commit `db6689f86` (minor 52; manifest, fingerprint and `routes.json` generated on the VM); the commit after it touches no wire file, so a chain merge retakes `db6689f86` alone.
  Rationale: each is a means the milestone left open; (2) and (4) close orderings §A's tables assume.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), M4's executor; folded by the controller.

- Decision: M4's review, three restatements in decision 4's direction (2026-11-16 (UTC)). The frontier review of M4 found transitions in which "who draws" read an uncertain state as `maws`. Each is restated so that `maws` rests on evidence and uncertainty reads `mod` (a session at rest published `mod` is harmless: a window with no surface attached draws the mirror). (1) *§A's second row.* An unbound reservation read for a running launch the row has not recorded never by itself yields `maws`: at 8 it reads as not known, and the start's reconciliation does not bind an unbound 8 to an unrecorded launch. The sequence: an unbound 8 left by a refused launch; a later lane-off launch whose reservation write fails goes with nothing and is not yet recorded; the abandoned 8 was read for it, `maws` while the loaded mod draws, and reconciliation made it lasting (an earlier build's lane-off launch with an interrupted record, the same). Numbers 9 and null read as before: what they can misread is "neither", never "both". Cost, accepted by decision 4: a real 8 launch whose bind was lost to a daemon stop shows no fold until its next launch. (2) *M4, "the declaration's read is asynchronous".* "Until a read answers the earlier fact stands" is restated: until a read answers for a new observation, the newly loaded plugin counts as declaring; its presence is known at once from the status; a declaration that fails to read with an I/O error other than "no such file" at a reload is unknown (`mod`); a missing or wrongly shaped one is a confirmed non-declaration. At the launch decision nothing changes (an unreadable declaration advertises 8). (3) *Before and between statuses.* A newly bound launch is provisionally live with its plugins unreported until its first status; before the first read of the live sessions the running launch is the row's recorded launch, not an older bound reservation's; an unreadable row before discovery is not known; a status of a launch other than the current one does not move the current one to rest, by launch identity and not observation order, and a snapshot is not applied over newer events.
  Rationale: the header's law and decision 4; each case was reproduced by the reviewer on the VM against the milestone's source.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), the controller of the second pull request, at M4's frontier review.

- Decision: M4, who draws, closed as one table (2026-11-16 (UTC)). At the architect's ruling that six findings on one rule meant fixing it cause by cause, §A now carries one table (A: what the row says; B: `drawnBy` over the advertisement, the engine and the declaration; C: which status counts), with two properties checked over every cell (never both; neither only in named cells, each with why and what ends it), in place of the prose rows. Writing it changed two readings beyond the review's restatements: (1) *A4* reads an unbound reservation for an unrecorded running launch as not known whatever its number, not only at 8, since an unbound 9 with a non-declaring installation loaded, or an unbound nothing with the plugin installed after it, could each still read `maws` while the mod draws; the start's reconciliation therefore binds nothing. (2) *B8*: no advertisement with the launch's `plugin` unknown (an earlier build's lane-off launch) reads `mod` before the plugins report, where the first table read `maws`. R2's restart proof holds through A4 and B12 (`mod`). The test pinning each cell is named in M4's report and folded here after the fix wave. In every *neither* cell the person sees the full transcript with no fold (the architect's question, answered from check 1's A1 and the mod's `native()`; M5 pins it).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), the architect (the ruling to close it as a table); the table by the controller of the second pull request.

- Decision: M4's second review, the table's evidence (2026-11-16 (UTC)). The re-review found the cells right as written and three places where the evidence behind a cell did not describe the running launch or the loaded worker. (1) *C3* ordered an unseen launch against the daemon's bind time: a launch acknowledged late (started at 1, replaced by another client's launch at 2, bound by the daemon at 3) made the real running launch's statuses look older, and a bound 8 kept `maws` while the later launch's mod drew. Restated: only the host's own `launchedAt` orders launches; where the running launch's is not known the unseen live launch becomes the running one, which A4 reads not known. (2) *B6* took "no such file" for a confirmed non-declaration even when the whole installation directory was gone. Restated: a missing file in a directory that is present is "no" (an installation's path names its release, so a present directory holds what was loaded from it); a directory that is gone is "failed", read `mod`. (3) *The first state across a daemon's restart*: with the row unreadable and no state built by the new daemon, `ownerChannel.state` fails and the window keeps the old daemon's `drawnBy`. The renderer's owner-channel store is the place that can fail closed (M4 touches no renderer): M5 makes a failed state read after a reconnect drop the held state, so no mirror is drawn on a value the daemon can no longer back, with a test. *B9* (no advertisement, the launch found no installation whose hooks load, before the plugins report: `maws`) rests on the installations the decision read being the ones the engine loads; a plugin installed between the two would be drawn by both until the plugins report. Brought to the architect with the controller's recommendation (keep B9, name the exception; reading it `mod` takes MAWS's turn fold from every lane-off session without the mod until its plugins report, which a resumed session waiting for a prompt may never do).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), the controller of the second pull request, at M4's second frontier review.

- Decision: B9 stays `maws`, the table's one named exception (2026-11-16 (UTC)). Both sides can draw only if a to-human plugin is installed or enabled within the launch's own startup, between the decision that found none and the engine's load; it ends when the plugins report, or at the next launch. Why not read it as not known: that would take MAWS's turn fold from every session that does not use the mod, possibly for as long as a resumed session waits for its first prompt (a start with no prompt sees no init), to cover an install made in the seconds of one launch; and in the exception nothing is lost or hidden, a fold is drawn by both until the report. The property above the table is restated to match; the *neither* cells stay as named. One test pins B9's reading and its end at the plugins' report. The first state across a daemon's restart (the previous entry's item 3) is built and tested in M5 before the cutover commit, so the route is never on with that hole open.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), the architect (the ruling); recorded by the controller of the second pull request.

- Decision: M4's fix waves 2 and 3, the means (2026-11-16 (UTC)). The host's launch answer already carries `session.launchedAt`; `setup.ts` and `resume.ts` hand it to `ownerChannelLaunched`, which hands it to the tracker's bind, so no wire changes for C3 (it is optional on the launch's answer record: a caller that omits it binds with the start time not known). Equal start times do not count as older. A bind is taken three ways: the running launch's own id fills in its start time; a launch already seen and not running is ignored whatever the times say, so a replaced launch is never rebound (the third review's equal-times sequence: L1 at 8 exits, L2 with the same `launchedAt` runs, L1's late acknowledgement had reinstated it); an unseen launch becomes the running one unless the host's times show it older. Only an applied bind counts as a newer event for C4. A file where an installation directory was reads "failed".
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), M4's executor; folded by the controller.

- Decision: M5, a call's ask by its row's live identity (2026-11-16 (UTC)). M5's executor found that MAWS asks `ToolGroup` for a live run of one (U2a draws it as `group:cell:<call>`) and, once the turn closes and U2a's derivation makes the single call a `cell`, `ToolUse`; read in the mod's branch at `e511aad9`, the mod answers that `ToolUse` as a non-opening member of the group (`groupOf` still names it) while no mounted row asks the group, so a stretch of one call in a closed turn ("a prompt, one call, a message") would have no record line where the mod draws. The earlier sentence of §E ("a call alone asks `ToolUse` under its own id, and from its second call the run asks `ToolGroup`") was true of the terminal and not of MAWS. Ruled (the architect): on the mod's route a call's ask never changes kind over its row's life; a call the live derivation draws as a run asks `ToolGroup` under the live run's ask id (`group:<call>`; the ruling's words named the row id `group:cell:<call>`, corrected at the build) closed too, decided from the cell alone by the live derivation's own test, never from a memory of an earlier ask; a call that never joins a run asks `ToolUse`. Rejected: a change to the mod (the mod cannot know that a group has stopped asking; MAWS's side has a recording, E3's `-group`). The mod-side evidence: four cases in its kit at `e79f701fe72e488155e6b43d4e9f7cd0bcd9ffc0` (tests only; eight runs, at 9 and with no advertisement; the kit's run 354 pass, 0 fail), the fourth confirming the defect of the sequence MAWS must not send. Built in M5's fix wave, red first, over real events through the real derivation, live, closed and from the file route. Evidence on the mod's side (a test in its kit of a group of one asked across a turn's end and with no earlier state) is the architect's.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-16 (UTC), found by M5's executor, ruled by the architect; recorded by the controller of the second pull request.

Found by M5's executor: in a window that answered the card itself and reads a running session from its file, the question's row has no `answers`, the answer comes back as the person's own `user` row (which the mod keeps in view, check 1's C5), and MAWS's `QuestionRow` draws the receipt from the attention store as well. - Decision: M7's frontier review, the reads' moments by identity (2026-11-16 (UTC)). A round of reads started only when the aggregate "the last stretch is closed" turned true or "working" turned false, so a batch that closed a second stretch while the first stayed closed and the turn ran on, or that ended a turn and started the next, started none. A round now starts at a stretch newly closed (by its key) or a turn newly ended (by its id); several in one batch start one round (`198339d6f` red in a mounted `Transcript`, `961a25a3d`).

- Decision: M7's means, as built (`91af902be`..`6038e458f`, then the per-turn step `212a75363`..`1e827b6c4` and three fix waves `198339d6f`..`c348eabe8`). (1) A row's place is counted per kept id, in row order; a row's place is its first id's (the mod's places, a group known by its first call). (2) `runOf` and `liveLine` take a trailing optional `turns`, and `StretchLabels` gains `turns?`: the inputs "handed in with the labels" (§M). (3) `keptIdsOf` per kind reads what each row's own ask reads; a call asking under `group:<call>` (§E) is kept under its members' tool-use ids, the ids a stored run's bounds name; a notice with no carrier asks under an anchored id the file never names and is kept under none. (4) `mergeReads` has had no caller since `5ab559bcd` (the derivation keeps each read its own call): its member list is pinned by unit tests calling it directly. (5) A round starts at a stretch newly closed (by its key) and a turn newly ended: a turn is ended when it is `closed`, or when no turn runs, it was accepted, and this watch saw it open while one ran (a queued prompt that never started has not ended; a turn whose rows reach the window before the bridge's port says it runs is not ended at its start: fix wave 4, `3b12ec010`..`2208833b6`); the effect reads the turns and whether one runs from one store entry; several moments in one batch start one round; the first observation seeds silently. (6) `stores/folds.ts` is listed in `GUARDED_ELSEWHERE`; the slot enables it for this machine's sessions alone. (7) `unknown_command`, or a refusal (`issues.refusal`), stops reads for that session for the window's life. (8) The runs a window read are kept after its last mount goes, so a remount draws them at once while its own round reads again. (9) The labels memo reads the turns through `getState()`, keyed on the rows. (10) The stored runs are indexed once per change of the rows (clause 3 newest first, clause 4 by binary search) and the rows' places kept per turn while the turn's state and rows are the same objects. It is cleared when the session's status leaves the store, which happens only when its engine host leaves ready or its machine's link goes down (`forgetStatuses`); a successor host is asked again after release, remount and invalidation. - Decision: the owner's ruling, 2026-11-20 (UTC): the sessions MAWS launches get fold summaries. **Decision 25 is amended:** under MAWS the mod summarises at an advertisement of 8 or more, not only where it draws MAWS's view (9 or more). At 8 the mod's tracker makes the summary in the session's engine process, as at 9, and the mirror puts it on the record line from the session's folds file (§M, "The reads"). Where §I's item 9 (its table's rows for 8) and §M's table (the row "MAWS draws (the advertisement 8)", "nobody at 8") say otherwise, this entry amends them from the installation of the mod's change; they are not rewritten in place. Unchanged: one maker; the switch, which `withOwnerChannel` writes as `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off` into the settings env of every launch decided while it is off, whatever the lane and the advertisement, and which the mod honours at 8 as at 9; no summaries in a headless session with no advertisement, or under `SMNS_INTERNAL`; a terminal session; the file's contract (§I item 12) and its bounds; one call per closed stretch and again when it grew, as in a terminal.
  The mod's change: doperpowers #505 (branch `to-human-maws-surface`, to-human 0.9.0, unmerged), commit `3487fe88`. `folds.tsx` gains `MAWS_READS_FOLDS_FROM = 8`: a headless session asks for a due run's summary where `MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN` is a number of 8 or more, and a non-empty value below 8, or no number, stays quiet. Its suite (`to-human/tests/mods/run.sh`) was run red first (356 pass, 7 fail, every failure a case at 8) and then green (363 pass, three bun checks pass); a case pins that a headless session at 8 asks exactly the model requests a terminal session asks for the same rows. `live-maws-check.py` now summarises in four launches (8 and 9, each also with summaries off), not yet run.
  MAWS needs no code change: the mirror's reads (`useSummaries`, `transcript/view/TranscriptSlot.tsx`) are gated by the session being this machine's and by `mirrorsView`, never by the advertisement, and `recordLabel` reads a stored summary whatever the switch says. One consequence for earlier builds: F, the installed build `2c7044231` and a MAWS at the first pull request also advertise 8 but have neither M7's reader nor the switch, so once the mod's change is installed their sessions make a summary call per closed stretch that nothing in them shows, and only `TO_HUMAN_FOLD_SUMMARIES=off` in the engine's own settings env stops it. §I's closing line ("an earlier MAWS build is unchanged") holds for the drawing only.
  Rationale: every MAWS launch so far advertises 8, and under the gate at 9 no session MAWS launches ever had a summary, so its record lines always read `working record`, which the ruling rejects.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-20 (UTC), the owner (the ruling); the mod's change built on #505's branch and recorded here by the session that carried it.

## Outcomes & Retrospective

**The first pull request (M1 to M3), at the controller's hand-back, 2026-11-15.** The mirror folds by stretch on every route MAWS draws. A message is drawn as `● message` over an indented body. The bar is gone, and the switch is one line above the composer, or above the holder notice. Every launch still advertises 8, and nothing is stored or sent. The proof by unit tests:
- the M1 and M2 folders, with the guards;
- the agreement test over 108 recorded marked strings;
- the full typecheck, eslint and the gate's script tests;
- the desk web app's typecheck, build and test project;
- the whole unit suite at `0482b013e`: ten files failed, six of them the VM's known list, four that pass alone (Decision Log).
Still open:
- the architect's Electron and desk e2e runs;
- the measured anchoring scene;
- the look on the built window;
- the owner's sitting;
- the branch's xhigh panel;
- class 1a.

What went as planned and what did not:
- M1's frontier review found three integration faults, each reproduced on the VM: a flag one mount could clear for another, a jump over rows other than those drawn, and an old fold animation hiding the record line. One fix wave closed them.
- M2 took three fix waves, all on one question: what is a message and what is record.
  - The first drew the closed head by cutting the authored Markdown tree. That held for paragraphs and broke inside containers.
  - The second drew `parseMarked`'s spans. That left four readers deciding the same partition apart.
  - The third put one reading in `src/shared/owner/marked.ts`, which every reader in the renderer derives from. For an open mark, a stray closer and crossing marks it keeps the shared grammar's literal reading (option 3), because the shared grammar's consumers reach stored marks and the wire. The difference from the mod is left to the second pull request.
- Lesson: when a drawing depends on a partition that other code already computes, the first version should derive from that computation. A second parser beside it gets review rounds instead.
- The desk web app's imports of the renderer were outside the milestones' proof until a sibling landing's class 1a run found the hole. From M2's third wave on, its typecheck, build and test project are part of every milestone's proof.

For the second pull request:
- the surface adapter's `isFirstOfReply` differs from the engine's (M5);
- §E's known differences, the backslash reading and option 3's cases;
- the record line's summary (card 1): designed at the revision of 2026-11-15 (UTC) as Interfaces §M, built by M4, M7, M5's test, M6 and P;
- check 1 is run and the mod's branch is built (2026-11-16 (UTC)); the two points it raised (reported points 21 and 22) are ruled and closed;
- the agreement test's comparison, which should become ordered runs;
- `ownerChannel.answered` and `ownerChannel.cancel`, which have no renderer caller left.

**The second pull request (M4 to M7), at the controller's hand-back, 2026-11-16.** Who draws a session's view is one decision, stated as §A's table and carried on the wire as `lane.drawnBy` (the stand-in for minor 52, `db6689f86`). Where the mod draws, every row asks its site for its whole life, under one kind fixed by the tool's own table row. The cutover `c89865275` turned `TO_HUMAN_MOD_ROUTE` on, after the fail-closed first state. The record line reads the mod's stored summaries where MAWS draws, and the switch turns new summaries off from a session's next start. The proof by unit tests:
- each milestone's touched folders and their importers, with the tokens guard;
- the whole unit suite once, by M6's executor at code before M7's fix wave 4: nine files failed, eight on the VM's known lists and `source.test.ts`, which is now on them;
- after that fix wave, the owner, stores, transcript and lane-a folders and the desk depth (5085 passed), the desk app's typecheck, build and tests, and the web and e2e typechecks;
- after M6's waves, the e2e typecheck, eslint, and the gate's script tests (588/588).
Still open:
- the architect's Electron runs of every edited or new scene, first alone, by group;
- the trial, a paid run, with its stand-ins (Decision Log, "M6's means", item 10): the jump step's target selector rests on how the mod draws a plain-text essential block, which no recording shows;
- the two cross-build runs over §H's four scenes, on a Mac;
- the looks and the owner's sitting;
- the branch's panel;
- class 1a.

What went as planned and what did not:
- M4 took three fix waves on one question: what counts as the launch a reservation names. The answer became §A's table, C3 orders launches only by the host's `launchedAt`, and a replaced launch is never bound again.
- M5's live check showed a single call asking `ToolUse` while a group asked `ToolGroup`, which left an empty member in the mod's drawing. A call's ask is now fixed for its whole life by its tool's table row, not by the classification its result can move.
- M7 took four fix waves, all on when a round of reads starts: batched triggers, a text-only turn, a queued prompt, then the order in which the port and the rows report. The rule as built starts a round at a stretch newly closed and at a turn newly ended, where "ended" at a port's "none runs" counts only for a turn the watch saw run. Batch 0's cost is a tracker row (ticket 723).
- M6 took three fix waves, all on the paid trial, plus the architect's I4 ruling, which raised the cross-build scenes from two to four. The trial's readings were written against what the product draws, not against what stays in the DOM. Under the mod, MAWS keeps its own drawing concealed, the mod draws a stretch's record line inside the stretch's first row, and a path is a chip that opens beside the chat. Each round of reading found a way the never-run scene could stop on a working product. The coordinator made the fourth review the last; its two findings, older than the waves, were fixed with no further round, and the first paid run is the real check.
- Lesson: a scene that cannot be run before its one paid run should be written against recorded answers (check 1's fixtures) and the concealment rules from the start, with each selector cited to its source. That is what the third wave did.
- Reviews after 15:30Z on 2026-11-16 ran on fable as general-purpose reviewers. One run of M7 re-reviews that another model served was run again.
